\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}p^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\frac{p^{\pm}}{(\rho^{\pm})^{2}}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\rho^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\mathfrak{P}(\rho^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

The boundary term gives
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted and
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted regularity of
\psi
and
\psi_{t}
. One has

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\sigma\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}+\kappa|(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\kappa|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

Then we insert the evolution equation of
b^{\pm}
in the third term in (
3.6
) to get the energy of
b^{\pm}
.

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})v^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}b^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}|b^{\pm}|^{2}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}|b^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}|b^{\pm}|^{2}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}|b^{\pm}|^{2}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t},

where the last two terms exactly cancels with the last term in (
3.6
). Finally,
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}S^{\pm}=0
and the Reynolds transport theorem shows that
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|S^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=0
. Therefore, we conclude that system (
3.1
) admits the following conserved quantity

\displaystyle E_{0}^{\kappa}(t):=

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|v^{\pm}|^{2}+|b^{\pm}|^{2}+2\mathfrak{P}(\rho^{\pm},S^{\pm})+\rho^{\pm}|S^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\sigma\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}+\kappa|(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\kappa|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

which can also be inherited to the original current-vortex sheet system (
1.30
) after taking
\kappa\to 0_{+}
.∎

3.2
Reformulations in Alinhac good unknowns

Let
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}:=(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\gamma_{4}}\partial_{t}^{\gamma_{0}}\partial_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}\partial_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}
be a tangential derivative with
\langle\gamma\rangle=\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{4}
. We define the Alinhac good unknown of a given function
f
with respect to
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
by
\mathbf{F}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}f-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi\partial_{3}^{\varphi}f
. The good unknown
\mathbf{F}
satisfies

\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\nabla_{i}^{\varphi}f=\nabla^{\varphi}_{i}\mathbf{F}^{\gamma}+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\gamma}(f),~~\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}D_{t}^{\varphi}f=D_{t}^{\varphi}\mathbf{F}^{\gamma}+\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f),

(3.8)

where the commutators
\mathfrak{C}^{\gamma}_{i}(f)
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f)
are defined by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\gamma}(f)=

\displaystyle~(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}f)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}f\right]+\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathbf{N}_{i},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]+\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\varphi

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f-\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\partial_{3}f[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\varphi,\quad i=1,2,3,

(3.9)

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f)=

\displaystyle~(D_{t}^{\varphi}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\bar{v}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{}f+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi),\partial_{3}f\right]+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi,\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]\partial_{3}f

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},v]\cdot\mathbf{N}\partial_{3}f-(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\varphi

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f+(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\frac{\partial_{3}f}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\varphi

(3.10)

with
\langle\gamma^{\prime}\rangle=1
. Here
\mathbf{N}:=(-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi,-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi,1)^{\top}
is the extension of normal vector
N
in
\Omega^{\pm}
. The third term on the right side of (
3.2
) is zero when
i=3
because
\mathbf{N}_{3}=1
is a constant.

Therefore, we can reformulate the
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-differentiated current-vortex sheets system (
3.1
) in terms of
\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm}
,
\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm}
,
\mathbf{P}^{\gamma,\pm}
,
\mathbf{S}^{\gamma,\pm}
(the Alinhac good unknowns of
v^{\pm},b^{\pm},p^{\pm},S^{\pm}
in
\Omega^{\pm}
) as follows

\displaystyle\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{Q}^{\gamma,\pm}=\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\gamma,\pm}-\mathfrak{C}^{\gamma}(q^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(3.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{p}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\gamma,\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm}=\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\gamma,\pm}-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\gamma}(v_{i}^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(3.12)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm}+b^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm})=\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\gamma,\pm}-b^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\gamma}(v_{i}^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(3.13)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b^{\pm}=0~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(3.14)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{S}^{\pm,\alpha}=\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(S^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(3.15)

with boundary conditions

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\mathbf{Q}^{\gamma}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathcal{H}-\kappa\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi-\kappa\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi-\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi~~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(3.16)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm}\cdot N=\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi+\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi-\mathcal{W}^{\gamma,\pm}~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(3.17)

\displaystyle b^{\pm}\cdot N=0~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(3.18)

\displaystyle b_{3}^{\pm}=v_{3}^{\pm}=\mathbf{B}_{3}^{\pm}=\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\pm}=0~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma^{\pm},

(3.19)

where
\mathcal{R}_{v},\mathcal{R}_{p},\mathcal{R}_{b}
terms consist of the following commutators

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\gamma,\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},b^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}b^{\pm}-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\rho^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}-\rho^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(v^{\pm})

(3.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\gamma,\pm}:=

\displaystyle-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(p^{\pm})

(3.21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\gamma,\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},b^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}v^{\pm}-\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(b^{\pm}),

(3.22)

and the boundary term
\mathcal{W}^{\gamma,\pm}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{\gamma,\pm}:=(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot N)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},N_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}],

(3.23)

Note that
\omega(x_{3})=0
on
\Sigma\cup\Sigma^{\pm}
, so all boundary conditions are vanishing when
\gamma_{4}>0
. Thus,
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
can be written as
\partial_{t}^{k+\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}^{(4+l)-(k+\alpha_{0})}
on
\Sigma
. We can replace
k+\alpha_{0}
by
k
(
0\leq k\leq 4+l
) in the boundary energy terms. In the rest of Section
3
, we aim to prove the following tangential estimates

Proposition 3.3

(Tangential estimates for the approximate system)
.

For fixed
l\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
and any
\delta\in(0,1)
, the following uniform-in-
(\kappa,\varepsilon)
energy inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle=2l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq 4-l\\
k+\alpha_{0}<4+l\end{subarray}}\left\|\left(\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k-l}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm},p^{\pm})\right)\right\|^{2}_{0,\pm}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{3+l}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|^{2}_{5-k-l}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{6+k-l}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi(\tau)\right|_{5+k-l}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\sum_{k=0}^{3+l}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi(0)\right|_{5.5+l-k}^{2}+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(t)\right)\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.24)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{k=0}^{4-l}\left\|\left(\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm},(\mathcal{F}_{p})^{\frac{1}{2}}p^{\pm})\right)\right\|^{2}_{4-k-l,\pm}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}\psi\right|^{2}_{1}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}\psi\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{5+l}\psi(\tau)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\psi(0)\right|_{2.5}^{2}+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(t)\right)\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.25)

Here the first inequality represents the case when there are at least one spatial tangential derivatives and the second inequality represents the case of full time derivatives. Moreover, the term
\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi(0)\right|_{5.5+l-k}^{2}
on the right side does not appear when
\kappa=0
.

3.3
Tangential estimates: full spatial derivatives

We first study the case when all tangential derivatives are spatial derivatives
\overline{\partial}{}_{1}
and
\overline{\partial}{}_{2}
, namely
\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{4}=0
in
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}:=(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\gamma_{4}}\partial_{t}^{\gamma_{0}}\partial_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}\partial_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}
. In view of the definition of
E(t)
and the div-curl decomposition, we need to prove the
L^{2}
estimates for the
\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}
-differentiated system
(0\leq l\leq 4)
. We now consider the case
l=0
, that is, the
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
-estimate for the approximate system (
3.1
) and aim to prove the following estimate

Proposition 3.4
.

Fix
l\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
. For the tangential derivative
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l},~(\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{4}=0,~\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}=4+l)
, the
\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}
-differentiated approximate system admits the following uniform-in-
(\kappa,\varepsilon)
estimate: For any
0<\delta<1

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\left(\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{S}^{\gamma,\pm},\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\gamma,\pm}\right)(t)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\psi(t)\right|_{1}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\psi(t)\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\partial_{t}\psi(\tau)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\psi_{0}\right|_{5.5+l}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{l}\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~~0\leq l\leq 4.

3.3.1
The case
l=0
:
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
-estimates

As stated in Section
2.2
, we introduce the Alinhac good unknowns for
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
and drop the script
\gamma
for simplicity of notations

\mathbf{V}^{\pm}:=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v^{\pm}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v^{\pm},~\mathbf{B}^{\pm}:=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}b^{\pm}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}b^{\pm},~\mathbf{P}^{\pm}:=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}p^{\pm}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}p^{\pm},~\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}:=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{\pm}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q^{\pm}.

Note that we have

\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}=\mathbf{P}^{\pm}+b\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{3}c_{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}b^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}b^{\pm}}_{=:\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\gamma,\pm}}

for some constants
c_{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.

Step 1: Interior energy structure.

We test the equation (
3.11
) by
\mathbf{V}^{\pm}
in
\Omega^{\pm}
and integrate by parts to get one boundary term and several interior terms

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot(\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}-\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{=:R_{1}^{\pm}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle\pm\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}(\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot N)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+R_{1}^{\pm}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

Invoking the equation (
3.13
) for the evolution of
\mathbf{B}
in the first integral above, the energy of
\mathbf{B}^{\pm}
is produced.

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm})(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm})\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\underbrace{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{R_{2}^{\pm}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm})(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm})\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t},

where the first term in the last line is cancelled with the second integral in (
3.27
), and the analysis of the second term in the last line will be postponed.

The third term in (
3.27
) produces the energy of
(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}
with the help of equation (
3.12
).

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\underbrace{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})|\mathbf{P}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{R_{3}^{\pm}}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

The last term in (
3.27
) can be controlled by inserting again the continuity equation and integrating
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}
by parts. We have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}\right)\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}\right)\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t},

where the first term on the right side is controlled under time integral by

\delta\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(t)\right\|_{0}^{2}+P(E^{\kappa}_{4}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E^{\kappa}_{4}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~\forall 0<\delta\ll 1

and the second term, the third term on the right side can be both controlled by
P(E_{4}(t))
via direct computation because
\mathcal{R}_{q}
only contains 3-rd order tangential derivative of
b
.

The entropy is directly bounded by testing the transport equation of
\mathbf{S}^{\pm}
with
\mathbf{S}^{\pm}
itself

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}(S^{\pm})\,\mathbf{S}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\leq\|\mathbf{S}^{\pm}\|_{0}\|\rho^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\sqrt{E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)}.

(3.31)

The remainder terms are controlled by direct computation. For the commutator
\mathfrak{C},\mathfrak{D}
, we have
\|\mathfrak{C}(f^{\pm})\|_{0,\pm}\lesssim C(|\psi|_{4})\|f^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}
and
\|\mathfrak{D}(f^{\pm})\|_{0,\pm}\lesssim C(|\psi|_{4},|\partial_{t}\psi|_{L^{\infty}}|)\|f^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}
when
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
by straightforward computation. Note that the initial data is well-prepared in the sense that
\partial_{t}v|_{t=0}=O(1)
with respect to Mach number, so there is no loss of
\varepsilon
-weight in
\mathcal{R}_{v}
term. We have

\displaystyle R_{1}^{\pm}+R_{2}^{\pm}+R_{3}^{\pm}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

(3.32)

Step 2: The boundary regularity contributed by surface tension.

We denote
Z^{\pm}:=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm}+b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm})\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
to be the remaining interior terms presented above which should be controlled together with some boundary terms involving
\mathcal{W}^{\pm}
. Now we analyze the boundary integral in (
3.27
). The sum of two boundary integrals can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{+}(\mathbf{V}^{+}\cdot N)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{-}(\mathbf{V}^{-}\cdot N)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{+}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\partial_{3}q^{+})(\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi+(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi-\mathcal{W}^{+})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\partial_{3}q^{-})(\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi+(\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi-\mathcal{W}^{-})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q^{+}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi(\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{+}\mathcal{W}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\mathcal{W}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ ST}+\text{ ST}^{\prime}+\text{ VS}+\text{ RT}+\text{ RT}^{+}+\text{ RT}^{-}+ZB^{+}+ZB^{-}.

We will see that the term ST gives the
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted boundary regularity (contributed by surface tension) and the
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted boundary regularity (contributed by the two regularization terms) which help us control the terms
\text{ ST}^{\prime}
, VS, RT,
\text{ RT}^{\pm}
. The terms
ZB^{\pm}
will be controlled together with
Z^{\pm}
by using Gauss-Green formula. Do note that the slip conditions imply
\mathbf{V}_{3}^{\pm}=\mathbf{B}_{3}^{\pm}=\overline{\partial}{}\psi=0
on
\Sigma^{\pm}
, which eliminates all integrals on
\Sigma^{\pm}
.

Inserting the jump condition
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{H}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi_{t}
into the term ST, we get

\displaystyle\text{ ST}=

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\psi\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi_{t}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

Integrating by parts in the mean curvature term and using

\overline{\partial}{}\left(\frac{1}{|N|}\right)=\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}\psi}{|N|^{3}},~~~|N|=\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}},

we get

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\psi}{|N|}\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi}{|N|^{3}}\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\left(\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\frac{1}{|N|}\right]\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\nabla}_{i}\psi+\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}\right](\overline{\nabla}_{k}\psi\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\nabla}_{k}\psi\overline{\nabla}_{i}\psi)-\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\overline{\nabla}_{i}\psi\overline{\nabla}_{k}\psi]\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\nabla}_{k}\psi\right)\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}_{=:\text{ ST}_{1}^{R}}

which is further equal to

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.36)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{|N|}\right)\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\psi\right|^{2}-\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}\right)\left|\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}_{=:\text{ ST}_{2}^{R}}+\text{ ST}_{1}^{R}

The control of
\text{ ST}_{1}^{R},\text{ ST}_{2}^{R}
is straightforward which has been analyzed in the author’s previous paper
[
38
, (4.77)-(4.78)]
, so we only record the result here

\text{ ST}_{1}^{R}+\text{ ST}_{2}^{R}\lesssim P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\psi\right|_{0}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right|_{0}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

Using Cauchy’s inequality

\forall\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},~~\frac{|\mathbf{a}|^{2}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\mathbf{a}|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\geq\frac{|\mathbf{a}|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}},

(3.37)

we obtain the
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted boundary regularity

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ ST}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi_{t}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.38)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ ST}_{1}^{R}+\text{ ST}_{2}^{R}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

So far, we already obtain the boundary regularity
\sqrt{\sigma}\psi\in H^{5}(\Sigma),~\sqrt{\kappa}\psi\in H^{6}(\Sigma)
and
\sqrt{\kappa}\psi_{t}\in L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{5}([0,T]\times\Sigma)
. Using this, we can easily control
\text{ ST}^{\prime}
term in (
3.33
). Invoking again the boundary condition for
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket
, we get

\text{ ST}^{\prime}=\int_{\Sigma}\sigma\mathcal{H}(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi~(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{t}(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(3.39)

Integrating by parts
1-\overline{\Delta}{}
in the second term and
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle=\sqrt{1-\overline{\Delta}{}}
in the third term above, we can easily use the
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted energy to control the last two terms.

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi~(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right)(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right)~[1-\overline{\Delta}{},\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla}]\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

where the first term is controlled by
|\bar{v}^{+}|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\sqrt{\kappa}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi|_{0}^{2}
after integrating
\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla}
by parts and using the symmetry, and the second term is directly controlled by
|\bar{v}^{+}|_{W^{2,\infty}}|\sqrt{\kappa}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi|_{0}|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi|_{2}
. Similarly, we have for any
\delta\in(0,1)

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{t}(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{t}~\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle((\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.41)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{t}\right|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x^{\prime}}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}|\bar{v}^{+}|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\delta E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Picking
\delta>0
to be sufficiently small, the
\delta
-term can be absorbed by
E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)
. The first term in
{\text{ ST}}^{\prime}
is controlled in the same way if we integrating
\overline{\nabla}\cdot
by parts. Here we only list the result and refer the details to
[
38
, (4.87)-(4.89)]

\int_{\Sigma}\sigma\mathcal{H}(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})|\bar{v}^{+}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

(3.42)

Next we control the terms RT and
\text{ RT}^{\pm}
in (
3.33
). Note that we do not have the Rayleight-Taylor sign condition
\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket|_{\Sigma}\geq c_{0}>0
, so we have to use the
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted energy to control these terms, we have

\text{ RT}\leq|\partial_{3}q|_{L^{\infty}}|\psi|_{4}|\psi_{t}|_{4}\leq\sigma^{-1}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

(3.43)

Similarly, integrating
\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla
by parts in
\text{ RT}^{\pm}
and using symmetry, the terms
\text{ RT}^{\pm}
can be directly controlled

\text{ RT}^{\pm}\leq|\bar{v}^{\pm}\partial_{3}q|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\psi|_{4}^{2}\leq\sigma^{-1}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

(3.44)

Step 3: The crucial term for vortex sheets problem.

Now we study the term VS in (
3.33
) which appears to be the most problematic term for the vortex sheets problem. Note that we do not have any boundary condition for
q^{\pm}
individually. Thus, we may alternatively integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{1/2}
by parts and use (
B.5
) to control VS.

\displaystyle\text{ VS}=\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}\|_{0,-}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}q^{-}\|_{0,-}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bar{v}^{\pm}\|_{2,\pm}|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi|_{1/2}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))|\psi|_{5.5},

(3.45)

where we have used the Kato-Ponce inequality (cf. Lemma
B.6
) for
s=1/2,~p_{1}=2,~p_{2}=\infty,~q_{1}=q_{2}=4
and Sobolev embedding
H^{1/2}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. Now we need to control
|\psi|_{5.5}
via the jump condition of
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket
. Without the
\kappa
-regularization terms, we may use the ellipticity of the mean curvature operator to control
|\psi|_{5.5}
by
\sigma^{-1}|\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{3.5}
. Now, we can still prove analogous result for the
\kappa
-regularized jump condition.

Lemma 3.5

(Elliptic estimate for the free interface)
.

For any
s\geq 0.5
and
\kappa>0
, we have the uniform-in-
\kappa
estimate

|\psi|_{s+1.5}\leq|\psi_{0}|_{s+1.5}+\sigma^{-1}\left(P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-0.5}+|\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{s-0.5}\right).

Moreover, when
\kappa=0
,
|\psi_{0}|_{s+1.5}
is not needed

\displaystyle|\sigma\psi|_{s+1.5}\leq~P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\sigma\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-0.5}+|\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{s-0.5}.

(3.46)

Proof.

We take
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}
in the jump condition to get

-\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket=-\sigma\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi_{t}.

Testing this equation with
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi
in
L^{2}(\Sigma)
, we get

-\int_{\Sigma}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket~\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-0.5}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{0}|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+1.5}\psi|_{0}.

For the right side, we can mimic the treatment of ST term to obtain the boundary regularity. The two regularization terms can be directly controlled

\int_{\Sigma}\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi~\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\int_{\Sigma}\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi~(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\psi\right|_{s+2.5}^{2},

\int_{\Sigma}\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi_{t}~\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\overset{\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle}{=}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\psi\right|_{s+1.5}^{2}.

The term involving surface tension is controlled as follows

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)\cdot\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\overline{\nabla}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\left(\left[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-0.5},\frac{1}{|N|}\right]\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\overline{\nabla}_{i}\psi+\left[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-0.5},\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}\right](\overline{\nabla}_{k}\psi\cdot\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\overline{\nabla}_{k}\psi\overline{\nabla}_{i}\psi)-\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-0.5},\overline{\nabla}\psi]\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\overline{\nabla}_{i}\psi\right)\cdot\overline{\nabla}_{i}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s+0.5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

Using Kato-Ponce commutator estimate (cf. (
B.8
) in Lemma
B.6
), the commutators in the last line of the above identity are controlled by
P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-0.5}.
Using again Cauchy’s inequality (
3.37
), we conclude the elliptic estimate by

\displaystyle\sigma|\psi|_{s+1.5}^{2}+\kappa\left|\psi\right|_{s+2.5}^{2}+\kappa\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\psi\right|_{s+1.5}^{2}\leq~\left(P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\sigma\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-0.5}+|\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{s-0.5}\right)|\psi|_{s+1.5}.

In particular, Lemma
B.7
suggests that we have

|\psi|_{s+1.5}\leq|\psi_{0}|_{s+1.5}+\sigma^{-1}\left(P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\sigma\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-0.5}+|\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{s-0.5}\right).

Moreover, when
\kappa=0
,
|\psi_{0}|_{s+1.5}
no longer appears as we do not need Lemma
B.7

\displaystyle|\sigma\psi|_{s+1.5}\leq~P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\sigma\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-0.5}+|\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{s-0.5}.

(3.47)

∎

Now we can easily obtain the control for the problematic term VS by setting
s=4
in Lemma
3.5

\text{ VS}\lesssim|\psi_{0}|_{5.5}+\sigma^{-1}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

(3.48)

Step 4: A cancellation structure for the incompressible limit.

It remains to control the term
Z^{\pm}
and
ZB^{\pm}
. In
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
-estimates, each of these terms can be directly controlled. However, in the control of
E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)
and the control of full time derivatives, there will be extra technical difficulties due to the loss of Mach number or the anisotropy of the function spaces. Thus, we would like to present a robust approach to control these terms. We take
Z^{-}+ZB^{-}
as an example and the “+” case is controlled in the same way by reversing the sign when integrating by parts. Recall that
\mathbf{Q}^{-}=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q^{-}
, so we have

\displaystyle ZB^{-}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}(\partial_{3}v^{-}\cdot N)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\partial_{3}q^{-}(\partial_{3}v^{-}\cdot N)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\int_{\Sigma}\binom{4}{k}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v^{-}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{k}N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(3.49)

The first two terms in
ZB^{-}
can be directly controlled

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}(\partial_{3}v^{-}\cdot N)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\partial_{3}q^{-}(\partial_{3}v^{-}\cdot N)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left(|\overline{\partial}{}^{7/2}q^{-}|_{0}|\psi|_{4.5}+|\psi|_{4}^{2}\right)|\partial_{3}v\cdot N|_{1.5}\leq\left(\|q^{-}\|_{4,-}|\psi|_{4.5}+|\psi|_{4}^{2}\right)\|\partial v^{-}\|_{2,-}|\psi|_{2.5}\leq P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

The last term in
ZB^{-}
is controlled together with
Z^{-}:=-\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
. Recall that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{-})=

\displaystyle~(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}v_{i}^{-})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{4},\frac{\partial_{i}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\right]-\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{4},\partial_{i}\varphi,\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]+\partial_{i}\varphi\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{3}\varphi,~i=1,2

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{3}(f)=

\displaystyle~(\partial^{\varphi}_{3})^{2}v_{3}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi+\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{4},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}v_{3}^{-}\right]-\partial_{3}v_{3}^{-}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{3}\varphi.

Note that
\mathbf{N}_{i}=-\partial_{i}\varphi
for
i=1,2
, so we have

\displaystyle-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{4},\frac{\partial_{i}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\right]=\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{4},\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{3}\binom{4}{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\left(\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right)\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{3}\binom{4}{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}-\binom{4}{k}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{k},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]\partial_{i}\varphi\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-},

where the contribution of the first term above gives us (using Gauss-Green formula)

\displaystyle ZB^{-}-\sum_{k=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\binom{4}{k}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{3}\binom{4}{k}\left(\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v^{-}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{k}N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{3}\binom{4}{k}\left(\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{3}\binom{4}{k}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Now invoking
\mathbf{Q}^{-}=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}q^{-}-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q^{-}
and integrating one
\overline{\partial}{}
by parts, we find that

\sum_{k=1}^{3}\binom{4}{k}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v_{i}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim(\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{3}q^{-}\|_{0,-}+|\psi|_{4}\|\partial_{3}q^{-}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{-})})|\psi|_{4}\|v_{i}^{-}\|_{4,-}.

(3.51)

Among other terms in
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{-})
, we shall focus on the case when there are 4 derivatives falling on
v_{i}^{-}
and
\varphi
, and the control of these terms (lised below) appears to be easier.

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\quad\text{from the first term in }\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{-})

(3.52)

\displaystyle 4\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}\varphi~\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}\,\mathrm{d}x\quad\text{from the second term in }\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{-})\text{ when }\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\text{ falls on }\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}.

Note that
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v^{-}\cdot N=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-}-\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{-}
, we have

-\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\lesssim\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\right\|_{0,-}\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{-}}\partial_{3}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}p^{-}\right\|_{0,-}\left|\psi\right|_{4},

(3.53)

and

\displaystyle 4\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}\varphi~\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}\,\mathrm{d}x\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle 4\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}\varphi~\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\,\mathrm{d}x-4\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\partial_{3}\overline{\partial}{}\varphi~\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{-})\,\mathrm{d}x

(3.54)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{-}}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\right\|_{0,-}\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{-}}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}p^{-}\right\|_{0,-}+\|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\mathbf{Q}^{-}\|_{0,-}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v^{-}\|_{0,-}\right).

Thus, combining the estimates in the above four steps, we conclude the
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
-estimate by: For the tangential derivative
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}~(\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{4}=0,~\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}=4)
and for any
0<\delta<1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{S}^{\gamma,\pm},\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\gamma,\pm}\right)(t)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\overline{\nabla}\psi(t)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\psi(t)\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\partial_{t}\psi(\tau)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.55)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\psi_{0}\right|_{5.5+l}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{l}\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~~0\leq l\leq 4.

Remark 3.2
.

It should be noted that we only have the
L^{2}
control of
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{B},\mathbf{S}
and
(\mathcal{F}_{p})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}
in the tangential estimates, but the term
\mathbf{Q}
without
\mathcal{F}_{p}
-weight does appear in tangential estimates. When
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
contains at least one spatial derivative, that is,
\gamma_{0}<\langle\gamma\rangle
, one can invoke the momentum equation to replace
\mathcal{T}q
by
D_{t}^{\varphi}v
and
(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b
to avoid the loss of Mach number. This also suggests that we can actually control
\|\mathbf{P}\|_{0}
instead of only
\|\mathcal{F}_{p}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}\|_{0}
when there is at least one spatial derivatives. However, when
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
only consists of time derivatives, we cannot do such substitution any longer. Thus, we have to use the above cancellation structure between
ZB
and
Z
to control these two terms together.

3.3.2
The case
l>0
: No loss of regularity or weights of Mach number

Next we consider the tangential estimates for
\varepsilon
-weighted spatial derivatives, namely
\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}
for
1\leq l\leq 4
. The proof is parallel to the case
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
, but we have to check the following aspects

a.

We have to guarantee that there is no loss of
\mathcal{F}_{p}
-weight in various commutators, especially those involving
q
.

b.

When
l=4
, we only have tangential regularity for 8 derivatives. Due to the anisotropy of the function space
H_{*}^{8}
, we have to put extra efforts to reduce the terms involving the derivative
\overline{\partial}{}^{7}\partial_{3}
.

We only show the detailed modifications for the case
l=4
, that is, the
\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}^{8}
-estimate. When
1\leq l\leq 3
, similar modifications can be made in the same way.

Commutators of type
\varepsilon^{8}[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},f]\mathcal{T}g
for
\mathcal{T}=\overline{\partial}{}
or
D_{t}^{\varphi}

This type of commutator includes the following terms

\displaystyle-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\rho]D_{t}^{\varphi}v\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{v},~~-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{p}]D_{t}^{\varphi}p\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{p},

\displaystyle[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\bar{v}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{}f\text{ and }\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},v]\cdot\mathbf{N}\text{ in }\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f)

It is controlled directly by expanding the commutator. We have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{8}[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},f]\mathcal{T}g=

\displaystyle~(\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}^{8}f)\mathcal{T}g+8(\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{7}f)(\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}\mathcal{T}g)+28(\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}f)(\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathcal{T}g)+56(\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{5}f)(\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\mathcal{T}g)

\displaystyle+70(\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}f)(\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\mathcal{T}g)+56(\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}f)(\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{5}\mathcal{T}g)+28(\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}f)(\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}\mathcal{T}g)+8(\overline{\partial}{}f)(\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}^{7}\mathcal{T}g),

whose
L^{2}(\Omega)
norm is controlled by

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}^{8}f\|_{0}\|\mathcal{T}g\|_{L^{\infty}}+8\varepsilon^{2}\|\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{7}f\|_{0}\|\overline{\partial}{}\mathcal{T}g\|_{L^{\infty}}+28\|\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}f\|_{L^{6}}\|\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathcal{T}g\|_{L^{3}}+56\|\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{5}f\|_{L^{6}}\|\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\mathcal{T}g\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle~70\|\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}f\|_{L^{3}}\|\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\mathcal{T}g\|_{L^{6}}+56\|\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}f\|_{L^{3}}\|\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{5}\mathcal{T}g\|_{L^{6}}+28\|\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{6}g\|_{L^{6}}\|\varepsilon^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathcal{T}f\|_{L^{3}}+8\varepsilon^{2}\|\varepsilon^{6}\overline{\partial}{}^{7}g\|_{0}\|\overline{\partial}{}\mathcal{T}f\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~(1+\varepsilon^{2})\left(\sqrt{E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)E_{4}^{\kappa}(t})+\sqrt{E_{7}^{\kappa}(t)E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)}+\sqrt{E_{7}^{\kappa}(t)E_{5}^{\kappa}(t)}\right),

where we use the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{6}
and
H^{1}\hookrightarrow H^{1/2}\hookrightarrow L^{3}
in 3D. In 2D case, we can replace
(L^{6},L^{3})
by
(L^{4},L^{4})
and use Ladyženskaya’s inequality
\|f\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\leq\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial f\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|f\|_{1}^{2}
to obtain the same bound.

Commutator
\varepsilon^{8}[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},b]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}f
for
f=b,v

This term appears when we commute
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
with
(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})
. Note that we can rewrite the directional derivative to be
(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})=\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(b\cdot\mathbf{N})\partial_{3}
. When commuting
\overline{\partial}{}^{8}
with
\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla}
, the estimate is exactly the same as
\varepsilon^{8}[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},f]\mathcal{T}g
. For the commutator
[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(b\cdot\mathbf{N})]\partial_{3}f
, we just need to put extra effort on the term
8\overline{\partial}{}((\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(b\cdot\mathbf{N}))\overline{\partial}{}^{7}\partial_{3}f
because the length of the multi-index exceeds 8 when
|x_{3}|\lesssim 1
. (Recall that the weight function
\omega(x_{3})
is comparable to
|x_{3}|
when
x_{3}\lesssim 1
and is comparable to 1 when
|x_{3}|\gg 1
.) In this case, we notice that
b\cdot\mathbf{N}|_{\Sigma}=0
, and thus its interior value can be expressed via the fundamental theorem of calculus

(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(b\cdot\mathbf{N})(x^{\prime},x_{3})=0+\int_{0}^{x_{3}}\partial_{3}\left((\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(b\cdot\mathbf{N})(x^{\prime},\xi_{3})\right)\mathrm{d}\xi_{3},

whose
L^{\infty}(\Omega)
norm is controlled by
C\omega(x_{3})\|\partial_{3}(b\cdot\mathbf{N})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}
.

Commutator
\mathfrak{D}(f)
for
f=v,~p,~b,~S

Among all terms in (
3.2
), we need to further analyze the third term, that is, the commutator
\varepsilon^{8}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi),\partial_{3}f\right]
for
f=v,b,p
. The problem is the same as above, that is,
\overline{\partial}{}^{7}
may fall on
\partial_{3}f
which is not directly controllable. Again, we notice that there is only one
\overline{\partial}{}
falling on
\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)
and
(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)|_{\Sigma}=0
, so we can use the same method (as in the control of
\varepsilon^{8}[\overline{\partial}{}^{8},b]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}f
) to control this commutator.

Commutator
\mathfrak{C}(q)

The problematic term is
-8(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(\overline{\partial}{}\mathbf{N}_{i})(\overline{\partial}{}^{7}\partial_{3}q)
arising from
[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathbf{N}_{i}/\partial_{3}\varphi,\partial_{3}q]
. To control this term, we can invoke the third component of the momentum equation to convert
\partial_{3}q
to tangential derivatives of other quantities

-\partial_{3}q=(\partial_{3}\varphi)\left(\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{3}-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b_{3}\right),

where
D_{t}^{\varphi}=\partial_{t}+\bar{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}
and
(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})=\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(b\cdot\mathbf{N})\partial_{3}
are both tangential derivatives. Also, there is no loss of weight of Mach number in this term because one can always replace
\overline{\partial}{}q
by
D_{t}^{\varphi}v
and
(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b
.

Commutator
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i})

The problematic term is
-8(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(\overline{\partial}{}\mathbf{N}_{i})(\overline{\partial}{}^{7}\partial_{3}v_{i})
arising from
[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathbf{N}_{i}/\partial_{3}\varphi,\partial_{3}v_{i}]
. In fact, this term may not be controlled independently, but its contribution only appears in
-\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
which has been analyzed in step 4 of Section
3.3.1
. Specifically, its contribution in the term
Z
, after combining it with
ZB
term, is

8\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{3}(\overline{\partial}{}^{8}q-\overline{\partial}{}^{8}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q)~\overline{\partial}{}\mathbf{N}_{i}~\overline{\partial}{}^{7}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x,

which is controlled by
(\|\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}^{7}\partial_{3}q\|_{0}+|\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}^{8}\psi|_{0}\|\partial q\|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}\|\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}^{8}v\|_{0}
after integrating one
\overline{\partial}{}
by parts. Then we convert
\partial_{3}q
to tangential derivatives of other quantities via the momentum equation, which has been presented in the control of
\mathfrak{C}(q)
.

Based on the above analysis, we can follow the same method as in
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
-estimate to prove the following inequality for
\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}
-estimates
(1\leq l\leq 4)
for the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximate problem (
3.1
): For any
0<\delta<1
and fixed
l\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\left(\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{S}^{\gamma,\pm},\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\gamma,\pm}\right)(t)\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\overline{\nabla}\psi(t)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\psi(t)\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}\partial_{t}\psi(\tau)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.56)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\psi_{0}\right|_{5.5+l}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{l}\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where
\left(\mathbf{V}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{S}^{\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{P}^{\gamma,\pm}\right)
represent that Alinhac good unknowns of
(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm},p^{\pm})
with respect to
\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l}
.

3.4
Tangential estimates: full time derivatives

Now we control the full time derivatives, that is, the
\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}
estimates for
0\leq l\leq 4
. We will take the most difficult case
l=4
for an example, that is, the
\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}
-estimate. The other cases
(0\leq l\leq 3)
can be treated in the same way.

3.4.1
Replacing one time derivative by a material derivative

Following the analysis in Section
3.3.1
and Section
3.3.2
, we expect to control the following norms

\left\|\varepsilon^{8}\left(\mathbf{V}^{\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\pm},\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm},\mathbf{S}^{\pm}\right)\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left|\varepsilon^{8}\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{1}^{2}+\left|\varepsilon^{8}\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\varepsilon^{8}\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{9}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2},

which further gives the control of
\left\|\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}p^{\pm},S^{\pm})\right\|_{0}^{2}
. However, there are several extra difficulties that may make our previous method invalid.

a.

We cannot substitute
\partial q
by
\mathcal{T}(v,b)
because there is no spatial derivative.

b.

\partial_{t}^{4+l}p
has weight
\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{2l}=O(\varepsilon^{1+2l})
instead of
\varepsilon^{2l}
. There might be a loss of
\varepsilon
-weight.

c.

\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}q
only has
L^{2}(\Omega)
regularity, so the trace lemma is no longer valid.

d.

We cannot integrate by parts for “half-order time derivative”
\partial_{t}^{1/2}
. Thus, the control of VS term will be rather different.

To overcome the abovementioned difficulties, especially (c) and (d) in the control of the crucial boundary term VS, we would like to replace the full-time derivative
\partial_{t}^{4+l}
by
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}
where
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}=\partial_{t}+\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}
and
v^{-}|_{\Omega^{+}}
is defined to be the Sobolev extension of
v^{-}
in
\Omega^{+}
. We aim to prove the following estimates.

Proposition 3.6
.

Fix
l\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
. For
\varepsilon^{2l}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}
-differentiated approximate system
(0\leq l\leq 4)
, we have the following uniform-in-
(\kappa,\varepsilon)
estimate for any
0<\delta<1

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\left(\mathbf{V}^{*,\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{B}^{*,\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{S}^{*,\gamma,\pm},(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{1/2}\mathbf{P}^{*,\gamma,\pm}\right)(t)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\overline{\nabla}\psi(t)\right|_{0}^{2}

(3.57)

\displaystyle+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\psi(t)\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\partial_{t}\psi(\tau)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\sum_{j=0}^{l}P(E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~~0\leq l\leq 4,

where
\left(\mathbf{V}^{*,\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{B}^{*,\gamma,\pm},\mathbf{S}^{*,\gamma,\pm},(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{1/2}\mathbf{P}^{*,\gamma,\pm}\right)
represent the Alinhac good unknowns of
(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm},p^{\pm})
with respect to
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}
, that is,
\mathbf{F}^{*,\gamma,\pm}=D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}f^{\pm}-(D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\varphi)\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f^{\pm}
.

For the case
l=4
, we introduce the Alinhac good unknowns with respect to
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}

(\mathbf{V}^{*,\pm},\mathbf{B}^{*,\pm},\mathbf{P}^{*,\pm},\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm},\mathbf{S}^{*,\pm}):=D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},p^{\pm},q^{\pm},S^{\pm})-(D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi)\partial^{\varphi}_{3}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},p^{\pm},q^{\pm},S^{\pm}).

They satisfy

D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}f^{\pm}=\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\mathbf{F}^{*,\pm}+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{*}(f^{\pm}),~~D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}f^{\pm}=D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{F}^{*,\pm}+\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{*}(f^{\pm})f,

where
\mathfrak{C}^{*}(f),\mathfrak{D}^{*}(f)
are defined in the same way as (
3.2
)-(
3.2
) by replacing
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
with
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}
. The boundary conditions of these good unknowns are

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\mathbf{Q}^{*}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\mathcal{H}-\kappa\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi-\kappa\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi-\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi

(3.58)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}^{*,\pm}\cdot N=\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi+(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi-\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi-\mathcal{W}^{*,\pm},

(3.59)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{*\pm}=(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot N)\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi+[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},N_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}],

(3.60)

where we use the fact that
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}|_{\Sigma}=\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}=\partial_{t}+\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}
. Note that
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}
does not directly commute with
\partial_{t}
or
\partial_{i}
, so there is an extra term
-\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi
in the expression of
\mathbf{V}^{\pm,*}\cdot N
.

3.4.2
Analysis of the interior commutators

Since we replaced
\partial_{t}^{8}
with
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}
and
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
does not directly commute with
\partial_{3}
, we need to further analyze the commutators
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f)
for
f=q
and
v_{i}
and
\mathfrak{D}(f)
for
f=v,b,p,S
. The problematic thing is that
\partial_{3}
may fall on
(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)
(in
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
) and produce a normal derivative without a weight function that vanishes on
\Sigma
, which may introduce a second-order derivative in the setting of anisotropic Sobolev space. This problem does not appear in
\mathfrak{D}(f)
, as we find that such commutator has the form
(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\partial_{3}]f
which already includes a weight
(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)
that vanishes on
\Sigma
. In
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f)
, according (
3.2
), we need to further analyze the term
\mathbf{N}_{i}(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\partial_{3}]f
for
f=q,v_{i}
. Using
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}=\partial_{t}+\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{N}_{i}(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\partial_{3}]f=\mathbf{N}_{i}(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}[D_{t}^{\varphi,-},\partial_{3}]\partial_{t}^{7}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{N}_{i}(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\partial_{3}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}f+\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}\left((\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\right)\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}f.

The first term above can be directly controlled in
L^{2}
because only tangential derivative falls on
\partial_{t}^{7}f
. For the second term, we can invoke the momentum equation and the continuity equation to convert this normal derivative to a tangential derivative.

•

When
f=q
, we use
-\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q=\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{3}-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b_{3}
.

•

When
f=v_{i}
, using
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v=\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}+\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N}
, the continuity equation becomes
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N}=-\varepsilon^{2}D_{t}^{\varphi}p-\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}
. Thus we have
\partial_{t}^{7}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N}=-\partial_{t}^{7}(\varepsilon^{2}D_{t}^{\varphi}p+\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v})+[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}]\cdot\partial_{3}v
in which both terms can be directly controlled in
\|\cdot\|_{8,*}
norm.

Also note that there is no extra loss of Mach number even if
\partial_{t}^{8}p
requires one more
\varepsilon
-weight. In fact, the only term in the commutators
\mathfrak{C},\mathfrak{D}
that contains
\partial_{t}^{8}p
is
\mathcal{R}_{p}
, but there is an extra weight
\mathcal{F}_{p}=O(\varepsilon^{2})
multiplying on it. Therefore, we can follow the same strategy presented in Section
3.3.1
and Section
3.3.2
to analyze the interior part. We can prove the following energy identity

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{\varepsilon^{16}}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|\mathbf{V}^{*,\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{*,\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{*,\pm})^{2}+\rho^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{*,\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{ ST}^{*}+{\text{ ST}^{*}}^{\prime}+\text{ VS}^{*}+\text{ RT}^{*}+\sum_{\pm}\text{ RT}^{*,\pm}+ZB^{*,\pm}+Z^{*,\pm}+R_{\Sigma}^{*,\pm}+R_{\Omega}^{*,\pm},

where

\displaystyle\text{ ST}^{*}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(3.62)

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}^{*}}^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(3.63)

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{*}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(3.64)

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{*}:=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(3.65)

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{*,\pm}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q^{\pm}\,\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(3.66)

\displaystyle R_{\Sigma}^{*,\pm}:=

\displaystyle\pm\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(3.67)

\displaystyle ZB^{*,\pm}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}\mathcal{W}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},~~Z^{*,\pm}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{16}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm,*}\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{*}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t},

(3.68)

and
R_{\Omega}^{*,\pm}
represents the controllable terms in the interior containing the analogues of
R_{1}^{\pm},R_{2}^{\pm},R_{3}^{\pm}
. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-16}R_{\Omega}^{*,\pm}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{*,\pm}\cdot(\mathcal{R}_{v}^{*,\pm}-\mathfrak{C}^{*}(q^{\pm}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{*,\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{*,\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{*,\pm}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{b}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{*,\pm}\mathcal{R}_{p}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})|\mathbf{B}^{*,\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})|\mathbf{P}^{*,\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}^{*}(S^{\pm})\,\mathbf{S}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.69)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{v}^{*,\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},b^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}b^{\pm}-[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\rho^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}-\rho^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}^{*}(v^{\pm}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{p}^{*,\pm}:=

\displaystyle-[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}^{*}(p^{\pm}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{b}^{*,\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},b^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}v^{\pm}-\mathfrak{D}^{*}(b^{\pm}),\quad\mathcal{R}_{q}^{*,\pm}:=~\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}-\mathbf{P}^{*,\pm}-b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{*,\pm}.

These terms can be directly controlled in the same way as presented in Section
3.3.1
, so we omit the details

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}R_{\Omega}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.70)

3.4.3
Analysis of the boundary integrals

Similarly as in Section
3.3.1
, we can decompose the control of these terms in the following steps.

Step 1: Boundary regularity of full time derivatives given by surface tension.

Invoking the boundary condition (
3.58
) for
\left\llbracket\mathbf{Q}^{*}\right\rrbracket
, the term ST becomes

\displaystyle\text{ ST}^{*}=

\displaystyle\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.71)

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ ST}_{0}^{*}+\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*}+\text{ ST}_{2,\kappa}^{*}.

Commuting
\overline{\nabla}\cdot
with
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}
, we have

\text{ ST}_{0}^{*}=\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(\overline{\nabla}\psi/|N|)\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\underbrace{\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{7}(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi/|N|)\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}_{\text{ ST}_{0}^{*,R}}

(3.72)

Integrating
\overline{\nabla}\cdot
by parts in the mean curvature term, we get an analogous energy term contributed by surface tension as in Section
3.3.1

\displaystyle\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.73)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi}{|N|}\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi}{|N|^{3}}\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi}{|N|}\cdot\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi}{|N|^{3}}\overline{\nabla}\psi\,\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}_{=:\text{ ST}_{1}^{*,R}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\left[\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{6},\frac{1}{|N|}\right]\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\psi+\left[\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{6},\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}\right]((\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\psi)\overline{\nabla}\psi)-\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}[\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{6},\overline{\nabla}\psi]\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\psi\right)\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}_{=:\text{ ST}_{2}^{*,R}}

where the right side is further equal to

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma\varepsilon^{16}}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.74)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\sigma\varepsilon^{16}}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{|N|}\right)\left|\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi\right|^{2}-\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{|N|^{3}}\right)\left|\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}_{=:\text{ ST}_{3}^{*,R}}+\text{ ST}_{1}^{*,R}+\text{ ST}_{2}^{*,R}.

The first line above together with the inequality (
3.37
) gives the
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted boundary regularity as in step 2 in Section
3.3.1
. The term
\text{ ST}_{1}^{*,R}
is generated by commuting
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}
with
\overline{\nabla}
(the one falling on
\partial_{t}^{7}\psi
) and is directly controlled by the energy. The term
\text{ ST}_{3}^{*,R}
is controlled in the same way as
\text{ ST}_{2}^{R}
in step 2 of Section
3.3
. The term
\text{ ST}_{2}^{*,R}
is controlled by integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts under time integral, which was also analyzed in
[
38
, Section 4.6]
. The term
\text{ ST}_{0}^{*,R}
is controlled by integrating by parts in
\partial_{t}
and then in
\overline{\partial}{}_{j}
under time integral (which is similar to
\text{ ST}_{2}^{*,R}
). Thus, we conclude their estimates by

\int_{0}^{t}\text{ ST}_{0}^{*,R}+\text{ ST}_{1}^{*,R}+\text{ ST}_{2}^{*,R}+\text{ ST}_{3}^{*,R}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.75)

Next we analyze the terms
\text{ ST}^{*}_{1,\kappa},\text{ ST}^{*}_{2,\kappa}
involving the
\kappa
-regularization terms. Note that we have to commute
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}
with
1-\overline{\Delta}{}
or
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle=\sqrt{1-\overline{\Delta}{}}
when deriving the
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighte terms. Integrating
1-\overline{\Delta}{}
by parts in
\text{ ST}^{*}_{1,\kappa}

\displaystyle\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*}=-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.76)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\,\partial_{t}\left(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}[\overline{D_{t}^{-}},1-\overline{\Delta}{}]\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\,\partial_{t}\left([1-\overline{\Delta}{},\overline{D_{t}^{-}}]\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right|_{0}^{2}+\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{11,\kappa}+\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{12,\kappa}

On
\Sigma
, the material derivative
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}=\overline{D_{t}^{-}}=\partial_{t}+\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}
, so the commutator is

[\overline{D_{t}^{-}},1-\overline{\Delta}{}]f=[\overline{\Delta}{},\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}]f=\overline{\Delta}{}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}f+2\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}f.

Then
\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{11,\kappa}
is controlled under time integral by integrating
\overline{\partial}{}_{j}
by parts in the second term

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{11,\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\Delta}{}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+2\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\text{ lower order terms}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{2}^{2}|\overline{\partial}{}\bar{v}^{-}|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau),E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.77)

The control of
\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{12,\kappa}
is easier because there is no term containing 9 time derivatives of
\psi
. It is directly controlled by using the
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted boundary energy obtained above.
