\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{12,\kappa}\lesssim\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right|_{0}\left(\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{2}\right)\sqrt{E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)}\leq E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)\sqrt{E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)}.

The control of
\text{ ST}_{2,\kappa}^{*}
is similar to
\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*}
. Using
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{2}=1-\overline{\Delta}{}
, we have

\displaystyle\text{ ST}_{2,\kappa}^{*}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{8}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.78)

\displaystyle+\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}[\overline{D_{t}^{-}},\overline{\partial}{}_{i}]\left(\partial_{t}^{8}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi\right)\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{8}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi\,\left([\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t},\overline{D_{t}^{-}}]\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{8}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{21,\kappa}+\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{22,\kappa},

where we use the concrete form of the commutators

[\overline{D_{t}^{-}},\overline{\partial}{}_{i}]=-\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}_{j}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}f,~~[\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t},\overline{D_{t}^{-}}]f=\partial_{t}\left(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}_{j}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}f\right)+\partial_{t}\bar{v}_{j}^{-}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}f

to get estimates similar to
\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{11,\kappa}
and
\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{12,\kappa}

\int_{0}^{t}\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{21,\kappa}+\text{ ST}^{*,R}_{22,\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim~\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau),E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Hence, the control of
\text{ ST}^{*}
in (
3.61
) is concluded by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ ST}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(3.79)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{8}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

The term
{\text{ ST}^{*}}^{\prime}
is controlled in the same way as
\text{ ST}^{*}
by replacing
\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi
with
(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi
. We no longer get energy terms, but we can integrate
(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})
by parts and use symmetry and the above boundary regularity to control them. Invoking the jump condition, we have

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}^{*}}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle~\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\mathcal{H}\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{0}^{*}}^{\prime}+{\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*}}^{\prime}+{\text{ ST}_{2,\kappa}^{*}}^{\prime}.

(3.80)

Following the analysis (
3.72
)-(
3.75
), the first term is controlled thanks to the boundary regularity and symmetric structure after integrating
(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})
by parts.

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{0}^{*}}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2}\sigma\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{+})\left(\frac{|\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi|^{2}}{|N|}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{\nabla}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi|^{2}}{|N|^{3}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}})|\bar{v}^{+}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{\nabla}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}.

(3.81)

Similarly, we can use the symmetric structure to control
{\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*}}^{\prime}+{\text{ ST}_{2,\kappa}^{*}}^{\prime}
. We only check the commutators arising in the control of
{\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*}}^{\prime}
as an example.

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}[\overline{D_{t}^{-}},1-\overline{\Delta}{}]\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left([1-\overline{\Delta}{},\overline{D_{t}^{-}}]\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,[1-\overline{\Delta}{},(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})]\left(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{11,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}+{\text{ ST}_{12,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}+{\text{ ST}_{13,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}.

(3.82)

The control of
{\text{ ST}_{11,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}+{\text{ ST}_{12,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}
is similar to
{\text{ ST}_{11,\kappa}^{*,R}}+{\text{ ST}_{12,\kappa}^{*,R}}
. We have

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{11,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\Delta}{}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+2\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\bar{v}^{-}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,\overline{\partial}{}_{j}(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|\bar{v}^{-}|_{W^{2,\infty}}|\bar{v}^{+}|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{3}\left(\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{3}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{2}\right)\lesssim E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)E_{8}^{\kappa}(t),

(3.83)

and

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{12,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}\lesssim~|\bar{v}^{-}|_{W^{2,\infty}}|\bar{v}^{+}|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{3}\right)^{2}\lesssim E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)E_{8}^{\kappa}(t).

(3.84)

The extra term
{\text{ ST}_{13,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}
is also directly controlled

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{13,\kappa}^{*,R}}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle~\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\left(\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right)\,\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\bar{v}_{j}^{+}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}+2\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\bar{v}_{j}^{+}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\right)\left(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|\bar{v}^{+}|_{W^{2,\infty}}|\bar{v}^{-}|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{3}\right)^{2}\lesssim E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)E_{8}^{\kappa}(t).

(3.85)

Thus we have

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}_{1,\kappa}^{*}}^{\prime}\lesssim\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{+})\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\right|^{2}+E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)E_{8}^{\kappa}(t).

(3.86)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}{\text{ ST}_{2,\kappa}^{*}}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=

\displaystyle~\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}[\overline{D_{t}^{-}},\overline{\partial}{}_{i}](\partial_{t}^{8}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi)\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{8}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi)\,\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\left((\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\kappa\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\partial_{t}\psi\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{8}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|\bar{v}^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\left(\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{3}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi\right|_{2}^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.87)

Hence, we have the estimate of
{\text{ ST}^{*}}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}{\text{ ST}^{*}}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.88)

What’s more, we can also control the remainder term
R_{\Sigma}^{*,\pm}:=\pm\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.
Indeed, we use Gauss-Green formula to write it to be an interior intergral.

\displaystyle R_{\Sigma}^{*,\pm}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}\,\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x

(3.89)

Recall that
\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}=D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}-D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\partial_{3}q^{\pm}
. Note that
[\partial^{\varphi}_{3},D_{t}^{\varphi,-}]\partial_{t}^{7}q=\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{j}^{-}\partial^{\varphi}_{j}q=(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}q+(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})\partial_{t}^{7}\partial_{3}q
, so one can still convert
\partial q
to a tangential derivative of
v,b
. We now integrate by parts
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}
to get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}R_{\Sigma}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial_{3}(\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}-\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial_{3}q^{\pm})\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial_{3}(\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}-\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial_{3}q^{\pm})\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x.

(3.90)

Using the reduction for
\partial_{3}q
again, we can control the above integral by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}R_{\Sigma}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\delta\|\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.91)

Step 2: Control of VS term.

Now we start to analyze the most difficult boundary term

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{*}:=~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(3.92)

Note that there is no spatial derivative
\overline{\partial}{}
in
\text{ VS}^{*}
, so we cannot integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{1/2}
by parts as in step 3 in Section
3.3.1
. To overcome this difficulty, we try to rewrite the term
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi
by invoking the kinematic boundary condition

\displaystyle\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi=

\displaystyle~\partial_{t}^{8}\psi+\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi=\partial_{t}^{7}(v^{-}\cdot N)-v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}N

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot N+[\partial_{t}^{7},N_{i},v_{i}^{-}],

and thus

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{*}=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})[\partial_{t}^{7},N_{i},v_{i}^{-}]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{*}_{0}+\text{ VS}^{*,ZB}_{1}+\text{ VS}^{*,ZB}_{2}

(3.93)

Using divergence theorem, we convert
\text{ VS}^{*}_{0}
to an interior integral in
\Omega^{-}

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{0}^{*}=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\left((\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\text{ VS}^{*}_{01}+\text{ VS}^{*}_{02},

(3.94)

where
\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket=\bar{v}^{+}-\bar{v}^{-}
is defined via Sobolev extension in
\Omega^{-}
. In
\text{ VS}^{*}_{01}
, we want to commute
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot
with
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
in order to get a similar cancellation structure as in
ZB+Z
. The commutator is

\displaystyle[\partial^{\varphi}_{i},\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}]f=

\displaystyle~\overline{\partial}{}_{i}(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}f)-\frac{\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\partial_{3}(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}f)-(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}f-\frac{\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\partial_{3}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket_{j})(\overline{\partial}{}_{j}f)-\frac{\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi}(\partial_{3}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket_{j})(\overline{\partial}{}_{j}f)+\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right)\partial_{3}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}f-(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f+\mathbf{N}_{i}\frac{(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{3}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f,~~i=1,2,

\displaystyle[\partial^{\varphi}_{3},\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}]f=

\displaystyle~\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}f+\frac{(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{3}\varphi}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f.

Commuting
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot
with
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
, we get

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{*}_{01}=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\left((\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{3}\varphi\,\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{*}_{011}+\text{ VS}^{*,Z}_{011}+\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{011}+\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{012}

(3.95)

Next we introduce
\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}:=\partial_{t}^{7}f-\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f
to be the Alinhac good unknown of
f
with respect to
\partial_{t}^{7}
in order to commute
\nabla^{\varphi}
with
\partial_{t}^{7}
. Namely, we have

\partial_{t}^{7}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}f=\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\sharp}(f),~~\partial_{t}^{7}D_{t}^{\varphi}f=D_{t}^{\varphi}\mathbf{F}^{\sharp}+\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}(f),

where
\mathfrak{C}^{\sharp},\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}
are defined in the same way as (
3.2
)-(
3.2
) with
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\partial_{t}^{7}
. With this formulation, we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}+\partial^{\varphi}_{i}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{i}^{-})=\partial_{t}^{7}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\sharp}(v_{i}^{-})+\partial^{\varphi}_{i}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{i}^{-}).

Now we insert the good unknowns in
\text{ VS}^{*}_{011}
to get

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{011}^{*}=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\underbrace{-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\sharp}(v_{i}^{-})-\partial^{\varphi}_{i}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{i}^{-})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{\text{ VS}^{*,Z}_{012}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}p^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}p^{-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}(\frac{1}{2}|b^{-}|^{2})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\text{ VS}^{*,Z}_{012}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{*}_{0111}+\text{ VS}^{*,B}_{0111}+\text{ VS}^{*,Z}_{012}.

(3.96)

By the definition of
\mathbf{P}^{\sharp,-}

D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}p^{-}=D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{P}^{\sharp,-}+D_{t}^{\varphi,-}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}p^{-}),~~\partial_{t}^{7}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}p^{-}=D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{P}^{\sharp}+\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}(p^{-}).

Then we integrate
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
by parts and use symmetry to find

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{0111}^{*}=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{P}^{\sharp,-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{P}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle\underbrace{+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{P}^{\sharp,-}\left((\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket)D_{t}^{\varphi,-}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}p^{-})-(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}(p^{-})-D_{t}^{\varphi,-}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}p^{-})\right)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{0111}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}p^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}(p^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{0112}}

\displaystyle\overset{\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}}{===}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket)(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{8}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{P}^{\sharp,-})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{0111}+\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{0112},

(3.97)

where the first term on the right side is controlled by
\left\|(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{8}\mathbf{P}^{*,-}\right\|_{0}^{2}\|\overline{\nabla}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}
. Next we adapt the analysis for
Z^{\pm}+ZB^{\pm}
term to the control of
\text{ VS}_{1}^{*,ZB}+\text{ VS}_{011}^{*,Z}
and
\text{ VS}_{2}^{*,ZB}+\text{ VS}_{012}^{*,Z}
. Using Gauss-Green formula, we have

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{1}^{*,ZB}+\text{ VS}_{011}^{*,Z}=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}[\partial^{\varphi}_{3},D_{t}^{\varphi,-}](\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}+D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\left((\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\right)\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\text{ VS}_{1}^{*,ZR}.

(3.98)

The main term is controlled by integrating
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
by parts under time integral and invoking the momentum equation to replace
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q^{-}
by tangential derivatives of
v^{-},b^{-}
:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-})\,D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\left((\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{N}\cdot\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\bigg|^{t}_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\|\varepsilon^{8}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\|_{0,-}^{2}+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0))E_{8}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\|\varepsilon^{8}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\|_{0,-}\|\varepsilon^{8}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-},\partial_{t}^{8}v^{-})\|_{0}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0))E_{8}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~~\forall\delta\in(0,1).

(3.99)

For
\text{ VS}_{2}^{*,ZB}+\text{ VS}_{012}^{*,Z}
, we recall that the term
\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\sharp}(v_{i}^{-})
in
\text{ VS}_{012}^{*,Z}
includes a term
[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i}/\partial_{3}\varphi,v_{i}^{-}]
which also appears in
\text{ VS}_{2}^{*,ZB}
. Thus we can again use the Gauss-Green formula to analyze this term. Let us first compute the commutator in
\text{ VS}_{012}^{*,Z}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\sharp}(v_{i}^{-})-\partial^{\varphi}_{i}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{i}^{-})=

\displaystyle-\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{i}+\left[\partial_{t}^{7},\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\right]-\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\left[\partial_{t}^{7}\mathbf{N}_{i},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]-\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\left[\partial_{t}^{6},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{i}+\left[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\right]+\sum_{k=1}^{6}\binom{7}{k}[\partial_{t}^{k},(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}]\mathbf{N}_{i}\,\partial_{t}^{7-k}\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}

\displaystyle-\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\left[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]-\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\left[\partial_{t}^{6},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\varphi

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\left[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\right]+\mathfrak{C}^{\sharp,R}_{i}(v_{i}^{-})

Then

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{2}^{*,ZB}+\text{ VS}_{012}^{*,Z}=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})[\partial_{t}^{7},N_{i},v_{i}^{-}]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left(\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\left[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{-}\right]\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathfrak{C}^{\sharp,R}_{i}(v_{i}^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{\text{ VS}_{2}^{*,ZR}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\left(D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\right)(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},v_{i}^{-}]\,\mathrm{d}x+\text{ VS}_{2}^{*,ZR}+\text{ lower order terms},

(3.100)

where the first term on the right side is again controlled by integrating
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
by parts under time integral. We omit the details and just list the result

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\varepsilon^{16}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\left(D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\right)(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})[\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},v_{i}^{-}]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0))E_{8}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~~\forall\delta\in(0,1).

Now the term
\text{ VS}_{1}^{*}
is controlled except for those remainder terms
\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{011}
,
\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{012}
,
\text{ VS}^{*,B}_{0111}
,
\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{0111}
,
\text{ VS}^{*,R}_{0112}
,
\text{ VS}_{1}^{*,ZR}
and
\text{ VS}_{2}^{*,ZR}
. In fact, apart from
\text{ VS}^{*,B}_{0111}
, the other remainder terms can be directly controlled by counting the number of derivatives and invoking the reduction for
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}
and
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}
. There is no loss of Mach number in these remainder terms. In fact, when
\partial_{t}^{8}p^{-}
appears in the remainder terms, either we have
\varepsilon^{16}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}
-weight to control it directly, or we can integrate by parts
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
and
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
under time integral to move one time derivative to
v_{i}^{-}
. Besides, the control of
\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi,\partial_{t}^{8}\varphi
depends on the boundary regularity contributed by surface tension and so depends on
\sigma^{-1}
. Therefore, we can conclude the estimates of
\text{ VS}_{1}^{*}
by

\text{ VS}_{01}^{*}+\text{ VS}^{*,ZB}_{1}+\text{ VS}^{*,ZB}_{2}\leq\text{ VS}^{*,B}_{0111}+\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0))E_{8}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau~~~\forall\delta\in(0,1).

(3.101)

Next we control
\text{ VS}_{02}^{*}=\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
. First, we commute
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
with
\partial^{\varphi}_{i}
to get

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{02}^{*}=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}v_{j}^{-}\partial^{\varphi}_{j}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{021}^{*}+\text{ VS}_{021}^{*,R}.

(3.102)

In the first term, we integrate by parts
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
under time integral and commute
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
with
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
to get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}_{021}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\bigg|^{t}_{0}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}\,[D_{t}^{\varphi,-},(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})]\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\text{ VS}_{022}^{*,R}+\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}_{023}^{*,R}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.103)

Next we insert the good unknowns
\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp,-}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}
and invoke again the momentum equation
\rho^{-}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}-(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}=-\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp,-}+\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\sharp,-}-\mathfrak{C}^{\sharp}(q^{-})
to get

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*}=

\displaystyle~-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}(\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp,-}-\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}(\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}-\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\rho^{-}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\cdot(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}\cdot(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{\sharp}(q^{-})-\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\sharp,-}\right)\cdot(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}(\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q^{-})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\rho^{-}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*,B}+\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*,R}+\text{ VS}_{0212}^{*,R},

(3.104)

where the first term is again controlled by integrating by parts in
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
and using symmetry

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\rho^{-}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=\frac{\varepsilon^{16}}{2}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left(\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\rho^{-}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket)\right)\left|D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))E_{8}^{\kappa}(t).

(3.105)

Next we wish to combine
\text{ VS}^{*,B}_{0211}
with
\text{ VS}^{*,B}_{0111}:=\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}(\frac{1}{2}|b^{-}|^{2})\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
to get a cancellation structure. In
\text{ VS}^{*,B}_{0111}
, we invoke the evolution equation
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}b_{j}^{-}=(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})v^{-}-b^{-}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})
to get

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{0111}^{*,B}\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{j}b_{j}^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{j}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}\left(b_{j}^{-}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{j}\,\left[b_{j},(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}\right](\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0111}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{j}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}_{j}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{j}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}((b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})v_{j}^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{j}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}(b_{j}^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0111},

(3.106)

where the first term on the right side is again controlled by integrating by parts in
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
and using symmetry, and the third term on the right side is controlled directly after inserting the expression of
\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}(b)
. We denote

\text{ VS}_{0112}^{*,B}:=\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{j}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}((b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})v_{j}^{-})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

to be the second term on the right side above. Inserting the good unknown
\mathbf{V}^{\sharp,-}
, the term
\text{ VS}_{0112}^{*,B}
is equal to

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left((b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp,-}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left([\partial_{t}^{7},b_{j}^{-}]\partial^{\varphi}_{j}v_{i}^{-}+b_{j}^{-}\mathfrak{C}_{j}^{\sharp}(v_{i}^{-})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0112}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\left((\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp,-}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,\left[(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}),(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\right]\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0112}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{0113}^{*,B}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0113}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0112}.

(3.107)

Now we can integrate by parts
(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})
and then
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
in
\text{ VS}_{0113}^{*,B}
in order to produce the cancellation with
\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*,B}
. Under time integral,
\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}_{0113}^{*,B}\,\mathrm{d}\tau
is equal to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\underbrace{\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{=-\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*,B}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\bigg|^{t}_{0}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}[(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}),D_{t}^{\varphi,-}]\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\sharp,-}_{i}\,[D_{t}^{\varphi,-},(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})]\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\sharp,-}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*,B}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0211}+\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0212}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0213}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.108)

Note that
[D_{t}^{\varphi,-},(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})]=-(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{-})(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})f
and when we commute
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
with either
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
or
(b^{-}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}),
, no normal derivative will be generated because the weight functions in front of
\partial_{3}
(namely,
b^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}
and
(v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)
) are still vanishing on the interface
\Sigma
after taking
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
. Therefore, the commutators above are all controllable in
\|\cdot\|_{8,*,-}
norm and no loss of Mach number occurs. The following remainder terms are controlled directly

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{022}^{*,R}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0211}+\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}_{021}^{*,R}+\text{ VS}_{023}^{*,R}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0111}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0112}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0212}+\text{ VS}^{*,BR}_{0213}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.109)

In the terms
\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*,R}+\text{ VS}_{0212}^{*,R}
, we can integrate
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
by parts to get to get the desired control thanks to the
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted boundary regularity of
\psi

\displaystyle\text{ VS}_{0211}^{*,R}+\text{ VS}_{0212}^{*,R}\lesssim_{\sigma^{-1}}\left(|\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi|_{2}+|\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{8}\psi|_{1}\right)\|\varepsilon^{8}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}v\|_{0,-}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)).

(3.110)

Thus, the control of
\text{ VS}_{02}^{*}
term is concluded by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}_{02}^{*}+\text{ VS}_{0111}^{*,B}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.111)

Finally, combining (
3.92
), (
3.93
), (
3.101
) and (
3.111
), we get the estimate of
\text{ VS}^{*}
term

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.112)

Step 3: Control of RT term.

In step 3, we control the terms
\text{ RT}^{*}
and
\text{ RT}^{*,\pm}
defined in (
3.65
)-(
3.66
), The latter one can be directly controlled by using symmetry

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{*,\pm}=\mp\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\partial_{3}q^{\pm}\,v^{\pm}))\left|\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq\,\sigma^{-1}E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)E_{8}^{\kappa}(t).

(3.113)

The term
\text{ RT}^{*}=-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}
cannot be controlled in the same way as in the estimates of spatial derivatives because we do not have
L^{2}(\Sigma)
-control for
\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi
without
\kappa
-weight nor can we integrate by parts
\partial_{t}^{1/2}
. To overcome this difficulty, we need to invoke the kinematic boundary condition to reduce the number of time derivatives. We have

\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi=\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot N+[\partial_{t}^{7},v^{-},N],~~\partial_{t}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi=\partial_{t}^{8}v^{-}\cdot N+8\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}N+\text{ lower order terms}.

Plugging it to
\text{ RT}^{*}
, we find

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot N\,\partial_{t}^{8}v^{-}\cdot N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-8\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot N\,\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=:\text{ RT}^{*}_{1}+\text{ RT}^{*}_{2}.

(3.114)

The term
\text{ RT}^{*}_{2}
can be controlled by using Gauss-Green formula

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{*}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~-8\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{N})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-8\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket(\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{N})\,\mathrm{d}x,

(3.115)

where
\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket
is defined via Sobolev extension. The first term above is directly controlled after invoking the reduction
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\partial_{t}^{7}(\varepsilon^{2}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}p^{-}+\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{-})
. For the second term, it suffices to integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts under time integral

\displaystyle-8\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket(\partial_{t}^{7}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{N})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-8\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket(\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{N})\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg|^{t}_{0}+8\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket(\partial_{t}^{8}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v^{-}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{N})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\|\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{6}v^{-}\|_{0,-}^{2}+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.116)

Using the same trick as above, the term
\text{ RT}^{*}_{1}
is directly controlled by repeated invoking
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\partial_{t}^{7}(\varepsilon^{2}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}p^{-}+\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{-})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ RT}^{*}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\left((\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial_{t}^{8}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})+(\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{8}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t},~L}{===}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\left((\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial_{t}^{8}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})-\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{-}}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket(\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\bigg|^{t}_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.117)

Hence, we conclude the estimate of
\text{ RT}^{*}
by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{ RT}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.118)

Step 4: The cancellation structure between
ZB^{*}
and
Z^{*}
.

Now we control the term
ZB^{*,\pm}+Z^{*,\pm}
. Note that we cannot integrate by parts
\overline{\partial}{}^{1/2}
due to the lack of spatial derivatives. First,
ZB^{*,\pm}
can be written as

\displaystyle ZB^{*,\pm}=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot N)\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\pm\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\partial_{3}q^{\pm}(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot N)\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}\,\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},N_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~ZB^{*,R,\pm}_{1}+ZB^{*,R,\pm}_{2}+ZB^{*,\pm}_{0}.

(3.119)

The second term on the right side can be directly controlled. We have

\displaystyle ZB^{*,R,\pm}_{2}\leq\left|D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\left|\partial_{3}q^{\pm}\,(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot N)\right|_{L^{\infty}}\leq P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))E_{8}^{\kappa}(t).

(3.120)

For the first term, using again
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}\partial_{t}^{7}\psi=\partial_{t}^{7}v\cdot N+
lower order terms, we can convert it to an interior integral.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}ZB^{*,R,\pm}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N})\left(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}\,\partial_{t}^{7}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}+D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}\,\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.121)

\displaystyle\overset{D_{t}^{\varphi,-}}{==}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N})\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}\,\partial_{t}^{7}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\bigg|^{t}_{0}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N})\left([\partial^{\varphi}_{3},D_{t}^{\varphi,-}]\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}\right)\,\partial_{t}^{7}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N})\left(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}\,D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}+D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{\pm}\,\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\text{l.o.t}

Now we can invoke the reduction for
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q
and
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N}
to convert
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}
to a tangential derivative

\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\partial_{t}^{7}(\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b),~~\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v\cdot\mathbf{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\partial_{t}^{7}(\mathcal{F}_{p}D_{t}^{\varphi}p+\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}).

Note that the second equation above produces an extra
\mathcal{F}_{p}=O(\varepsilon^{2})
weight, so there is no loss of Mach number when
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q
appears. When
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q
is multiplied by
\partial_{t}^{7}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}
, we can further integrate by parts in
\partial_{t}
and then in
\overline{\nabla}\cdot
to move one time derivative to
v
. Hence,
ZB^{*,R,\pm}_{1}
is controlled in
\|\cdot\|_{8,*}
norm without loss of
\varepsilon
-weights

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}ZB^{*,R,\pm}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim~\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.122)

Next we will see again the cancellation structure in
ZB_{0}^{*,\pm}+Z^{*,\pm}
. From (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{*}(v_{i})=

\displaystyle~\partial^{\varphi}_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi+\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}v_{i}\right]+\partial_{3}v_{i}\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+(\partial_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N})\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{6},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\varphi+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\mathbf{N}\cdot[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\partial_{3}]v+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N})[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\partial_{3}]\varphi,

where we have further analysis on the second term and the fifth term

\displaystyle\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}v_{i}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\partial_{3}v_{i}\right]-\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{6}v\cdot\mathbf{N},

(3.123)

\displaystyle(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\mathbf{N}\cdot[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\partial_{3}]v=

\displaystyle~\left(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\right)\partial_{t}^{7}v\cdot\mathbf{N}+\partial_{3}\left((\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\right)\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial_{t}^{7}v\cdot\mathbf{N}.

(3.124)

Thus, we find that, apart from the term
(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\partial_{3}v_{i}\right]
, all the other terms in
\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{*}(v_{i})
include either a tangential derivative falling on the leading order term or the term
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N}
(possibly with some derivatives) such that
\mathcal{F}_{p}D_{t}^{\varphi}p
and
\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}
are produced by invoking the continuity equation. Thus, when
\mathbf{Q}^{*}
is multiplied with these terms, its contribution in
Z^{*,\pm}
can be directly controlled without any loss of weights of Mach number.

It now remains to control
ZB^{*,\pm}_{0}+Z^{*,\pm}
with
Z_{0}^{*,\pm}:=\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\partial_{3}v_{i}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
. Using
\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x
and Gauss-Green formula, we have

\displaystyle ZB^{*,\pm}_{0}+Z_{0}^{*,\pm}=\mp\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}\,\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},N_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{16}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}\left[D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7},\mathbf{N}_{i},\partial_{3}v_{i}^{\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{k=1}^{6}\varepsilon^{16}\binom{7}{k}\left(\pm\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{j}\partial_{t}^{k}v_{i}^{\pm}\,(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{1-j}\partial_{t}^{6-k}N_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{j}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{3}v_{i}^{\pm}\,(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{1-j}\partial_{t}^{6-k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{k=1}^{6}\varepsilon^{16}\binom{7}{k}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{*,\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{j}\partial_{t}^{k}v_{i}^{\pm}\,(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{1-j}\partial_{t}^{6-k}\mathbf{N}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

(3.125)

Recall that
\mathbf{Q}^{*}=D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}q^{-}-D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{7}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q
, we can integrate by parts this
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}
under time integral and invoke the momentum equation to reduce
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{*}
to
-\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}\mathbf{V}^{*}+(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{*}+
lower order terms. Note that
[\partial^{\varphi}_{3},D_{t}^{\varphi,-}]\partial_{t}^{7}q=\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v_{j}^{-}\partial^{\varphi}_{j}q=(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{7}q+(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N})\partial_{t}^{7}\partial_{3}q
, so one can still convert the normal derivative
\partial q
to a tangential derivative of
v,b
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}ZB^{*,\pm}_{0}+Z_{0}^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{k=1}^{6}\varepsilon^{16}\binom{7}{k}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp,\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\left((D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{j}\partial_{t}^{k}v_{i}^{\pm}\cdot,(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{1-j}\partial_{t}^{6-k}\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{16}\binom{7}{k}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp,\pm}\left((D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{j}\partial_{t}^{k}v_{i}^{\pm}\cdot,(D_{t}^{\varphi,-})^{1-j}\partial_{t}^{6-k}\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\bigg|^{t}_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\|\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\mathbf{Q}^{\sharp,\pm}\|_{0}^{2}+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~~\forall\delta\in(0,1).

(3.126)

Combining this with the control of remainder terms and commutators, we can easily obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}ZB^{*,\pm}+Z^{*,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\delta E_{8}^{\kappa}(0)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~~\forall\delta\in(0,1).

(3.127)

3.5
Tangential estimates: general cases and summary

Let
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\gamma_{4}}\partial_{t}^{\gamma_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}
be a tangential derivative with length of the multi-index
\langle\gamma\rangle:=\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+2\times 0+\gamma_{4}
. Section
3.3.1
-Section
3.4
are devoted to the control of full spatial derivatives (
\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}=\langle\gamma\rangle
) and full time derivatives (
\gamma_{0}=\langle\gamma\rangle
). Now we analyze how to handle the general case.

Space-time mixed derivatives:
\gamma_{0}>0
and
\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}>0

Let us temporarily assume
\gamma_{4}=0
. In this case, the tangential derivatives that we need to consider have the form
\overline{\partial}{}^{4-l-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=2l,~\alpha_{4}=0
and weights of Mach number
\varepsilon^{2l}
. That is, we need to consider
\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k-\alpha_{0}}
-estimates. Following the previous paper
[
38
]
by Luo and the author, the control of space-time mixed tangential derivatives (
0<k+\alpha_{0}<4+l
) is the same as the control of purely spatial tangential derivatives. In particular, compared with the one-phase fluid problem
[
38
]
, we only need one spatial derivative to do integration by parts in order for the control of the extra problematic term

\text{ VS}:=\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{k+\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k-\alpha_{0}}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{k+\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k-\alpha_{0}}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

in which we need to integrate by parts
\overline{\partial}{}^{1/2}
and seek for the control of
\varepsilon^{2l}\left|\partial_{t}^{k+\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k-\alpha_{0}}\right|_{1.5}
. Mimicing the proof of Lemma
3.5
, we can show that (replacing
k+\alpha_{0}
by
k
)

Lemma 3.7

(Elliptic estimate for the time derivatives of the free interface)
.

Fix
l\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
. For
0<k<4+l
, we have the following uniform-in-
(\varepsilon,\kappa)
inequality, in which the first term on the right side disappears when
\kappa=0
.

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{5.5+l-k}\leq

\displaystyle~\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi(0)\right|_{5.5+l-k}+\sigma^{-1}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\right|_{3.5+l-k}

\displaystyle+P\left(\sigma^{-1},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{L^{\infty}},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(t)\right)\left(\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{4.5+l-k}+\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k-1}\psi\right|_{5.5+l-k}\right).

Weighted normal derivatives:
\gamma_{4}>0

In the most general case,
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
may contain weighted normal derivative
\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3}
, so we have to analyze the commutator involving
[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]
in
\mathfrak{C}(f)
and
\mathfrak{D}(f)
defined in (
3.2
)-(
3.2
). The problematic thing is that
\partial_{3}
may fall on
\omega(x_{3})
which converts a “tangential” derivative
\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3}
(a first-order derivative) to a normal derivative
\partial_{3}
(considered to be second-order under the setting of anisotropic Sobolev spaces). Such terms in
\mathfrak{D}(f)
are

(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f+(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\frac{\partial_{3}f}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\varphi.

They can be directly controlled because an extra weight
(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)
, which vanishes on
\Sigma
, is automatically generated to compensate the possible loss of weight function. As for
\mathfrak{C}(f)
, we notice that the terms involving
[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]
can be written to be

\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f-\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\varphi,~~f=q\text{ or }v_{i}.

The second term above is easy to control because
\varphi(t,x)=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\psi(t,x^{\prime})
implies the
C^{\infty}
-regularity of
\varphi
in
x_{3}
direction. For the first term, it may generate a term
\mathcal{T}^{\beta}\partial_{3}f\mathbf{N}_{i}
with
\beta_{i}=\gamma_{i}(i=0,1,2),~\beta_{4}=\gamma_{4}-1
, whose
L^{2}(\Omega)
norm may be not directly bounded. Luckily, for
f=q
or
v_{i}
, we can again invoke the momentum equation or the continuity equation to reduce
-\partial^{\varphi}_{3}q
and
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v\cdot\mathbf{N}
to tangential derivatives
\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b
and
-\mathcal{F}_{p}D_{t}^{\varphi}p-\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}
respectively. Therefore, there is no extra loss of derivative in the commutators
\mathfrak{C}(f)
and
\mathfrak{D}(f)
when
\gamma_{4}>0
.

Summary of tangential estimates

Finally, we need to recover the estimates of
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(v,b,S,\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}}p)
from the
L^{2}
-estimates of their Alinhac good unknowns. By definition, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}f^{\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{F}^{\gamma,\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\|\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}^{2},

in which
\left\|\mathbf{F}^{\gamma,\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}
and
\left|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\right|_{0}
have been controlled by
\delta E^{\kappa}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
. When
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
contains at least one spatial derivative, we can use
-\mathcal{T}q\sim D_{t}^{\varphi}v+(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b
to get the control of
\mathcal{T}q
instead of
\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}}\mathcal{T}q
. For the full time derivatives, we use
D_{t}^{\varphi,-}=\partial_{t}+(\bar{v}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla})+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v^{-}\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}
to convert the
\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}
-estimate to
\varepsilon^{2l}D_{t}^{\varphi,-}\partial_{t}^{3+l}
-estimate,
\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3+l}
-estimate and
\varepsilon^{2l}(\omega\partial_{3})\partial_{t}^{3+l}
-estimate, in the second part of which the norm
|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\psi(0)|_{2.5}
is needed to control the VS term. Also, since
\omega(x_{3})=0
on the interface,
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
can be expressed as
\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\partial_{t}^{k}
for
0\leq k\leq 4+l,~0\leq l\leq 4
. Hence, we conclude the tangential estimates by the following inequalities

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle=2l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq 4-l\\
k+\alpha_{0}<4+l\end{subarray}}\left\|\left(\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k-l}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm},p^{\pm})\right)\right\|^{2}_{0,\pm}+\sum_{k=0}^{4+l}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|^{2}_{5+l-k}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{6+l-k}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi(\tau)\right|_{5+l-k}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\sum_{k=0}^{3+l}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi(0)\right|_{5.5+l-k}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{l}P\left(\sigma^{-1},E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)

\displaystyle+P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(t)\right)\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.128)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{k=0}^{4-l}\left\|\left(\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm},(\mathcal{F}_{p})^{\frac{1}{2}}p^{\pm})\right)\right\|^{2}_{4-k-l,\pm}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}\psi\right|^{2}_{1}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}\psi\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{5+l}\psi(\tau)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\psi(0)\right|_{2.5}^{2}+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(t)\right)\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(3.129)

These are exactly the desired uniform-in-
(\kappa,\varepsilon)
energy estimates in Proposition
3.3
.

3.6
Div-Curl analysis and reduction of pressure

The tangential derivatives of the variables
(v,b,p)
are analyzed in Section
3.3
-Section
3.5
. Here we show the reduction of normal derivatives of pressure and the analysis for the divergence and vorticity. We use the div-curl decomposition (cf. Lemma
B.1
) such that the normal derivatives of
(v,b)
are controlled via their divergence and curl parts. For
0\leq l\leq 3,~0\leq k\leq 3-l,~\langle\alpha\rangle=2l,~\alpha_{3}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{4-k-l,\pm}^{2}\leq C\bigg(

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k-l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\bigg)

(3.130)

with

C=C\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}\sum_{k=0}^{3+j}|\varepsilon^{2j}\partial_{t}^{j}\psi|_{4+l-j}^{2},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)>0

a positive continuious function linear in
|\varepsilon^{2j}\partial_{t}^{j}\psi|_{4+l-j}^{2}
.
The conclusion for the div-curl analysis is

Proposition 3.8
.

Fix
l\in\{0,1,2,3\}
. For any
0\leq k\leq l-1
, any multi-index
\alpha
satisfying
\langle\alpha\rangle=2l
and any constant
\delta\in(0,1)
, we can prove the following estimates for the curl part

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}

(3.131)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)+E_{4+l+1}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

and for the divergence part

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}

(3.132)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

3.6.1
Reduction of pressure and divergence

Let us start with
l=0
. The spatial derivative of
q
is controlled by invoking the momentum equation:

\displaystyle-\partial_{3}q=

\displaystyle~(\partial_{3}\varphi)\left(\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{3}-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b_{3}\right);

(3.133)

\displaystyle-\overline{\partial}{}_{i}q=

\displaystyle-(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\varphi\,\partial_{3}q+\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{i}-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b_{i},~~i=1,2.

(3.134)

Let
\mathcal{T}
be
\partial_{t}
or
\overline{\partial}{}
or
\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{3}q\|_{3-k}\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|\partial_{t}^{k}(\rho\mathcal{T}v_{3})\|_{3-k}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b\mathcal{T}b_{3})\|_{3-k}

(3.135)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}q\|_{3-k}\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|\partial_{t}^{k}(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\varphi\partial_{3}q)\|_{3-k}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}(\rho\mathcal{T}v_{i})\|_{3-k}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b\mathcal{T}b_{i})\|_{3-k},

(3.136)

in which the leading order terms are
\|\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}(v,b)\|_{3-k}
and
|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi|_{4-k}
. This shows that we can convert the control of spatial derivative of
q
to
tangential estimates
of
v
and
b
.

Next we turn to the div-curl analysis for
v,b
. Let us first analyze
E_{4}(t)
. For
0\leq k\leq 3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k}^{2}\leq C(|\psi|_{4-k},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}^{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{0}^{2}\right),

(3.137)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}b\|_{4-k}^{2}\leq C(|\psi|_{4-k},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\partial_{t}^{k}b\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}b\|_{3-k}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}b\|_{3-k}^{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}\partial_{t}^{k}b\|_{0}^{2}\right).

(3.138)

For the divergence, we can directly invoke the continuity equation to convert
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v
to time derivative of
p
together with square weights of Mach number. When
k=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v\|_{3}^{2}=\|\mathcal{F}_{p}D_{t}^{\varphi}p\|_{3}^{2},

(3.139)

which is further reduced to the tangential derivatives of
v
and
b
by using the above reduction of
q
. Note that the magnetic tension term
\frac{1}{2}|b|^{2}
in the total pressure
q
does not involve extra normal derivatives thanks to
\mathcal{T}(\frac{1}{2}|b|^{2})=b\cdot\mathcal{T}b
. Taking
\partial_{t}
in the continuity equation and omitting lower order terms, we have

\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}v\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\mathcal{F}_{p}\partial_{t}^{k}D_{t}^{\varphi}p+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi\cdot\partial_{3}v,

which gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}v^{\pm}\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left(\left\|\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}+\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{4-k}^{2}\right)+\text{ lower order terms}.

(3.140)

Again, this can be reduced to tangential derivatives of
v,b
until there is no spatial derivative falling on
p
.
As for the divergence of magnetic fields, we can invoke the div-free constraint to convert it to lower order terms. Namely, using
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b=0
, we have

\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}b\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\underbrace{\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b)}_{=0}+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi\cdot\partial_{3}b

and thus

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}b^{\pm}\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{4-k}^{2}+\text{ lower order terms}.

(3.141)

The term
\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{4-k}^{2}
has been controlled in tangential estimates of
E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)
. Combining the result of tangential estimates in Proposition
3.3
, the control of divergence of time derivatives is concluded by

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left\|\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}+C(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{4-k}^{2}

(3.142)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left\|\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}+\delta E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0))+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where the term involving
p^{\pm}
can be further reduced to
\mathcal{T}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})
when
3-k>0
so that one can further apply the div-curl analysis to it.

3.6.2
Vorticity analysis for
E_{4}

Taking
\nabla^{\varphi}\times
in the momentum equation of
v
and the evolution equation of
b
, we get the evolution equation for the vorticity
\nabla^{\varphi}\times v
and the current density
\nabla^{\varphi}\times b

\displaystyle\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v)-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b)=

\displaystyle~-(\nabla^{\varphi}\rho)\times(D_{t}^{\varphi}v)-\rho(\nabla^{\varphi}v_{j})\times(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}v)+(\nabla^{\varphi}b_{j})\times(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}b),

(3.143)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b)-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v)-b\times\nabla^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)=

\displaystyle-(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b)(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)-(\nabla^{\varphi}v_{j})\times(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}b)+(\nabla^{\varphi}b_{j})\times(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}v),

(3.144)

and taking
\partial^{3}
gives

\displaystyle\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times v)-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b)=

\displaystyle~RK_{v},

(3.145)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b)-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times v)-b\times\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)=

\displaystyle~RK_{b},

(3.146)

where

\displaystyle RK_{v}:=

\displaystyle-[\partial^{3},\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}](\nabla^{\varphi}\times v)+[\partial^{3},(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})](\nabla^{\varphi}\times b)+\partial^{3}(\text{right side of }\eqref{curlveq}),

\displaystyle RK_{b}:=

\displaystyle-[\partial^{3},D_{t}^{\varphi}](\nabla^{\varphi}\times b)+[\partial^{3},(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})](\nabla^{\varphi}\times v)+\partial^{3}(\text{right side of }\eqref{curlbeq}).

Direct computation shows that the highest-order terms in
RK_{v},~RK_{b}
only have 4 spatial derivatives and do not contain time derivative of
q
. Therefore, we can prove the
H^{3}
-control of the voriticity and current density by standard energy estimates.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\cdot(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.147)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}RK_{v}^{\pm}\cdot(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{=:L_{1}^{\pm}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})}{===}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm}))\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{=:K_{1}^{\pm}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot RK_{b}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{L_{2}^{\pm}}+L_{1}^{\pm},

where
L_{1}^{\pm},~L_{2}^{\pm}
are directly controlled

\displaystyle L_{1}^{\pm}+L_{2}^{\pm}\leq P(\|(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\|_{4,\pm},|\psi|_{4}).

(3.148)

It remains to analyze the term
K_{1}^{\pm}
in which there is a key observation for the energy structure of compressible MHD system. We invoke the continuity equation
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm}=\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}
and commute
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}
with
\nabla^{\varphi}
to get

\nabla^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})=-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\nabla^{\varphi}p^{\pm}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}v_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}p^{\pm}).

Next, we rewrite the momentum equation to be
\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}+b^{\pm}\times(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})=-\nabla^{\varphi}p^{\pm}
and plug it into the highest-order term
-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\nabla^{\varphi}p^{\pm}
to get

\displaystyle-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\nabla^{\varphi}p^{\pm}=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm})+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(b^{\pm}\times(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(b^{\pm}\times(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm}))+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\rho^{\pm})(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}).

Thus, the term
K_{1}^{\pm}
becomes

\displaystyle K_{1}^{\pm}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot\left(b^{\pm}\times(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.149)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right)\cdot D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot RK_{p}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t},

where

\displaystyle RK_{p}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\partial^{3}\left((\nabla^{\varphi}v_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}p^{\pm})+(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\rho^{\pm})(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm})\right)+[\partial^{3},\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}](D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}[\partial^{3},D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}](b^{\pm}\times(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm}))+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\left([\partial^{3},b^{\pm}\times](\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right)

consists of
\leq 4
derivatives in each term and its contribution can be directly controlled

\displaystyle L_{3}^{\pm}:=\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot RK_{p}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\leq P(\|b^{\pm},v^{\pm},\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}p^{\pm}\|_{4,\pm},\|\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},p^{\pm})\|_{3,\pm})

(3.150)

Note that the second term on the right side of
K_{1}^{\pm}
is obtained by using the vector identity
\mathbf{a}\cdot(\mathbf{b}\times\mathbf{c})=-\mathbf{c}\cdot(\mathbf{b}\times\mathbf{a})
:
