(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot\left(b^{\pm}\times D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right)\right)=-D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right)\cdot\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.151)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right)\cdot D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\left(b^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\cdot\left(b^{\pm}\times(\mathcal{F}_{p}\rho^{\pm}\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{K^{\pm}}+L_{1}^{\pm}+L_{2}^{\pm}+L_{3}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|b^{\pm}\times\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\left(\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|b^{\pm}\times\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+K^{\pm}+L_{1}^{\pm}+L_{2}^{\pm}+L_{3}^{\pm},

which further gives the control of vorticity and current density simultaneously

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|b^{\pm}\times\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.152)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))+P(|\psi|_{4})\|b^{\pm}\|_{4,\pm}\|b^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}\left\|\varepsilon^{2}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))+E_{5}^{\kappa}(t).

Hence, the vorticity analysis for compressible ideal MHD cannot be closed in standard Sobolev space because of the term
\varepsilon^{2}\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}
in
K^{\pm}
. Instead, the appearance of this term indicates us to
trade one normal derivative (in the curl operator) for two tangential derivatives
(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}
together with square weights of Mach number
\varepsilon^{2}
. Besides, the normal derivative part involving
\partial^{3}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})
contributes to the energy of current density thanks to the special structure of Lorentz force
-b^{\pm}\times(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})
. This is exactly the motivation for us to define the energy functional
E(t)
under the setting the anisotropic Sobolev spaces instead of standard Sobolev spaces.

Similarly, the curl estimates for the time derivatives (in
E_{4}(t)
) can be proven in the same way by replacing
\partial^{3}
with
\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}~(1\leq k\leq 3)
. We omit the details and list the conclusion

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\left|\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\left|\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|b^{\pm}\times\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.153)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))+\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))+E_{5}^{\kappa}(t).

Finally, we need to commute
\partial_{t}^{k}
with
\nabla^{\varphi}\times
when
k\geq 1
. We have

(\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v)_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v)_{i}+\epsilon_{ijl}(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{k}\varphi)(\partial_{3}v_{l}),

where
\epsilon_{ijl}
is the sign of permutation
(ijl)\in S_{3}
. This gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v^{\pm}\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left(\left\|\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}+\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{4-k}^{2}\right)+\text{ lower order terms},

(3.154)

where both leading order terms have been controlled in tangential estimates of
E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)
. The same result holds for
b^{\pm}
. Using the result of tangential estimates of
E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)
, we have: for any
k\in\{0,1,2,3\}
and any
\delta\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}b^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k,\pm}^{2}

(3.155)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))+E_{5}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau+P(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{4-k}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)+P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4}^{\kappa}(0))+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau))+E_{5}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

3.6.3
Further div-curl analysis for
E_{5}\sim E_{7}

The vorticity analysis for
E_{4}(t)
requires the control of
\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k}^{2}
for
0\leq k\leq 3
. When
0\leq k\leq 2
, there are still normal derivatives in this term. Thus, we shall do further div-curl analysis on
\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-k}^{2}
for
0\leq k\leq 2
. Let
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}=\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\alpha_{4}}
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=2
. The divergence part can be reduced in the same way as in Section
3.6.1
. We take
\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
in the continuity equation and omit the lower order terms to get

\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}D_{t}^{\varphi}p+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\varphi\cdot\partial_{3}v,

which gives

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\|_{2-k}^{2}\lesssim C(\|v\|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{2-k,\pm}^{2}+\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{2-k}^{2}\right)+\text{ lower order terms}.

(3.156)

Remark 3.3
.

The term generated when commuting
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with
\nabla^{\varphi}
is actually of lower order. One can check that (see also
[
65
, (3.24)-(3.25)]
)

[(\omega\partial_{3})^{m},\partial_{3}]f=\underbrace{(\omega\partial_{3})^{m}\partial_{3}f-\partial_{3}((\omega\partial_{3})^{m}f)}_{\text{both are (m+1)-th order terms}}=\sum_{k\leq m-1}c_{m,k}(\omega\partial_{3})^{k}\partial_{3}f=\sum_{k\leq m-1}d_{m,k}\partial_{3}(\omega\partial_{3})^{k}f

for some smooth functions
c_{m,k},d_{m,k}
depending on
m,k
and the derivatives (up to order
m
) of
\omega
, and the right side only contains
\leq m
-th order terms.

Similarly, using
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b=0
, we have

\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\underbrace{\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b)}_{=0}+(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\varphi\cdot\partial_{3}b

and thus

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}b^{\pm}\|_{2-k,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{2-k}^{2}+\text{ lower order terms}.

(3.157)

The control of divergence part in the analysis of
E_{5}^{\kappa}(t)
is concluded by the following energy inequality. For any
k\in\{0,1,2\}
, any multi-index
\alpha
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=2
and any
\delta\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{2-k,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{2-k,\pm}^{2}+C(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{3-k}^{2}

(3.158)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{2-k,\pm}^{2}+\delta E_{5}^{\kappa}(t)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0),E_{5}^{\kappa}(0))+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau),E_{5}^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where the term involving
\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}
can be further reduced to
\mathcal{T}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})
when
2-k>0
so that one can further apply the div-curl analysis to it.

As for the curl part, we can still mimic the proof in Section
3.6.2
to get the control of
\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times(v,b))\right\|_{2-k}
for
0\leq k\leq 2
and
\langle\alpha\rangle=2
with
\alpha_{3}=0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial^{2-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times v^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial^{2-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|\varepsilon^{2}b^{\pm}\times\partial^{2-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b^{\pm})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(3.159)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t),E_{5}^{\kappa}(t))+\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right\|_{2-k}^{2}\leq P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t),E_{5}^{\kappa}(t))+E_{6}^{\kappa}(t).

Then we commute
\partial^{2-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with
\nabla^{\varphi}\times
to get: for any
k\in\{0,1,2\}
, any multi-index
\alpha
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=2
and
\alpha_{3}=0
, and any
\delta\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{2-k,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b^{\pm}\right\|_{2-k,\pm}^{2}

(3.160)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(0),E_{5}^{\kappa}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau),E_{5}^{\kappa}(\tau))+E_{6}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau+P(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{2-k}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{5}^{\kappa}(t)+P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4}^{\kappa}(0),E_{5}^{\kappa}(0))+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau),E_{5}^{\kappa}(\tau))+E_{6}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where we use the result of tangential estimates to control
\left|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{2-k}
.
When
k\leq 1
in the above energy estimate, we shall continue to apply the div-curl analysis to
\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right\|_{2-k}^{2}
.

For
E_{6}^{\kappa}
and
E_{7}^{\kappa}
, we have analogous div-curl inequalities. For
l=2,3
, we continue to analyze the divergence and the curl according to (
3.130
). Similarly as above, we have the following estimates for any
k\in\{0,1\}
, any multi-index
\alpha
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=4,~\alpha_{3}=0
and any
\delta\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{1-k,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\nabla^{\varphi}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b^{\pm}\right\|_{1-k,\pm}^{2}

(3.161)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{6}^{\kappa}(t)+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{2}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{2}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)+E_{7}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where this
E_{7}^{\kappa}
term is contributed by
\left\|\varepsilon^{6}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right\|_{1-k,\pm}
. When
k=0
in this term, we again apply the div-curl analysis to it in order to eliminate all normal derivatives falling on
v,b
. For any multi-index
\alpha
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=6
and
\alpha_{3}=0
and any
\delta\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{6}\nabla^{\varphi}\times\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{6}\nabla^{\varphi}\times\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b^{\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}

(3.162)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{7}^{\kappa}(t)+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{3}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{3}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)+E_{8}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

The control of divergence part for
E_{6}^{\kappa}(t),~E_{7}^{\kappa}(t)
also follows the same way as
E_{4}^{\kappa}(t),E_{5}^{\kappa}(t)
. For any
k\in\{0,1\}
, any multi-index
\alpha
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=4,~\alpha_{3}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{4}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{1-k,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left\|\varepsilon^{6}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{1-k,\pm}^{2}+C(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{2-k}^{2}

(3.163)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{6}^{\kappa}(t)+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{2}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{2}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where the term
\left\|\varepsilon^{6}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{1-k,\pm}^{2}
does not appear because it can be converted to full tangential derivatives (part of
E_{6}^{\kappa}(t)
) which has been controlled in Section
3.3
-Section
3.5
. For any multi-index
\alpha
with
\langle\alpha\rangle=6,~\alpha_{3}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{6}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\|v^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left\|\varepsilon^{8}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+C(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})})\left|\varepsilon^{6}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{1}^{2}

(3.164)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta E_{7}^{\kappa}(t)+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{3}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(E_{4}^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{l=0}^{3}E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

where the term
\left\|\varepsilon^{8}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{T}p^{\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}
does not appear because it has been included in tangential estimates for
E_{7}^{\kappa}(t)
.

3.6.4
Modifications for the 2D case

In the case of 2D, the equations of vorticity
\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot v
and current density
\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b
are

\displaystyle\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot v)-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b)=

\displaystyle-(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\rho)\cdot(D_{t}^{\varphi}v)-\rho(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}v_{j})\cdot(\nabla^{\varphi}_{j}v)+(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}b_{j})\cdot(\nabla^{\varphi}_{j}b),

(3.165)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b)-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot v)-b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)=

\displaystyle-(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b)(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)-(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}v_{j})\cdot(\nabla^{\varphi}_{j}b)+(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}b_{j})\cdot(\nabla^{\varphi}_{j}v),

(3.166)

which has the same structure as (
3.143
)-(
3.144
). Thus, we expect to adopt the strategy in Section
3.6
to prove the div-curl estimates. The only slight difference is the structure of Lorentz force. Let us take the
\partial^{3}
-estimate of
\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot(v,b)
for an example. In this case, the problematic term (in the analogue of
K_{1}^{\pm}
in (
3.147
)) becomes

{K_{1}^{\pm}}^{\prime}=\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm})\,\left(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

Again, we invoke the continuity equation, commute
\nabla^{\varphi}
with
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}
to get

\displaystyle b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{2}(b_{1}^{\pm}\partial^{3}\partial^{\varphi}_{2}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}-b_{2}^{\pm}\partial^{3}\partial^{\varphi}_{1}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\varepsilon^{2}(b_{1}^{\pm}\partial^{3}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\partial^{\varphi}_{2}p^{\pm})-b_{2}^{\pm}\partial^{3}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\partial^{\varphi}_{1}p^{\pm})).

Then we plug the momentum equation

\displaystyle-\partial^{\varphi}_{1}p=\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{1}-b_{1}\partial^{\varphi}_{1}b_{1}-b_{2}\partial^{\varphi}_{2}b_{1}+b_{1}\partial^{\varphi}_{1}b_{1}+b_{2}\partial^{\varphi}_{1}b_{2}=\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{1}+b_{2}(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b)

\displaystyle-\partial^{\varphi}_{2}p=\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{2}-b_{1}\partial^{\varphi}_{1}b_{2}-b_{2}\partial^{\varphi}_{2}b_{2}+b_{1}\partial^{\varphi}_{2}b_{1}+b_{2}\partial^{\varphi}_{2}b_{2}=\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v_{2}-b_{1}(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b)

to get

\displaystyle b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}(b^{\pm,\perp}\cdot\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm})-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left((b_{1}^{\pm})^{2}+(b_{2}^{\pm})^{2}\right)\partial^{3}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b),~~b^{\perp}:=(-b_{2},b_{1}).

Thus, the term
{K_{1}^{\pm}}^{\prime}
can be controlled in a similar manner as in Section
3.6

\displaystyle{K_{1}^{\pm}}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm})\,\left(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}b^{\pm,\perp}\cdot\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}|b^{\pm}|^{2}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm})\,(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm})\,\left(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}b^{\pm,\perp}\cdot\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}v^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|b^{\pm}\right|^{2}\left|\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|b^{\pm}\right|^{2}\left|\partial^{3}\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

Hence, the curl estimate (
3.152
) should be modified to be

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot v^{\pm})\right|^{2}+(1+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}|b^{\pm}|^{2})\left|\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot b^{\pm})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\lesssim P(\widetilde{E}_{4}(t))+\widetilde{E}_{5}(t).

(3.167)

3.7
Uniform estimates for the nonlinear approximate system

3.7.1
Control of the entropy

It remains to control the full (anisotropic) Sobolev norms of the entropy functions
S^{\pm}
. This can be easily proven thanks to
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}S^{\pm}=0
. In the control of
E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)
for fixed
0\leq l\leq 4
, we need to take the derivative
\partial_{*}^{\alpha}:=\partial^{4-l-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\partial_{3}^{\gamma_{3}^{\prime}}(\omega\partial_{3})^{\gamma_{4}}\partial_{t}^{k+\gamma_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{2}^{\prime}}
with
\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{4}=2l
,
\gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\gamma_{2}^{\prime}+\gamma_{3}^{\prime}=4-k-l
and
0\leq k\leq 4-l
and also multiply the weight
\varepsilon^{2l}
. Then we can introduce the Alinhac good unknown
\mathbf{S}^{\partial}
with respect to this general derivative
\partial_{*}^{\alpha}
by

\mathbf{S}^{\partial,\pm}:=\partial_{*}^{\alpha}S^{\pm}-\partial_{*}^{\alpha}\varphi\partial^{\varphi}_{3}S^{\pm},

which satisfies the evolution equation
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{S}^{\partial,\pm}=\mathfrak{D}^{\partial}(S^{\pm})
in
\Omega^{\pm}
where
\mathfrak{D}^{\partial}(S^{\pm})
is defined by (
3.2
) after replacing
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
with
\partial_{*}^{\alpha}
. We will get

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}S^{\pm}\right\|_{4-k-l,\pm}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathbf{S}^{\partial,\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left|\partial_{*}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\|\partial_{3}S^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta E_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+E_{4}^{\kappa}(t)\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(3.168)

where we again invoke the estimate of
\left|\partial_{*}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{0}
that has been proven in Section
3.3
-Section
3.5
.

3.7.2
Uniform-in-
\kappa
estimates for the nonlinear approximate system

Summarizing Proposition
3.2
(
L^{2}
-energy conservation), Proposition
3.3
(tangential estimates), Proposition
3.8
(div-curl estimates) and (
3.168
) (entropy estimates), we conclude the estimates of the energy functional
E^{\kappa}(t)
for the nonlinear approximate system (
3.1
) by

E^{\kappa}(t)\lesssim\delta E^{\kappa}(t)+P(E^{\kappa}(0))+P(E^{\kappa}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},E^{\kappa}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~\forall\delta\in(0,1)

(3.169)

Thus, choosing
\delta
suitably small such that
\delta E^{\kappa}(t)
can be absorbed by the left side and then using Gronwall-type argument, we find that there exists a time
T_{\sigma}>0
that depends on
\sigma
and the initial data and is independent of
\kappa
and
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T_{\sigma}}E^{\kappa}(t)\leq C(\sigma^{-1})P(E^{\kappa}(0)),

(3.170)

which is exactly the conclusion of Proposition
3.1
.

4
Well-posedness of the nonlinear approximate system

We already prove the uniform-in-
\kappa
estimates for the nonlinear approximate problem (
3.1
). If we can prove the well-posedness of (
3.1
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
, then the uniform estimates allow us to take the limit
\kappa\to 0_{+}
and prove the local existence of system (
1.30
) for the compressible current-vortex sheets with surface tension. Since there is no loss of regularity in the estimate of
E^{\kappa}(t)
, we would like to use Picard iteration to construct the solution to (
3.1
) for each fixed
\kappa
.

4.1
Construction of the linearized problem and the iteration scheme

We start with
\psi^{[-1]}=\psi^{[0]}=0
and
(v^{[0],\pm},b^{[0],\pm},\rho^{[0],\pm},S^{[0],\pm})=(\vec{0},\vec{0},\underline{\rho}^{\pm},0)
for some constants
\underline{\rho}^{\pm}\geq\bar{\rho_{0}}
. Then for any
n\geq 0,n\in\mathbb{N}
, given
\{(v^{[k],\pm},b^{[k],\pm},\rho^{[k],\pm},S^{[k],\pm})\}_{k\leq n}
, we define
(v^{[n+1],\pm},b^{[n+1],\pm},q^{[n+1],\pm},S^{[n+1],\pm},\psi^{[n+1]})
by the following linear system with variable coefficients only depending on
(v^{[n],\pm},b^{[n],\pm},q^{[n],\pm},S^{[n],\pm},\psi^{[n]},\psi^{[n-1]})

\begin{cases}\rho^{{[n]},\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}v^{{[n+1]},\pm}-(\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm})b^{{[n+1]},\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}q^{{[n+1]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[n]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}q^{{[n+1]},\pm}-(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[n]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}b^{{[n+1]},\pm}\cdot\bm{b}^{[n],\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}\cdot v^{{[n+1]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}b^{{[n+1]},\pm}-(\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm})v^{{[n+1]},\pm}+\bm{b}^{[n],\pm}\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}\cdot v^{{[n+1]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}S^{{[n+1]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\left\llbracket q^{{[n+1]}}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi^{[n]})-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi^{{[n+1]}}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi^{{[n+1]}}&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\psi^{[n+1]}=v^{[n+1],\pm}\cdot N^{[n]}&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v_{3}^{[n+1],\pm}=0,~~&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma^{\pm},\\
(v^{{[n+1]},\pm},b^{{[n+1]},\pm},q^{{[n+1]},\pm},S^{{[n+1]},\pm},\psi^{\pm})|_{t=0}=(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa}),\end{cases}

(4.1)

where
\bm{b}_{i}^{[n],\pm}:=b_{i}^{[n],\pm}
for
i=1,2
and
\bm{b}_{3}^{[n],\pm}
is defined by

\displaystyle\bm{b}_{3}^{[n],\pm}:=b_{3}^{[n],\pm}+\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}\left(b_{1}^{[n],\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\psi^{[n]}+b_{2}^{[n],\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\psi^{[n]}-b_{3}^{[n],\pm}\right)\big|_{\Sigma}

(4.2)

where
\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}
is the lifting operator defined in Lemma
B.3
. The initial data
(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\rho_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa})
is the same as (
3.1
). The basic state
(v^{[n],\pm},\bm{b}^{[n],\pm},\rho^{[n],\pm},S^{[n],\pm},\psi^{[n]},\psi^{[n-1]})
satisfies

1.

(The hyperbolicity assumption)
\rho^{{[n]},\pm}>0
is determined by the equation of state (
1.18
) where
p^{{[n]},\pm}
is defined by
p^{{[n]},\pm}:=q^{{[n]},\pm}-\frac{1}{2}|b^{{[n]},\pm}|^{2}
. Then define
\mathcal{F}^{[n]}=\log\rho^{[n]},~~\mathcal{F}_{p}^{{[n]},\pm}:=\frac{\partial\mathcal{F}^{{[n]},\pm}}{\partial p}(p^{{[n+1]},\pm},S^{{[n+1]},\pm})>0.

2.

(Tangential magnetic fields)
\bm{b}^{[n],\pm}\cdot N^{{[n]}}=0\text{ on }\Sigma,
and
\bm{b}^{[n],\pm}_{3}=0
on
\Sigma^{\pm}
.

3.

(Linearized material derivatives and covariant derivatives)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}:=

\displaystyle~\partial_{t}+\bar{v}^{[n]}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[n]}}(v^{[n]}\cdot\mathbf{N}^{[n-1]}-\partial_{t}\varphi^{[n]})\partial_{3},

(4.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}:=

\displaystyle~\partial_{t}-\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi^{[n]}}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[n]}}\partial_{3},~\nabla_{a}^{\varphi^{[n]}}=\partial_{a}^{\varphi^{[n]}}:=\partial_{a}-\frac{\partial_{a}\varphi^{[n]}}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[n]}}\partial_{3},~~a=1,2,\quad\nabla_{a}^{\varphi^{[n]}}=\partial_{3}^{\varphi^{[n]}}:=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[n]}}\partial_{3}

(4.4)

where
N^{[n]}:=(-\partial_{1}\psi^{[n]},-\partial_{2}\psi^{[n]},1)^{\top}
and
\mathbf{N}^{[n]}
is the extension of
N^{[n]}
with
\varphi^{{[n]}}=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\psi^{{[n]}}(t,x^{\prime})
.

After solving the linear problem (
4.1
), we define
p^{{[n+1]},\pm}=q^{{[n+1]},\pm}-\frac{1}{2}|b^{{[n+1]},\pm}|^{2}
and use the equation of state
p^{{[n+1]}}=p^{{[n+1]}}(\rho^{[n+1]},S^{[n+1]})
to determine the density
\rho^{[n+1]}>0
. We shall also define the “modified magnteic fields”
\bm{b}^{[n+1],\pm}
as follows in order to guarantee
\bm{b}^{[n+1],\pm}\cdot N^{{[n+1]}}=0
on
\Sigma
and
\Sigma^{\pm}
:

\displaystyle\bm{b}_{1}^{[n+1],\pm}=b_{1}^{{[n+1]},\pm},\quad\bm{b}_{2}^{[n+1],\pm}=b_{2}^{{[n+1]},\pm},

\displaystyle\bm{b}_{3}^{[n+1],\pm}=b_{3}^{[n+1],\pm}+\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}\left(b_{1}^{[n+1],\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\psi^{[n+1]}+b_{2}^{[n+1],\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\psi^{[n+1]}-b_{3}^{[n+1],\pm}\right)\big|_{\Sigma}.

(4.5)

Remark 4.1

(The boundary constraint of magnetic fields)
.

The modified basic state
\mathring{\bm{b}}
is necessary here, because the quantity
b^{[n+1]}
solved from (
4.1
) may not be tangential on
\Sigma
and so integrating
(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})
by parts produces uncontrollable boundary terms. When taking the limit
n\to\infty
, we can show that the limit function
b^{[\infty]}
also satisfy the boundary constraint
b^{[\infty]}\cdot N^{[\infty]}|_{\Sigma}=0
which then indicates that
\bm{b}_{3}^{[\infty]}=b_{3}^{[\infty]}
in order to recover the nonlinear approximate system (
3.1
). We refer to Section
4.1.1
for details.

Remark 4.2

(The divergence constraint of magnetic fields)
.

Notice that the divergence-free condition for
b^{\pm}
no longer propagates from the initial data for the linear problem, but we will show that the contribution of the divergence of part of
b^{\pm}
is still controllable and does not introduce extra substantial difficulty. After solving the nonlinear problem (
3.1
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
,
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b^{\pm}=0
in (
3.1
) is automatically recovered from the initial constraint
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm}=0
.

For simplicity of notations, given any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote
(v^{[n+1],\pm},b^{[n+1],\pm},q^{[n+1],\pm},p^{[n+1],\pm},\rho^{[n+1],\pm},S^{[n+1],\pm},\psi^{[n+1]})
,

(v^{[n],\pm},b^{[n],\pm},\bm{b}^{[n],\pm},q^{[n],\pm},\rho^{[n],\pm},p^{[n],\pm},S^{[n],\pm},\psi^{[n]})
,
\psi^{[n-1]}
respectively by
(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},q^{\pm},p^{\pm},\rho^{\pm},S^{\pm})
,
(\mathring{v}^{\pm},\mathring{b}^{\pm},\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm},\mathring{q}^{\pm},\mathring{\rho}^{\pm},\mathring{p}^{\pm},\mathring{S}^{\pm},\mathring{\psi})
and
\dot{\psi}
. Also, we denote
D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]},\pm}
and
\partial_{i}^{\varphi^{[n]}},~\nabla_{i}^{\varphi^{[n]}}
by
D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}
and
\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i},~\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}
. Thus, the linear problem above becomes

\begin{cases}\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}v^{\pm}-(\mathring{\bm{b}}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})b^{\pm}+\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}q^{\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}q^{\pm}-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}+\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v^{\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}b^{\pm}-(\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})v^{\pm}+\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v^{\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}S^{\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{H}(\mathring{\psi})-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\psi=v^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{N}&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v_{3}^{\pm}=0&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma^{\pm},\\
(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},q^{\pm},S^{\pm},\psi)|_{t=0}=(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa}),\end{cases}

(4.6)

where
D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}=\partial_{t}+\mathring{\bar{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}
and
\mathcal{H}(\mathring{\psi})=\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}/|\mathring{N}|).

4.1.1
The way to recover the nonlinear approximate system

Before going to those tedious heavy calculations, let us first see how to construct the solution to the nonlinear system (
3.1
) for fixed
\kappa>0
if the existence of (
4.6
) and the strong convergence result has been proven. We assume that the expected limit is denoted by
(v^{[\infty],\pm},b^{[\infty],\pm},\bm{b}^{[\infty],\pm},q^{[\infty],\pm},\psi^{[\infty]})
. Then the limit functions satisfy the following system

\begin{cases}\rho^{[\infty],\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[\infty]},\pm}v^{{[\infty]},\pm}-(\bm{b}^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}})b^{{[\infty]},\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}q^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[\infty]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}q^{{[\infty]},\pm}-(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[\infty]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot\bm{b}^{{[\infty]},\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}\cdot v^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}b^{{[\infty]},\pm}-(\bm{b}^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}})v^{{[\infty]},\pm}+\bm{b}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}\cdot v^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}S^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\left\llbracket q^{[\infty]}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi^{{[\infty]}})-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi^{{[\infty]}}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi^{{[\infty]}}&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\psi^{{[\infty]}}=v^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{{[\infty]}}&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v_{3}^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma^{\pm},\\
(v^{{[\infty]},\pm},b^{{[\infty]},\pm},q^{{[\infty]},\pm},S^{{[\infty]},\pm},\psi^{{[\infty]}})|_{t=0}=(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa}),\end{cases}

(4.7)

where
\rho^{[\infty]}
is defined via the equation of state
\rho=\rho(p,S)
and
p^{[\infty]}:=q^{[\infty]}-\frac{1}{2}|b^{[\infty]}|^{2}
. Also we have

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi^{[\infty]},\pm}=

\displaystyle~\partial_{t}+\bar{v}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[\infty]}}(v^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}^{{[\infty]}}-\partial_{t}\varphi^{[\infty]})\partial_{3},

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}=

\displaystyle~\bar{\bm{b}}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[\infty]}}(\bm{b}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]})\partial_{3}.

We must prove that
b_{3}^{[\infty],\pm}=\bm{b}_{3}^{[\infty],\pm}
in
\Omega^{\pm}
. According to the definition of
\bm{b}^{[n]}
, the limit function satisfies

\bm{b}_{3}^{[\infty],\pm}=b_{3}^{[\infty],\pm}+\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}\left(b_{1}^{[\infty],\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\psi^{[\infty]}+b_{2}^{[\infty],\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\psi^{[\infty]}-b_{3}^{[\infty],\pm}\right)\big|_{\Sigma}\Rightarrow\bm{b}_{3}^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot N^{{[\infty]}}|_{\Sigma}=0.

Since Lemma
B.3
implies that
\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}(0)=0
, then the remaining step is to show
b^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}|_{\Sigma}=0
holds with in the lifespan of the solution to (
4.7
) provided
b^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}|_{t=0}=0
on
\Sigma
. On
\Sigma
, we compute that

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}(b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]})=D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}+b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}N^{[\infty]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{(\bar{\bm{b}}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})}_{=\bar{b}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}}v^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}+\underbrace{(\bm{b}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]})}_{=0\text{ on }\Sigma}\partial_{3}v^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}+\underbrace{(\bm{b}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]})}_{=0\text{ on }\Sigma}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}\cdot v^{{[\infty]},\pm})

\displaystyle-\bar{b}_{i}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}\psi^{{[\infty]}}-\bar{b}_{i}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi^{{[\infty]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\bar{b}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\underbrace{\left(v_{3}^{{[\infty]},\pm}-\bar{v}_{j}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi^{{[\infty]}}\right)}_{=\partial_{t}\psi^{{[\infty]}}}+\bar{b}_{i}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi^{{[\infty]}}-\bar{b}_{i}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}\psi^{{[\infty]}}-\bar{b}_{i}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{{[\infty]},\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\psi^{{[\infty]}}=0.

Thus standard
L^{2}
energy estimate shows that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{{[\infty]},\pm})\left|b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq|\overline{\partial}{}v^{{[\infty]},\pm}|_{L^{\infty}}\left|b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}\right|_{0}^{2}.

(4.8)

Since
b^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}|_{t=0}=0
on
\Sigma
, we conclude that
b^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot N^{[\infty]}=0
always holds on
\Sigma
by using Grönwall’s inequality. Plugging it back to the expression of
\bm{b}_{3}^{[\infty],\pm}
, we find
\bm{b}_{3}^{{[\infty]},\pm}=b_{3}^{{[\infty]},\pm}
in
\Omega^{\pm}
as desired. Then we can replace
\bm{b}
by
b
in the limit system (
4.7
)

\begin{cases}\rho^{[\infty],\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[\infty]},\pm}v^{{[\infty]},\pm}-(b^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}})b^{{[\infty]},\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}q^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[\infty]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}q^{{[\infty]},\pm}-(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[\infty]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot b^{{[\infty]},\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}\cdot v^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}b^{{[\infty]},\pm}-(b^{[\infty],\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}})v^{{[\infty]},\pm}+b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}\cdot v^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{{[\infty]}},\pm}S^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\left\llbracket q^{[\infty]}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi^{{[\infty]}})-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi^{{[\infty]}}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi^{{[\infty]}}&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\psi^{{[\infty]}}=v^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{{[\infty]}},\quad b^{{[\infty]},\pm}\cdot N^{{[\infty]}}=0&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v_{3}^{{[\infty]},\pm}=b_{3}^{{[\infty]},\pm}=0&~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma^{\pm},\\
(v^{{[\infty]},\pm},b^{{[\infty]},\pm},q^{{[\infty]},\pm},S^{{[\infty]},\pm},\psi^{{[\infty]}})|_{t=0}=(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa}),\end{cases}

(4.9)

in which
(v^{[\infty],\pm},b^{[\infty],\pm},q^{[\infty],\pm},\psi^{[\infty]})
exactly gives the solution to the nonlinear approximate system (
3.1
). Finally, the divergence constraint
\nabla^{\varphi^{[\infty]}}\cdot b^{[\infty],\pm}=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
automatically holds thanks to the second equation, the fourth equation in (
4.9
) and
\nabla^{\varphi_{0}^{\kappa}}\cdot b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm}=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
.

4.2
Well-posedness of the linearized approximate problem

Although (
4.6
) is a first-order linear symmetric hyperbolic system with characteristic boundary conditions, it is still not easy to apply the duality argument in Lax-Phillips
[
29
]
to prove the well-posedness of (
4.6
) in
L^{2}([0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm})
because the boundary condition for the dual system of (
4.6
) may not be explicitly calculated due to the appearance of the regularization term
\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi
. Instead, we apply the classical Galerkin’s method to construct the weak solution in
L^{2}([0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm})
to the linearized problem (
4.6
). Once this is done, the weak solution is actually a strong solution by the argument in
[
43
, Chapter 2.2.3]
.

We assume the basic state
(\mathring{v},\mathring{b},\mathring{q},\mathring{\rho},\mathring{p},\mathring{S},\mathring{\psi})
and
\dot{\psi}
satisfy the following bounds: There exists some
\mathring{K}_{0}>0
and a time
T_{\kappa}>0
(depending on
\kappa>0
) such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\sum_{l=0}^{4}\bigg(\sum_{\pm}\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle=2l}\sum_{k=0}^{4-l}\left\|\left(\varepsilon^{2l}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(\mathring{v}^{\pm},\mathring{b}^{\pm},\mathring{S}^{\pm},(\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm})^{\frac{(k+\alpha_{0}-l-3)_{+}}{2}}\mathring{q}^{\pm})\right)\right\|^{2}_{4-k-l,\pm}

(4.10)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{4+l}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\psi}\right|^{2}_{6+l-k}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{5+l}\mathring{\psi}\right|^{2}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathring{K}_{0},

where
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}:=(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\alpha_{4}}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}
with the multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},0,\alpha_{4}),~\langle\alpha\rangle=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+2\times 0+\alpha_{4}
. Moreover, we have

\forall 0\leq T\leq T_{\kappa},\quad\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\right\|_{4-k-l,\pm}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0}).

Remark 4.3
.

The
L_{t}^{2}
-type bound of
\mathring{\bm{b}}
is obtained by using the second part of Lemma
B.3
and the
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted enhanced regularity for the free interface. Indeed, the modification term
\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}\left(\mathring{b}_{1}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\psi}+\mathring{b}_{2}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\psi}-\mathring{b}_{3}^{\pm}\right)\big|_{\Sigma}
has
vanishing initial value
thank to
b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm}\cdot N_{0}^{\kappa}=0
on
\Sigma
. Thus, one can extend this function to
(-\infty,T]\times\Omega^{\pm}
and then apply the trace lemma for anisotropic Sobolev spaces (cf. Trakhinin-Wang
[
61
, Lemma 3.4]
or Lemma
B.3
in this paper) to show that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(\mathring{b}^{\pm}-\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})\right\|_{4-k-l,\pm}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\left\|\mathring{b}^{\pm}-\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\right\|_{8,*,T,\pm}^{2}\lesssim\left|\mathring{b}_{1}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\psi}+\mathring{b}_{2}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\psi}-\mathring{b}_{3}^{\pm}\right|_{7,T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\left\|\mathring{b}_{1}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\varphi}+\mathring{b}_{2}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\varphi}-\mathring{b}_{3}^{\pm}\right\|_{8,*,T,\pm}^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\mathring{b}_{1}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\varphi}+\mathring{b}_{2}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\varphi}-\mathring{b}_{3}^{\pm}\right\|_{8,*,\pm}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim T\mathring{K}_{0},~~\forall T\in[0,T_{\kappa}],

where
\|\cdot\|_{m,*,T,\pm},|\cdot|_{m,T}
norms are defined in Appendix
B
.
Notice that this
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted enhanced regularity is necessary here, otherwise we lose the control of
|\overline{\partial}{}\psi(t)|_{8}
and a loss of tangential derivative occurs as in lots of previous works
[
6
,
60
,
61
,
62
]
and references therein.

4.2.1
Construction of Galerkin sequence

Since our domain
\Omega:=\mathbb{T}^{2}\times(-H,H)
is bounded, there exists an orthonormal basis
\{\bm{e}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}\subset C^{\infty}(\Omega)
for
L^{2}(\Omega)
which is also an orthogonal basis of
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
. To construct the Galerkin sequence, we first write the linearized system (
4.6
) into a symmetric hyperbolic system of
U^{\pm}:=(q^{\pm},v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{8}
:

\displaystyle A_{0}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\partial_{t}U^{\pm}+A_{1}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\partial_{1}U^{\pm}+A_{2}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\partial_{2}U^{\pm}+A_{3}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\partial_{3}U^{\pm}=0\quad\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}

(4.11)

where the coefficient matrices are

\displaystyle A_{0}(\mathring{U}):=\begin{bmatrix}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}&\vec{0}^{\top}&-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\top}&0\\
\vec{0}&\mathring{\rho}\bm{I}_{3}&\bm{O}_{3}&\vec{0}\\
-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}&\bm{O}_{3}&\bm{I}_{3}+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}\otimes\mathring{\bm{b}}&\vec{0}\\
0&\vec{0}^{\top}&\vec{0}^{\top}&1\end{bmatrix},~A_{i}(\mathring{U}):=\begin{bmatrix}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bar{v}}_{i}&\vec{e_{i}}^{\top}&-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bar{v}}_{i}\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\top}&0\\
\vec{e_{i}}&\mathring{\rho}\mathring{\bar{v}}_{i}\bm{I}_{3}&-\mathring{\bar{\bm{b}}}_{i}\bm{I}_{3}&\vec{0}\\
-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bar{v}}_{i}\mathring{\bm{b}}&-\mathring{\bar{\bm{b}}}_{i}\bm{I}_{3}&\mathring{\bar{v}}_{i}\bm{I}_{3}+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bar{v}}_{i}(\mathring{\bm{b}}\otimes\mathring{\bm{b}})&\vec{0}\\
0&\vec{0}^{\top}&\vec{0}^{\top}&\mathring{\bar{v}}_{i}\end{bmatrix}~(i=1,2),

\displaystyle A_{3}(\mathring{U}):=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}\begin{bmatrix}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})&\mathring{\mathbf{N}}^{\top}&-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\top}&0\\
\mathring{\mathbf{N}}&\mathring{\rho}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\bm{I}_{3}&-(\mathring{\bm{b}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{N}})\bm{I}_{3}&\vec{0}\\
-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\mathring{\bm{b}}&-(\mathring{\bm{b}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{N}})\bm{I}_{3}&(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\bm{I}_{3}+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})(\mathring{\bm{b}}\otimes\mathring{\bm{b}})&\vec{0}\\
0&\vec{0}^{\top}&\vec{0}^{\top}&\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}\end{bmatrix}.

Also notice that the matrix
A_{3}(\mathring{U})
is equal to the following matrix on the boundary

A_{3}(\mathring{U})|_{\Sigma,\Sigma^{\pm}}:=\begin{bmatrix}0&\mathring{\mathbf{N}}^{\top}&\bm{0}_{4}^{\top}&\\
\mathring{\mathbf{N}}&\bm{O}_{3}&\\
\bm{0}_{4}&&\bm{O}_{4}\end{bmatrix},

which has constant rank 2 and has one negative eigenvalue and one positive eigenvalue, so the correct number of boundary conditions to solve
U^{\pm}
in (
4.11
) is
1+1=2
(the jump conditions for
q
and
v\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{N}}
), and we need one more equation, namely
\partial_{t}\psi=v^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{N}
, to determine the free interface.

Given
2\leq m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we introduce the Galerkin sequence
\{U^{m,\pm}(t,x),\psi^{m}(t,x^{\prime})\}
by

\displaystyle U_{j}^{m,\pm}(t,x):=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{m}U_{lj}^{m,\pm}(t)\bm{e}_{l}(x)\quad 1\leq j\leq 8,

(4.12)

\displaystyle\psi^{m}(t,x^{\prime}):=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{m}\psi_{l}^{m}(t)\bm{e}_{l}(x^{\prime},0).

(4.13)

The Galerkin sequence is assumed to satisfy the boundary conditions

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\psi^{m}=

\displaystyle~U_{4}^{m,\pm}-U_{2}^{m,\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\psi}-U_{3}^{m,\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\psi}

(4.14)

\displaystyle\left\llbracket U_{1}^{m}\right\rrbracket=

\displaystyle~\sigma\mathcal{H}(\mathring{\psi})-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi^{m}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi^{m}

(4.15)

Now we introduce an ODE system as the “truncated version” of (
4.11
) in
\text{Span}\{\bm{e}_{1},\cdots,\bm{e}_{m}\}
by testing the Galerkin sequence by a vector field
\bm{\phi}:=(\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{8})^{\top}
with

\phi_{i}:=\sum_{l=1}^{m}\phi_{il}(t)\bm{e}_{l}(x)\in\text{Span}\{\bm{e}_{1},\cdots,\bm{e}_{m}\}.

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}A_{0}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})(\partial_{t}U_{j}^{m,\pm})\phi_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{2}A_{k}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})(\overline{\partial}{}_{k}U_{j}^{m,\pm})\phi_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}A_{3}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})(\partial_{3}U_{j}^{m,\pm})\phi_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}=0

(4.16)

where
\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}:=\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x
Integrating by parts in
\overline{\partial}{}_{k}
and
\partial_{3}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}A_{0}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})(\partial_{t}U_{j}^{m,\pm})\phi_{i}-\sum_{k=1}^{3}U_{j}^{m,\pm}\partial_{k}(A_{k}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\phi_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}\mp\int_{\Sigma}A_{3}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})U_{j}^{m,\pm}\phi_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=0.

(4.17)

Plugging the Galerkin sequence into the above identity, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}A_{0}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\bm{e}_{l}(x)\phi_{i}(U_{lj}^{m,\pm})^{\prime}(t)-\sum_{k=1}^{3}\partial_{k}(A_{k}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\phi_{i})\bm{e}_{l}(x)U_{lj}^{m,\pm}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=\pm\int_{\Sigma}A_{3}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})U_{j}^{m,\pm}\phi_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(4.18)

Taking sum for the two parts in
\Omega^{\pm}
, setting
\phi_{i}(x)=\bm{e}_{i}(x)
and using the jump condition for
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket
and
v^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{N}}
, we obtain a first-order linear ODE system for
\{U_{lj}^{\pm}(t)\}

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\left(\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}A_{0}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\bm{e}_{l}(x)\bm{e}_{i}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}\right)(U_{lj}^{m,\pm})^{\prime}(t)-\left(\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial_{k}(A_{k}^{ij}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})\bm{e}_{i}(x))\bm{e}_{l}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}\right)U_{lj}^{m,\pm}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\underbrace{\left(-\bm{e}_{2}(x^{\prime},0)\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\psi}(t,x^{\prime})-\bm{e}_{3}(x^{\prime},0)\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\psi}(t,x^{\prime})+\bm{e}_{4}(x^{\prime},0)\right)}_{=:\phi_{v}\cdot\mathring{N}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}/|\mathring{N}|)\cdot\overline{\nabla}\left(\phi_{v}\cdot\mathring{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi^{m}\,(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\left(\phi_{v}\cdot\mathring{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\left(\phi_{v}\cdot\mathring{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\left(\phi_{v}\cdot\mathring{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(4.19)

Since the basis
\{\bm{e}_{l}\}
are smooth and the coefficients
(\mathring{U}^{m,\pm},\mathring{\psi})
are sufficiently regular, standard ODE theory guarantees the local existence and uniqueness of the above ODE system (
4.19
) with initial data

U_{lj}^{m,\pm}(0):=\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}U_{j}^{m,\pm}(0,x)\bm{e}_{k}(x)\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x.

4.2.2
Existence of solutions to the linearized problem

The existence of weak solution is guaranteed by uniform-in-
m
estimates for the Galerkin sequence
\{U^{m,\pm}(t,x),\psi^{m}(t,x^{\prime})\}
. Now we let the test function
\bm{\phi}=U^{m,\pm}
in
\Omega^{\pm}
respectively to obtain the standard
L^{2}
-type energy estimates thanks to the symmetric property of the coefficient matrices and the concrete form of
A_{3}(\mathring{U})
on the boundary

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(U^{m,\pm})^{\top}\cdot A_{0}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})U^{m,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(U^{m,\pm})^{\top}\cdot\partial_{t}(A_{0}(\mathring{U}^{\pm}))U^{m,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(U^{m,\pm})^{\top}\cdot\partial_{k}(A_{k}(\mathring{U}^{\pm}))U^{m,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket U_{1}^{m}\right\rrbracket(U_{4}^{m,\pm}-U_{2}^{m,\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\psi}-U_{3}^{m,\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.20)

where the interior term can be controlled directly by
C(\mathring{K}_{0})\|U^{m,\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}
. For the boundary term, using the boundary conditions (
4.14
)-(
4.15
), we get the energy bounds under time integral

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket U_{1}^{m}\right\rrbracket(U_{4}^{m,\pm}-U_{2}^{m,\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\psi}-U_{3}^{m,\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\sigma\mathcal{H}(\mathring{\psi})-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi^{m}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\right)\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}/|\mathring{\mathbf{N}}|)\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi^{m}\,(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi_{m}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\partial_{t}\psi_{m}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi_{m}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{t}P(|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\delta|\sqrt{\kappa}\psi^{m}|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\psi^{m}\right|_{2}^{2}\bigg|^{t}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.21)

We define

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{m}(t):=\sum_{\pm}\left\|\left(\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}}U_{1}^{m,\pm},U_{2}^{m,\pm},\cdots,U_{8}^{m,\pm}\right)\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\psi^{m}\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}\psi^{m}\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.22)

Since
A_{0}(\mathring{U}^{\pm})>0
, we obtain the uniform-in-
m
estimate for the Galerkin sequence
\{U^{m,\pm}(t,x),\psi^{m}(t,x^{\prime})\}
.

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{m}(t)\leq\bm{N}^{m}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\bm{N}^{m}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(4.23)

and thus there exists a time
T_{\bm{N}}>0
(depending on
\kappa
and
\bm{N}^{m}(0)
, independent of
m
) such that

\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T_{\bm{N}}}\bm{N}^{m}(t)\leq C^{\prime}(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\bm{N}^{m}(0).

Because
L^{\infty}([0,T_{\bm{N}}];L^{2}(\Omega^{\pm}))
is not reflexive, we alternatively consider the weak convergence in
L^{2}([0,T_{\bm{N}}];L^{2}(\Omega^{\pm}))
. By Eberlein-Šmulian theorem and the uniqueness of expansion in Galerkin basis
\{\bm{e}_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
, there exists a subsequence
\{U^{m_{k},\pm}(t,x),\psi^{m_{k}}(t,x^{\prime})\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}}U_{1}^{m_{k},\pm},U_{2}^{m_{k},\pm},\cdots,U_{8}^{m_{k},\pm}\right)\rightharpoonup\left(\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}}q^{\pm},v^{\pm},\cdots,b^{\pm},S^{\pm}\right)\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{\bm{N}}];L^{2}(\Omega^{\pm}))

(4.24)

\displaystyle\psi^{m_{k}}\rightharpoonup\psi\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{\bm{N}}];H^{2}(\Sigma)),\quad\partial_{t}\psi^{m_{k}}\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}\psi\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{\bm{N}}];H^{1}(\Sigma)).

(4.25)

This proves the existence of weak solution to (
4.11
) (and equivalently (
4.6
)). The uniqueness easily follows from the estimate of
\bm{N}(t)
and the linearity of (
4.11
). The weak solution is actually a strong solution according to the argument in
[
43
, Chapter 2.2.3]
.

4.3
Higher-order estimates of the linearized approximate problem

To proceed the Picard iteration, we shall prove that the bounds (
4.10
) for the coefficients
(\mathring{U},\mathring{\psi},\dot{\psi})
can be preserved by the solution to (
4.6
). Fix
\kappa>0
, we define the energy functional for (
4.6
) to be

\displaystyle\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t):=

\displaystyle\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4}(t)+\cdots+\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{8}(t)

(4.26)

\displaystyle\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4+l}(t):=

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle=2l}\sum_{k=0}^{4-l}\left\|\left(\varepsilon^{2l}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},S^{\pm},(\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm})^{\frac{(k+\alpha_{0}-l-3)_{+}}{2}}q^{\pm})\right)\right\|^{2}_{4-k-l,\pm}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{4+l}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|^{2}_{6+l-k}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{5+l}\psi\right|^{2}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

where
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}:=(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\alpha_{4}}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}
with the multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},0,\alpha_{4}),~\langle\alpha\rangle=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+2\times 0+\alpha_{4}
. We aim to prove that

Proposition 4.1
.

There exists some
T_{\kappa}>0
depending on
\kappa
and
\mathring{K}_{0}
, such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\leq C(\kappa^{-1},\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0).

A large part of the proof of proposition (
4.1
) is similar to the analysis in Section
3
. Moreover, since
\kappa>0
is fixed, we obtain higher boundary regularity for the free interface
\psi
, which allows us to avoid some technical steps (such as the analysis in Section
3.4
). We will skip some details for the part substantially similar to Section
3
and emphasize the different part. Now we start with div-curl analysis.

4.3.1
Div-Curl analysis

We start with
\mathring{E}_{4}(t)
. Using (
B.1
) and the boundary conditions for
v,b
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\right\|_{4,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(|\mathring{\psi}|_{4},|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\|_{0}^{2}\right).

(4.27)

Remark 4.4
.

Here we cannot use the div-curl inequality (
B.2
) to estimate the normal traces because the boundary constraint
b\cdot\mathring{N}=0
no longer holds for the linearized problem.

The
L^{2}
-estimates are already proven in the uniform estimates of Galerkin sequence, so we no longer repeat it. The treatment of
\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v
is also the same as in Section
3.3.1
, that is, invoking the continuity equation. For
\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot b
, we no longer have the div-free constraint. Instead, we can take
\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot
in the linearized evolution equation of
b
to get

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot b^{\pm})=

\displaystyle~\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}(\mathring{\bm{b}}_{j}^{\pm}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}v_{i}^{\pm})-\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}(\mathring{\bm{b}}_{i}^{\pm}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}v_{j}^{\pm})+[D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm},\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot]b^{\pm}

(4.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}\mathring{\bm{b}}_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}v_{i}^{\pm})-(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\bm{b}^{\pm})(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v^{\pm})+[D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm},\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot]b^{\pm}.

(4.29)

Direct calculation shows that
[D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm},\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}](\cdot)=-(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}\mathring{v}_{j})\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}(\cdot)-(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}\partial_{t}(\mathring{\varphi}-\dot{\varphi}))\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}(\cdot)
. On the other hand, the
\kappa
-regularization term provides extra regularity for
\varphi_{t},\mathring{\varphi}_{t},\dot{\varphi}_{t}
. Thus, standard
H^{3}
estimates give the control of divergence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot b^{\pm}\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}\lesssim C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\left(\|b^{\pm}\|_{4,\pm}\|v^{\pm}\|_{4,\pm}\right)\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4}(t).

(4.30)

The vorticity part is analyzed in a similar way as in Section
3.6
. The evolution equations are

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times v)-(\mathring{\bm{b}}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)=

\displaystyle~(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\rho})\times(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}v)-(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\bm{b}}_{j})\times(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}b)

\displaystyle-\mathring{\rho}\left((\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{v}_{j})\times(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}v)+\nabla^{\varphi}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}-\partial_{t}\dot{\varphi})\times\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}v\right),

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)-(\mathring{\bm{b}}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times v)-\mathring{\bm{b}}\times\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v)=

\displaystyle-(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times\mathring{\bm{b}})(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v)-(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\bm{b}}_{j})\times(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}v)

\displaystyle-(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{v}_{j})\times(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}b)-\nabla^{\varphi}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}-\partial_{t}\dot{\varphi})\times\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}b,

on the right side of which the highest-order derivative is 1 (except the mismatch term). Thus, we can still follow the analysis in Section
3.6.2
to get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\rho}^{\pm}\left|\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times v^{\pm}\right|^{2}+\left|\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b^{\pm}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}\leq P(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4}(t),\mathring{K}_{0})+\mathring{K}_{1}^{\pm},

(4.31)

where

\displaystyle\mathring{K}_{1}^{\pm}:=\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b^{\pm})\cdot\left(\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v^{\pm}))\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}.

(4.32)

Again, we invoke the continuity equation and the momentum equation to get

\displaystyle\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot v))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}q)+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}\times((\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}b_{j})\mathring{\bm{b}}_{j})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\rho}\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}})^{2}v)-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}\times D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\mathring{b}_{j}\partial^{3}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}b)+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}\times D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}((\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}b_{j})\mathring{\bm{b}}_{j})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\rho}\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}})^{2}v)+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}\mathring{\bm{b}}\times D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b))

where we use the vector identity
(\mathbf{a}\times(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times\mathbf{b}))_{i}=(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}\mathbf{b}_{j})\mathbf{a}_{j}-\mathbf{a}_{j}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{j}\mathbf{b}_{i}
, and the omitted terms are directly controlled by
P(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4}(t),\mathring{K}_{0})
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{K}_{1}^{\pm}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{\rho}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)\cdot\left(\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}})^{2}v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)\cdot\left(\mathring{\bm{b}}\times D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}(\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}b))\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{\rho}(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)\cdot\left(\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}})^{2}v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}\left(\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)\right)\cdot\left(\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\left|\mathring{\bm{b}}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b)\right|_{0}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4}(t)+\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{5}(t).

So, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\rho}^{\pm}\left|\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times v^{\pm}\right|^{2}+\left|\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b^{\pm}\right|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\left|\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\times(\partial^{3}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times b^{\pm})\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~P(\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}_{4}^{\kappa}(t)+\mathring{E}_{5}^{\kappa}(t).

(4.33)

Similarly as in Section
3.6.2
and Section
3.6.3
, we can prove the div-curl estimates for time-differentiated system and
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
-differentiated system. For
0\leq l\leq 3,0\leq k\leq 3-l,\langle\alpha\rangle=2l,~\alpha_{3}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{4-l-k,\pm}^{2}\leq C(|\mathring{\psi}|_{3})\bigg(

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k-l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\bigg).

(4.34)

Then the curl part has the following control

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-l-k,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b^{\pm}\right\|_{3-l-k,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{b}\times(\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b^{\pm})\right\|_{3-l-k,\pm}^{2}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(\mathring{K}_{0})\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=0}^{l}\mathring{E}_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)+\mathring{E}_{4+l+1}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Similarly, the divergence part is controlled by

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3-l-k,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b^{\pm}\right\|_{3-l-k,\pm}^{2}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}(q^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3-l-k,\pm}^{2}+C(\mathring{K}_{0})\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}E_{4+j}^{\kappa}(0)\right)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=0}^{l}\mathring{E}_{4+j}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

in which the first term will be controlled via tangential estimates.

For the pressure
q
, we still use the linearized momentum equation to convert it to tangential derivatives of
v
and
b
. This step is exactly the same as Section
3.6.1
, so we do not repeat the details here.

4.3.2
Tangential estimates

The tangential estimates are also proved in the same way as Section
3.3
-Section
3.5
. What’s more, the rather technical part in the estimates of full time derivatives can be simplified a lot thanks to the
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted extra regularity of the free interface. For
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-differentiated linearize system (
4.6
), we introduce the corresponding Alinhac good unknown
\mathring{\mathbf{F}}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}f-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\varphi}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}f
which satisfies

\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}f)=\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}\mathring{\mathbf{F}}^{\gamma}+\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}^{\gamma}_{i}(f),\quad\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}f)=D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\mathbf{F}}^{\gamma}+\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\gamma}(f),

where

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}^{\gamma}(f)=

\displaystyle~(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}f)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\varphi}+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\frac{\mathring{\mathbf{N}}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}},\partial_{3}f\right]+\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\mathbf{N}}_{i},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}\right]+\mathring{\mathbf{N}}_{i}\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi})^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathring{\mathbf{N}}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f-\frac{\mathring{\mathbf{N}}_{i}}{(\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi})^{2}}\partial_{3}f[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\mathring{\varphi},

(4.37)

and

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\gamma}(f)=

\displaystyle~(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}f)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\bar{v}}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{}f+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}),\partial_{3}f\right]+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}),\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}\right]\partial_{3}f

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{v}]\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}\partial_{3}f-(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi})^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f+(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\frac{\partial_{3}f}{(\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi})^{2}}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\mathring{\varphi}+\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\partial_{t}(\dot{\varphi}-\mathring{\varphi})\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}f

(4.38)

with
\langle\gamma^{\prime}\rangle=1
. Since
\mathring{\mathbf{N}}_{3}=1
, the third term in
\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}^{\gamma}(f)
does not appear when
i=3
. Under this setting, the
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-differentiated linearized system is reformulated as follows

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\gamma,\pm}-(\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\gamma,\pm}+\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\gamma,\pm}=\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{v}^{\gamma,\pm}-\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}^{\gamma}(q^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(4.39)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\gamma,\pm}-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\gamma,\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}+\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\gamma,\pm}=\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{p}^{\gamma,\pm}-\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}^{\gamma}(v_{i}^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(4.40)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\gamma,\pm}-(\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\gamma,\pm}+\mathring{b}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\gamma,\pm})=\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{b}^{\gamma,\pm}-\mathring{b}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}^{\gamma}(v_{i}^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(4.41)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{S}}^{\pm,\alpha}=\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\gamma}(S^{\pm})~~

\displaystyle\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(4.42)

with boundary conditions

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\gamma}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathcal{H}(\mathring{\psi})-\kappa\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\psi-\kappa\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi-\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}~~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(4.43)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\gamma,\pm}\cdot\mathring{N}=\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi+\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}-\mathcal{W}^{\gamma,\pm}~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(4.44)

where
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{v},\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{p},\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{b}
terms consist of the following commutators

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{v}^{\gamma,\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}b^{\pm}-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\rho}^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}v^{\pm}-\mathring{\rho}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\gamma}(v^{\pm})

(4.45)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{p}^{\gamma,\pm}:=

\displaystyle-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}q^{\pm}-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\gamma}(q^{\pm})

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\gamma}(b^{\pm})\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}]\cdot D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}b^{\pm}

(4.46)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{b}^{\gamma,\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}v^{\pm}-\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\gamma}(b^{\pm}),

(4.47)

and the boundary term
\mathcal{W}^{\gamma,\pm}
is

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{W}}^{\gamma,\pm}:=(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{N})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathring{N}_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}].

(4.48)

Given
0\leq l\leq 4
, we shall consider the tangential estimates for
\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k-l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
for
0\leq k\leq 4-l
and
\langle\alpha\rangle=2l,~\alpha_{3}=0
. Following the analysis in Section
3.3
-Section
3.5
, using the linearized Reynolds transport theorem (Lemma
A.7
), dropping
\gamma
for simplicity of notations, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\rho}|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}(\mathring{b}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{v}^{\pm}-\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}(q^{\pm}))\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\left(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\rho}+\mathring{\rho}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{v}^{\pm}+\mathring{\rho}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}(\mathring{\bar{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})(\mathring{\varphi}-\dot{\varphi})\right)|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t},

(4.49)

where the last two terms can be directly controlled by
C(\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)
. In the rest of this section, we will use the notation
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}
to skip some of these remainder terms. We then analyze the first line. Integrating
(\mathring{b}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})
and
\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}
by parts and using
\mathring{b}\cdot\mathring{N}|_{\Sigma}=0
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}(\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot(\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}(\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}(\varepsilon^{2l}\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

(4.50)

and
