\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}=\underbrace{\pm\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}(\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{N})\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}_{=:\mathring{I}^{\pm}}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\mathring{I}^{\pm}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}(\varepsilon^{2l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}(\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}\underbrace{-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}}_{=:\mathring{Z}^{\pm}}.

(4.51)

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}\pm}(\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}(\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t},

(4.52)

we find that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}(\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}})\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\mathring{I}^{\pm}+\mathring{Z}^{\pm}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{p}^{\pm}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\left(\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}-\mathring{\mathbf{B}}^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{\bm{b}}^{\pm}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}.

(4.53)

Thus, we already get the energy terms for
\mathring{\mathbf{V}},\mathring{\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}
, and it remains to analyze the boundary term
\mathring{I}^{\pm}
. Again, following the analysis in Section
3.3
-Section
3.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{I}^{+}+\mathring{I}^{-}=\mathring{\text{ ST}}+\mathring{\text{ ST}}^{\prime}+\mathring{\text{ VS}}+\mathring{\text{ RT}}+\mathring{\text{ RT}}^{+}+\mathring{\text{ RT}}^{-}+\mathring{ZB}^{+}+\mathring{ZB}^{-}

(4.54)

where

\displaystyle\mathring{\text{ ST}}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\,\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.55)

\displaystyle{\mathring{\text{ ST}}}^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\,(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.56)

\displaystyle\mathring{\text{ VS}}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.57)

\displaystyle\mathring{\text{ RT}}:=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}\,\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.58)

\displaystyle\mathring{\text{ RT}}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q^{\pm}\,\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}\,(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.59)

\displaystyle\mathring{ZB}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathcal{W}}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},~~\mathring{Z}^{\pm}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}.

(4.60)

4.3.3
Analysis of the boundary integrals

Since the weight function
\omega(x_{3})
vanishes on
\Sigma
, we can alternatively write
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}=\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}^{2l-\alpha_{0}}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\partial_{t}^{k+\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-(k+\alpha_{0})}
. Replacing
k+\alpha_{0}
by
k
, it suffices to analyze the case
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}
for
0\leq k\leq 4+l,~0\leq l\leq 4
. First, there is no need to analyze
\mathring{\text{ RT}}
and
\mathring{\text{ RT}}^{\pm}
because they can be directly controlled by using the energy bounds (
4.10
) for the basic state. For the term
\mathring{\text{ ST}}
, the boundary regularity is given by the
\kappa
-regularization terms instead of the surface tension because we do not need a uniform-in-
\kappa
estimate for the linearized problem. Using the jump conditions for
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket
and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\text{ ST}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=

\displaystyle~\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\,\partial_{t}^{k+1}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\psi\right|_{2}^{2}\bigg|^{t}_{0}-\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\delta\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{\kappa}\int_{0}^{t}C(\mathring{K}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.61)

For the term
\mathring{\text{ ST}}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}{\mathring{\text{ ST}}}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=

\displaystyle~\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)\cdot\overline{\nabla}\left((\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\mathring{\psi}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\psi\,(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\left((\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\mathring{\psi}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\psi\,\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\left((\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\mathring{\psi}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\sigma C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})t+C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\psi\right|_{2}\|v^{+}\|_{4,\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+\delta\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\|v^{+}\|_{4,\pm}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\sigma C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})t+C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{E}_{4+l}^{\kappa}(\tau)+\mathring{E}_{4}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.62)

The term
\mathring{\text{ VS}}
can also be directly controlled even if
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
only contains time derivatives. When
k<4+l
, we can use the
\kappa
-weighted energy to control it after integrating
\overline{\partial}{}^{\frac{1}{2}}
by parts and using Lemma
B.4

\displaystyle\mathring{\text{ VS}}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3.5+l-k}q^{-}\,\overline{\partial}{}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left((\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\mathring{\psi}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}q^{-}\|_{0,-}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k}\partial_{3}q^{-}\|_{0,-}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\bar{v}^{\pm}|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},\infty}}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\psi}\right|_{5.5+l-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4}(t)+\mathring{E}^{\kappa}_{4+l}(t))C(\mathring{K}_{0}).

(4.63)

When
k=4+l
, we can first integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts and then integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{\frac{1}{2}}
by parts

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{\text{ VS}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\overline{\partial}{}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{3+l}q^{-})\,\overline{\partial}{}^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{5+l}\mathring{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{t}^{3+l}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4+l}\mathring{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\bigg|^{t}_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\int_{0}^{t}\|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3+l}q^{-}\|_{0,-}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\partial_{3}q^{-}\|_{0,-}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\bar{v}^{\pm}|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},\infty}}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{5+l}\mathring{\psi}\right|_{0.5}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+\delta\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{3+l}q^{-}\|_{1,-}^{2}+|\bar{v}^{\pm}|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}\right|_{1}^{2}+C(\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\mathring{E}_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\left(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right).

(4.64)

For
\mathring{ZB}+\mathring{Z}
, the cancellation structure obtained in Section
3.3.1
and Section
3.4
still holds. Following step 4 in Section
3.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{ZB}^{\pm}+\mathring{Z}^{\pm}=

\displaystyle~\mp\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}(\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}q^{\pm}-\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\mathring{\psi}\partial_{3}q^{\pm})(\partial_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathring{N})\,\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}\mathring{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mp\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k},\mathring{N}_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t},

(4.65)

where the first line is controlled in the same way as
\mathring{\text{ VS}}
. Mimicing the proof in step 4 in Section
3.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mp\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k},\mathring{N}_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k},\mathring{\mathbf{N}}_{i},v_{i}^{\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t},

(4.66)

whose time integral can be directly controlled by

\delta\mathring{E}_{4+l}^{\kappa}(t)+C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\left(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

after integrating by parts one tangential derivative in
\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}
.

4.3.4
Uniform-in-
n
estimates for the linearized approximate system

Summarizing the estimates obtained in Section
4.3.1
-Section
4.3.3
, we prove that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\lesssim\delta\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)+C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\left(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right).

Choosing
\delta>0
suitably small such that the
\delta
-term can be absorbed by the left side and using Grönwall’s inequality, we find that there exists a time
T_{\kappa}>0
(independent of
\varepsilon
and
n
), such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\leq C^{\prime}(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)

for some positive function
C^{\prime}
continuous in its arguments. Following the argument in remark
4.3
, it is straightforward to show that

\sum_{\pm}\sum_{l=0}^{4}\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle=2l}\sum_{k=0}^{4-l}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}\bm{b}^{\pm}\right\|_{4-k-l,\pm}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau<P(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t))\quad\forall t\in[0,T_{\kappa}].

4.4
Picard iteration: well-posedness of the nonlinear approximate problem

We already establish the local existence of the linear system (
4.1
) for each
n
and the uniform-in-
n
estimates for the solution to (
4.1
). It suffices to prove
\{(v^{{[n]},\pm},b^{{[n]},\pm},\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm},q^{{[n]},\pm},\psi^{{[n]}})\}
has a strongly convergent subsequence (in certain anisotropic Sobolev norms). For a function sequence
\{f^{{[n]},\pm}\}
, we define
[f]^{{[n]},\pm}:=f^{{[n+1]},\pm}-f^{{[n]},\pm}
. Then we can write the linear system of
\{([v]^{{[n]},\pm},[b]^{{[n]},\pm},[q]^{{[n]},\pm},[\psi]^{{[n]}})\}
as follows

\begin{cases}\rho^{{[n]},\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[v]^{{[n]},\pm}-(\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}})[b]^{{[n]},\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}[q]^{{[n]},\pm}+\nabla^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}q^{{[n]},\pm}=-\mathring{f}_{v}^{{[n]},\pm}&\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm}\\
\mathcal{F}_{p}^{{[n]},\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[q]^{{[n]},\pm}-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{{[n]},\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[b]^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[v]^{{[n]},\pm}+\nabla^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}\cdot v^{{[n]},\pm}=-\mathring{f}_{p}^{{[n]},\pm}&\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm}\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[b]^{{[n]},\pm}-(\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}})[v]^{{[n]},\pm}+\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[v]^{{[n]},\pm}+\nabla^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}\cdot v^{{[n]},\pm})=-\mathring{f}_{b}^{{[n]},\pm}&\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm}\\
D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[S]^{{[n]},\pm}=-\mathring{f}_{S}^{{[n]},\pm}&\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm}\\
\left\llbracket[q]^{{[n]},\pm}\right\rrbracket=\sigma(\mathcal{H}(\psi^{[n]})-\mathcal{H}(\psi^{[n-1]}))-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}[\psi]^{[n]}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}[\psi]^{[n]}&\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma\\
\partial_{t}[\psi]^{[n]}=[v]^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot N^{[n]}+v^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot[N]^{{[n-1]}}&\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma\\
v_{3}^{{[n]},\pm}=v_{3}^{{[n-1]},\pm}=\bm{b}_{3}^{{[n]},\pm}=\bm{b}_{3}^{{[n-1]},\pm}=0&\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma^{\pm}\\
([v]^{[n]},[b]^{[n]},[q]^{[n]},[\psi]^{[n]})|_{t=0}=(\vec{0},\vec{0},0,0),\end{cases}

(4.67)

where the source terms are defined by

\displaystyle\mathring{f}_{v}^{{[n]},\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\rho]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{t}v^{{[n]},\pm}+[\rho\bar{v}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}v^{{[n]},\pm}+[\rho V_{\mathbf{N}}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{3}v^{{[n]},\pm}

\displaystyle~-[\bm{b}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}b^{{[n]},\pm}-[\bm{B}_{\mathbf{N}}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{3}b^{{[n]},\pm},

(4.68)

\displaystyle\mathring{f}_{q}^{{[n]},\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{F}_{p}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{t}q^{{[n]},\pm}+[\mathcal{F}_{p}\bar{v}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}q^{{[n]},\pm}+[\mathcal{F}_{p}V_{\mathbf{N}}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{3}q^{{[n]},\pm}

\displaystyle-([\mathcal{F}_{p}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{t}b^{{[n]},\pm}+[\mathcal{F}_{p}\bar{v}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}b^{{[n]},\pm}+[\mathcal{F}_{p}V_{\mathbf{N}}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{3}b^{{[n]},\pm})\cdot\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}

\displaystyle-(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{{[n-1]}}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n-1]}}b^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot[\bm{b}]^{{[n-1]},\pm},

(4.69)

\displaystyle\mathring{f}_{p}^{{[n]},\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\bar{v}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}b^{{[n]},\pm}+[V_{\mathbf{N}}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{3}b^{{[n]},\pm}-[\bm{b}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}v^{{[n]},\pm}-[\bm{B}_{\mathbf{N}}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{3}v^{{[n]},\pm}

\displaystyle+[\bm{b}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n-1]}}\cdot v^{{[n]},\pm}),

(4.70)

\displaystyle\mathring{f}_{S}^{{[n]},\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\bar{v}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}S^{{[n]},\pm}+[V_{\mathbf{N}}]^{{[n-1]},\pm}\partial_{3}S^{{[n]},\pm},

(4.71)

with

\displaystyle V_{\mathbf{N}}^{[n]}:=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[n]}}(v^{[n]}\cdot\mathbf{N}^{[n-1]}-\partial_{t}\varphi^{[n]}),\quad\bm{B}_{\mathbf{N}}^{[n]}:=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi^{[n]}}(\bm{b}^{[n]}\cdot\mathbf{N}^{[n]}),\quad\nabla^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}f^{[n]}:=-[\mathbf{N}/\partial_{3}\varphi]^{[n-1]}\partial_{3}f^{[n]}

For
1\leq n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we define the energy for the linear system (
4.67
) as follows

\displaystyle[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(t):=

\displaystyle~[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]_{3}^{[n]}(t)+\cdots+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]_{6}^{[n]}(t),

\displaystyle[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]_{3+l}^{[n]}(t):=

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle=2l}\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}([v]^{{[n]},\pm},[b]^{{[n]},\pm},[q]^{{[n]},\pm},[S]^{{[n]},\pm})\right\|_{3-k-l}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{3+l}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}[\psi]^{[n]}\right|_{5+l-k}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}[\psi]^{[n]}\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,~~0\leq l\leq 3,

(4.72)

where
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}:=(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\alpha_{4}}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}
with the multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},0,\alpha_{4}),~\langle\alpha\rangle=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+2\times 0+\alpha_{4}
. It should be noted that the initial value of
[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}
. Thus, we shall prove the following proposition in order for the strong convergence.

Proposition 4.2
.

There exists a time
T_{\kappa}^{\prime}>0
depending on
\kappa
and
\mathring{K}_{0}
, such that

\displaystyle\forall 2\leq n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{{[n]}}(t)\leq\frac{1}{4}\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{{[n-1]}}(t)+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{{[n-2]}}(t)\right).

(4.73)

The proof of proposition is substantially similar to the estimates of
\mathring{E}(t)
in Section
4.3
, so we will not go into every detail but only write the sketch of the proof.

Step 1: Div-Curl analysis and reduction of pressure

The reduction of pressure follows in the same way as in Section
3.6.1
. Invoking the momentum equation, we have

-(\partial_{3}\varphi^{{[n]}})^{-1}\partial_{3}[q]^{{[n]},\pm}=\rho^{{[n]},\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[v]^{{[n]},\pm}-(\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}})[b]^{{[n]},\pm}+\mathring{f}_{v}^{{[n]},\pm}+(\partial_{3}\varphi^{{[n]}})^{-1}\partial_{3}q^{{[n]},\pm}.

Then using
\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}=\overline{\partial}{}_{i}-\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\mathring{\varphi}\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{3}
, we can convert
\partial q
to a spatial derivative of
v
and
b
plus the given term
\partial_{3}q^{{[n]},\pm}
.

For the div-curl analysis, using (
B.1
), we have for
0\leq l\leq 2,~0\leq k\leq 2-l

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}([v]^{{[n]},\pm},[b]^{{[n]},\pm})\right\|_{3-k-l,\pm}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0})

\displaystyle\bigg(\|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}([v]^{{[n]},\pm},[b]^{{[n]},\pm})\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}([v]^{{[n]},\pm},[b]^{{[n]},\pm})\right\|_{2-k-l,\pm}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}([v]^{{[n]},\pm},[b]^{{[n]},\pm})\right\|_{2-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3-k-l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}([v]^{{[n]},\pm},[b]^{{[n]},\pm})\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\bigg).

(4.74)

The
L^{2}
estimate is straightforward, so we skip the proof. For the curl part, we again analyze the evolution equations of vorticity and current

\displaystyle\rho^{[n]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times[v]^{[n]})-(\bm{b}^{[n]}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}})(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times[b]^{[n]})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times\mathring{f}_{v}^{[n]}-\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\rho^{[n]}\times D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[v]^{[n]}+(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\bm{b}_{j}^{{[n]}})\times(\partial_{j}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[b]^{[n]})+\rho^{[n]}[D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}},\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times][v]^{[n]},

(4.75)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times[b]^{[n]})-(\bm{b}^{[n]}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}})(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times[v]^{[n]})-\bm{b}^{[n]}\times(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[v]^{[n]}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times\mathring{f}_{b}^{[n]}+[D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}},\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times][b]^{[n]}+(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\bm{b}_{j}^{{[n]}})\times(\partial_{j}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[v]^{[n]})

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times(\bm{b}^{[n]}\nabla^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}\cdot v^{{[n]},\pm})-(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times\bm{b}^{[n]})(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[v]^{[n]}).

(4.76)

Mimicing the proof in Section
4.3.1
and using the vanishing initial value of system (
4.67
), we can prove

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}[v]^{{[n]},\pm}\right\|_{2-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}[b]^{{[n]},\pm}\right\|_{2-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\sqrt{(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[n]}}\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}\times(\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}[b]^{\pm})\right\|_{2-l-k,\pm}^{2}

(4.77)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=0}^{l}[E]_{3+j}^{{[n]}}(\tau)+[E]_{3+l+1}^{{[n]}}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Similarly, the divergence of
[v]^{[n]}
can be converted to tangential derivatives of
[q]^{[n]}
and
[b]^{[n]}
by invoking the continuity equation, and the evolution equation of
\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot[b]^{[n]}
is

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[b]^{[n]})=

\displaystyle~(\partial_{i}^{\varphi^{[n]}}\bm{b}^{[n]}_{j})(\partial_{j}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[v]_{i}^{[n]})-(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot\bm{b}^{[n]})(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[v]^{[n]})+[D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}},\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot][b]^{[n]}

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot(\mathring{f}_{b}^{[n]}+\bm{b}^{[n]}\nabla^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}\cdot v^{{[n]},\pm}),

(4.78)

so the divergence part is controlled by

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}[v]^{{[n]},\pm}\right\|_{2-k-l,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}[b]^{{[n]},\pm}\right\|_{2-k-l,\pm}^{2}

(4.79)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{[n]}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}([q]^{\pm},[b]^{\pm})\right\|_{2-l-k,\pm}^{2}+C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=0}^{l}[E]_{3+j}^{{[n]}}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

in which the first term will be reduced to tangential estimates.

Step 2: Tangential estimates

It remains to prove the tangential estimates for
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-differentiated system where
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3-l-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
satisfies
\alpha_{3}=0,\langle\alpha\rangle=2l,~0\leq k\leq 3-l,~0\leq l\leq 3
. We shall introduce the Alinhac good unknowns
([\mathbf{V}],[\mathbf{B}],[\mathbf{Q}])
as below instead of directly taking tangential derivatives in (
4.67
).

\displaystyle[\mathbf{F}]^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle~\mathring{\mathbf{F}}^{[n+1]}-\mathring{\mathbf{F}}^{[n]}=\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[f]^{[n]}-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi^{[n]}\partial_{3}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[f]^{[n]}-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi^{[n]}\partial_{3}^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}q^{[n]}-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\varphi]^{[n-1]}\partial_{3}^{\varphi^{[n-1]}}q^{[n]}

and it satisfies

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(\partial_{i}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[f]^{[n]}+\partial_{i}^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}f^{[n]})=\partial_{i}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{F}]^{[n]}+[\mathfrak{C}]_{i}^{[n]}(f),\quad\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}(D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[f]^{[n]}+D_{t}^{[\varphi]^{[n-1]}}f^{[n]})=D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{F}]^{[n]}+[\mathfrak{D}]^{[n]}(f)

with

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{C}]_{i}^{[n]}(f)=\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n]}(f^{[n+1]})-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n-1]}(f^{[n]})+\text{lower-order controllable terms}

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{D}]^{[n]}(f)=\mathfrak{D}^{[n]}(f^{[n+1]})-\mathfrak{D}^{[n-1]}(f^{[n]})+\text{lower-order controllable terms}

where
\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n]}(f^{[m]}),\mathfrak{D}^{[n]}(f^{[m]})
are defined by setting
\mathring{\varphi}=\varphi^{[n]},~\dot{\varphi}=\varphi^{[n-1]},~f^{[n+1]}=f,~f^{[n]}=\mathring{f}
in (
4.3.2
)-(
4.3.2
). This can be seen by substracting the corresponding identities of
\mathring{\mathbf{F}}
with superscript
{[n-1]}
from the ones with superscript
{[n]}
. The evolution equations of the good unknowns are (with
\pm
dropped)

\displaystyle\rho^{[n]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{V}]^{[n]}-(\bm{b}^{[n]}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}})[\mathbf{B}]^{[n]}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{Q}]^{[n]}=-\mathfrak{C}^{[n]}(q^{[n+1]})+\mathfrak{C}^{[n-1]}(q^{[n]})+[\mathcal{R}]_{v}

\displaystyle~~\text{ in }\Omega

(4.80)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{p}^{[n]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{Q}]^{[n]}-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{[n]}D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{B}]^{[n]}\cdot\bm{b}^{[n]}+\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]^{[n]}=-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n]}(v_{i}^{[n+1]})+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n-1]}(v_{i}^{[n]})+[\mathcal{R}]_{q}

\displaystyle~~\text{ in }\Omega

(4.81)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{B}]^{[n]}-(\bm{b}^{[n]}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}})[\mathbf{V}]^{[n]}+\bm{b}^{[n]}(\nabla^{\varphi^{[n]}}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]^{[n]})=-\bm{b}^{[n]}\left(\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n]}(v_{i}^{[n+1]})-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n-1]}(v_{i}^{[n]})\right)+[\mathcal{R}]_{b}

\displaystyle~~\text{ in }\Omega

(4.82)

where
[\mathcal{R}]
terms are controllable in
L^{2}(\Omega)
by

\|[\mathcal{R}]\|_{0}^{2}\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0})([\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(t)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}(t)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-2]}(t)).

The boundary conditions of these good unknowns on the interface
\Sigma
are

\displaystyle[\mathbf{Q}]^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle~\sigma\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left(\mathcal{H}(\psi^{[n]})-\mathcal{H}(\psi^{[n-1]})\right)-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})^{2}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n]}-\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n]}

\displaystyle-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi^{[n]}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}[q]^{[n]}\right\rrbracket-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q^{[n]}\right\rrbracket

(4.83)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{V}]^{[n]}\cdot N^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\partial_{t}[\psi]^{[n]}+[\bar{v}]^{[n]}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi^{[n]}+(\bar{v}^{[n]}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}+\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\bar{v}^{[n]}\cdot\overline{\nabla}[\psi]^{[n-1]}-[\mathcal{W}]^{[n]}

(4.84)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{W}]^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle~(\partial_{3}[v]^{[n]}\cdot N^{[n]})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi^{[n]}+(\partial_{3}v^{[n]}\cdot N^{[n]})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},N_{i}^{[n]},v_{i}^{[n+1]}\right]-\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},N_{i}^{[n-1]},v_{i}^{[n]}\right]

(4.85)

Given
0\leq l\leq 3
, following Section
4.3.2
, we can similarly prove that

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{2l}\rho^{[n]}|[\mathbf{V}]^{{[n]},\pm}|^{2}+|[\mathbf{B}]^{{[n]},\pm}|^{2}+(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{{[n]},\pm}([\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}-[\mathbf{B}]^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{[n]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~[\text{ ST}]^{[n]}+[\text{ ST}^{\prime}]^{[n]}+[\text{ VS}]^{[n]}+[\text{ RT}]^{[n]}+\sum_{\pm}[\text{ RT}]^{{[n]},\pm}+([ZB]^{{[n]},\pm}+[Z]^{{[n]},\pm})

(4.86)

\displaystyle+C(\mathring{K}_{0})([\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(t)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}(t)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-2]}(t))

where the term
[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-2]}
is produced from the estimates of
[\varphi]^{[n-1]},[\varphi]^{[n-2]}
. The above terms on the right side are defined by

\displaystyle[\text{ ST}]^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left\llbracket[q]^{[n]}\right\rrbracket\,\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n]}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.87)

\displaystyle[\text{ ST}^{\prime}]^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left\llbracket[q]^{[n]}\right\rrbracket\,([\bar{v}^{+}]^{[n]}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi^{[n]}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left\llbracket[q]^{[n]}\right\rrbracket\,(\bar{v}^{{[n]},+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.88)

\displaystyle[\text{ VS}]^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[q]^{{[n]},-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket^{[n]}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi^{[n]}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[q]^{{[n]},-}\,\left(\left\llbracket\bar{v}^{{[n]}}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}\right)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.89)

\displaystyle[\text{ RT}]^{[n]}:=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\left\llbracket\partial_{3}[q]^{[n]}\right\rrbracket\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi^{[n]}+\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q^{[n]}\right\rrbracket\right)\,\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.90)

\displaystyle[\text{ RT}]^{{[n]},\pm}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\partial_{3}[q]^{{[n]},\pm}\,\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi^{[n]}+\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}\partial_{3}q^{{[n]},\pm}\right)\,\left((\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathring{\psi}+(\bar{v}^{{[n]},\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}[\psi]^{[n-1]}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.91)

\displaystyle[ZB]^{{[n]},\pm}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}[\mathcal{W}]^{{[n]},\pm}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},~~[Z]^{{[n]},\pm}=-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}(\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n]}(v_{i}^{{[n+1]},\pm})-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{[n-1]}(v_{i}^{{[n]},\pm}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{[n]}.

(4.92)

Step 3: Boundary regularity of
[\psi]

The analysis of the boundary integrals is still similar to Section
4.3.3
. Since
\omega(x_{3})=0
on
\Sigma
, we can rewrite
\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
to be
\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k}
for
0\leq k\leq 3+l,~0\leq l\leq 3
. Then the term
[\text{ ST}]^{[n]}
gives the regularity of
[\psi]^{[n]}
after inserting the jump condition for
[q]^{[n]}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\text{ ST}]^{[n]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim-\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}[\psi]^{[n]}\right|_{5+l-k}^{2}\bigg|^{t}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\left|\sqrt{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k+1}[\psi]^{[n]}\right|_{4+l-k}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{\kappa}C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(\tau)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.93)

The term
[\text{ ST}^{\prime}]^{[n]}
can be controlled by inserting the jump condition for
[q]^{[n]}
and then integrating by parts
\overline{\nabla}\cdot,1-\overline{\Delta}{},\sqrt{1-\overline{\Delta}{}}
in the three terms in
[q]^{[n]}
respectively. This is essentially the same as shown in Section
4.3.3
, so we only list the result

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}[\text{ ST}]^{[n]}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\sigma C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})t+C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(\tau)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(4.94)

The terms
[\text{ RT}]^{[n]},[\text{ RT}]^{{[n]},\pm}
are also controlled directly with the help of
\kappa
-weighted enhanced regularity. The term
[\text{ VS}]^{[n]}
is also controlled directly by integrating by parts for one tangential derivative in
\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k}
as in Section
4.3.3
. Finally, for the term
([ZB]^{{[n]},\pm}+[Z]^{{[n]},\pm})
, we still have the previously-used cancellation structure

\displaystyle[ZB]^{{[n]},\pm}+[Z]^{{[n]},\pm}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\mp\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k},N_{i}^{[n]},v_{i}^{{[n]},\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}^{[n]}_{i}(v_{i}^{{[n]},\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{[n]}

\displaystyle\pm\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k},N_{i}^{[n-1]},v_{i}^{{[n+1]},\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}\mathfrak{C}^{[n-1]}_{i}(v_{i}^{{[n]},\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{[n]}

(4.95)

where the omitted terms are controlled in the same way as
[\text{ VS}]^{[n]}
. Mimicing the proof in step 4 in Section
3.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mp\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k},N_{i}^{[n]},v_{i}^{{[n]},\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}^{[n]}_{i}(v_{i}^{{[n]},\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{[n]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}\partial_{3}^{\varphi^{[n]}}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k},\mathbf{N}_{i}^{{[n]}},v_{i}^{{[n+1]},\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\mathring{\mathcal{V}}_{t},

(4.96)

whose time integral can be directly controlled by

\delta[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(t)+C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\int_{0}^{t}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(\tau)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

after integrating by parts for one tangential derivative in
\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k}
. Similar estimate applies to the second line of
[ZB]^{{[n]},\pm}+[Z]^{{[n]},\pm}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\pm\int_{\Sigma}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}\left[\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{3+l-k},N_{i}^{[n-1]},v_{i}^{{[n+1]},\pm}\right]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\varepsilon^{4l}[\mathbf{Q}]^{{[n]},\pm}\mathfrak{C}^{[n-1]}_{i}(v_{i}^{{[n]},\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{[n]}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(t)+C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\int_{0}^{t}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(\tau)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}(\tau)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-2]}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

Step 4: Convergence

Summarizing the above estimates and using
[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(0)=0
, we obtain the energy inequality

[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(t)\lesssim\delta[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(t)+C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})\int_{0}^{t}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n]}(\tau)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-1]}(\tau)+[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{[n-2]}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Choosing
0<\delta\ll 1
suitably small, the
\delta
-term can be absorbed by the left side. Thus, there exists a time
T_{\kappa}^{\prime}>0
depending on
\kappa,\mathring{K}_{0}
and independent of
n
, such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{{[n]}}(t)\leq\frac{1}{4}\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{{[n-1]}}(t)+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{{[n-2]}}(t)\right),

(4.97)

and thus we know by induction that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}[\mathring{E}^{\kappa}]^{{[n]}}(t)\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0},\kappa^{-1})/2^{n-1}\to 0\text{ as }n\to+\infty.

(4.98)

Hence, for any fixed
\kappa>0
, the sequence of approximate solutions
\{(v^{{[n]},\pm},b^{{[n]},\pm},\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm},q^{{[n]},\pm},\psi^{[n]})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
has a strongly convergent subsequence. We write the limit function to be
\{(v^{[\infty],\pm},b^{[\infty],\pm},\bm{b}^{[\infty],\pm}q^{[\infty],\pm},\psi^{[\infty]})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
to keep consistent with the notations in Section
4.1.1
.

Step 5: Well-posedness of the nonlinear approximate problem for each fixed
\kappa

According to the argument about the limiting process in Section
4.1.1
, we know the limit of
b^{{[n]},\pm}
coincides with the limit of
\bm{b}^{{[n]},\pm}
. Thus, the limit functions
\{(v^{[\infty],\pm},b^{[\infty],\pm},q^{[\infty],\pm},\psi^{[\infty]})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
introduced in Section
4.1.1
exactly give the solution to the nonlinear
\kappa
-problem (
3.1
) in the time interval
[0,T_{\kappa}^{\prime}]
for each fixed
\kappa>0
. The uniqueness follows from a parallel argument.

5
Well-posedness and incompressible limit

5.1
Well-posedness of compressible current-vortex sheets with surface tension

We are ready to prove the local well-posedness of the original system (
1.30
) for 3D compressible current-vortex sheets with fixed surface tension coefficient
\sigma>0
. Recall that we introduce the nonlinear approximate system (
3.1
) indexed by
\kappa>0
. In Section
4
, we use Galerkin approximation and Picard iteration to prove the well-posedness of (
3.1
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
. The lifespan for (
3.1
) may rely on
\kappa>0
. Then we prove the uniform-in-
\kappa
estimates for (
3.1
)
without loss of regularity
so that we can extend the solution of (
3.1
) to a
\kappa
-independent lifespan
[0,T]
. In Appendix
C
, we construct the initial data of (
3.1
) that converges to the given initial data of (
1.30
) as
\kappa\to 0
. Thus, by taking
\kappa\to 0
, we obtain the local existence of the original system (
1.30
) and the energy estimates for
E(t)
defined in (
1.33
) without loss of regularity.

It remains to prove the uniqueness. Namely, we assume
(v^{[1],\pm},b^{[1],\pm},q^{[1],\pm},\psi^{[1]})
and
(v^{[2],\pm},b^{[2],\pm},q^{[2],\pm},\psi^{[2]})
are two solutions to (
1.30
)
with the same initial data
. Define
[f]:=f^{[1]}-f^{[2]}
, and we need to prove
([v]^{\pm},[b]^{\pm},[q]^{\pm},[\psi])
are identically zero. In fact, the argument for uniqueness is quite similar to the analysis in Section
4.4
. The only difference is that the boundary regularity is now given by the surface tension instead of the
\kappa
-regularization terms. This has been studied in the previous paper
[
38
, Section 6]
by Luo and the author, so we refer to
[
38
, Section 6]
and omit the details here.

5.2
Incompressible limit of compressible current-vortex sheets with surface tension

Next, we justify the incompressible limit of the solution obtained above, that is the limiting behavior of the local-in-time solution of (
1.30
) as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Given
\sigma>0
, we introduce the equations of
(\xi^{\sigma},w^{\pm,\sigma},h^{\pm,\sigma})
describing the motion of incompressible non-uniform current-vortex sheets together with a transport equation of entropy
\mathfrak{S}^{\sigma}

\begin{cases}\mathfrak{R}^{\pm,\sigma}(\partial_{t}+w^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}})w^{\pm,\sigma}-(h^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}})h^{\pm,\sigma}+\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}}\Pi^{\pm,\sigma}=0&~~~\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}}\cdot w^{\pm,\sigma}=0&~~~\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
(\partial_{t}+w^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}})h^{\pm,\sigma}=(h^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}})w^{\pm,\sigma}&~~~\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}}\cdot h^{\pm,\sigma}=0&~~~\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
(\partial_{t}+w^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot\nabla^{\Xi^{\sigma}})\mathfrak{S}^{\pm,\sigma}=0&~~~\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},\\
\left\llbracket\Pi^{\sigma}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\xi^{\sigma}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\xi^{\sigma}|^{2}}}\right)&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\xi^{\sigma}=w^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot N^{\sigma}&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
h^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot N^{\sigma}=0&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
(w^{\pm,\sigma},h^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{S}^{\pm,\sigma},\xi^{\sigma})|_{t=0}=(w_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},h_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},\xi_{0}^{\sigma}),\end{cases}

(5.1)

where
\Xi^{\sigma}(t,x)=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\xi^{\sigma}(t,x^{\prime})
to be the extension of
\xi^{\sigma}
in
\Omega
and
N^{\sigma}:=(-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\xi^{\sigma},-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\xi^{\sigma},1)^{\top}
. The quantity
\Pi^{\pm}:=\bar{\Pi}^{\pm}+\frac{1}{2}|h^{\pm}|^{2}
represent the total pressure for the incompressible equations with
\bar{\Pi}^{\pm}
the fluid pressure functions. The quantity
\mathfrak{R}^{\pm}
satisfies the evolution equation
(\partial_{t}+w^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathfrak{R}^{\pm}=0
with initial data
\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{\pm}:=\rho^{\pm}(0,\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{\pm})
.

Denote
(\psi^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},\rho^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})
to be the solution of (
1.30
) (indexed by
\sigma
and
\varepsilon
) with initial data
(\psi_{0}^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},\rho_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})
. For fixed
\sigma>0
, we want to show the convergence from the solutions to (
1.30
) to the solution to (
5.1
) as
\varepsilon\to 0
provided the convergence of initial data. We assume

1.

(Constraints for compressible initial data) The sequence of initial data
(\psi_{0}^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},\rho_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})\in H^{9.5}(\Sigma)\times(H_{*}^{8}(\Omega^{\pm}))^{4}
of (
1.30
) satisfy the constraints
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}}=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
,
b^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}}\cdot N^{\sigma}|_{t=0}=0
on
\Sigma\cup\Sigma^{\pm}
, the compatibility conditions (
1.31
) up to 7-th order,
|\psi_{0}^{{\varepsilon,\sigma}}|\leq 1
and
|\left\llbracket\bar{v}_{0}\right\rrbracket|>0
.

2.

(Convergence of initial data)
(\psi_{0}^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},\rho_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})\to(\xi_{0}^{\sigma},w_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},h_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{\pm,\sigma})
in
H^{5.5}(\Sigma)\times(H^{4}(\Omega^{\pm}))^{4}
.

3.

(Constraints for incompressible initial data) The incompressible data
(\xi_{0}^{\sigma},w_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},h_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{\pm,\sigma})\in H^{5}(\Sigma)\times(H^{4}(\Omega^{\pm}))^{4}
satisfies the constraints
\nabla^{\xi^{\sigma}_{0}}\cdot h_{0}^{\pm}=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
,
h^{\pm,\sigma}\cdot N^{\sigma}|_{t=0}=0
on
\Sigma\cup\Sigma^{\pm}
,
|\xi_{0}^{{\varepsilon,\sigma}}|\leq 2
and
\left\llbracket\bar{w}_{0}\right\rrbracket>0
.

Under these assumptions, we can prove that there exists a time
T_{\sigma}>0
that depends on
\sigma
and initial data and is independent of Mach number
\varepsilon
, such that the corresponding solutions to (
1.30
) converge to the solution to (
5.1
) as the Mach number
\varepsilon\to 0

\displaystyle(\psi^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},\rho^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})\to(\xi^{\sigma},w^{\pm,\sigma},h^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{R}^{\pm,\sigma},\mathfrak{S}^{\pm,\sigma})

\displaystyle\text{ strongly in }C([0,T_{\sigma}];H_{\text{loc}}^{5.5-\delta}(\Sigma)\times(H_{\text{loc}}^{4-\delta}(\Omega^{\pm}))^{4}),\text{ and weakly-* in }L^{\infty}([0,T_{\sigma}];H^{5.5}(\Sigma)\times(H^{4}(\Omega^{\pm}))^{4}).

In fact, according to estimates obtained in Theorem
1.1
, we already have the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
boundedness for
\psi^{\varepsilon,\sigma},~v^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}}
,
b^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},~S^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}}
as well as their first-order time derivatives. Thus, using Aubin-Lions compactness lemma, the above convergence is a straightforward result of uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates. Theorem
1.2
is proven.
