Appendix A
Reynolds transport theorems

We record the Reynolds transport theorems used in this paper. For the proof, we refer to Luo-Zhang
[
38
, Appendix A]

Lemma A.1
.

Let
f,g
be smooth functions defined on
[0,T]\times\Omega
. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\varphi}_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}g)\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(A.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}f)g\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(\partial_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}g)\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\mathring{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(A.2)

Lemma A.2

(
Integration by parts for covariant derivatives
)
.

Let
f,g
be defined as in Lemma
A.1
. Then:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}f)g\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}g)\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fgN_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(A.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}f)g\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\mathring{\varphi}}_{i}g)\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\mathring{N}_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(A.4)

The following theorem holds.

Theorem A.3

(
Reynolds transport theorem
)
.

Let
f
be a smooth function defined on
[0,T]\times\Omega
. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\rho(D_{t}^{\varphi}f)f\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x.

(A.5)

Theorem
A.3
leads to the following two corollaries. The first one records the integration by parts formula for
D_{t}^{\varphi}
.

Corollary A.4

(
Reynolds transport theorem - a variant
)
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(D_{t}^{\varphi}f)g\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(D_{t}^{\varphi}g)\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)fg\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x.

(A.6)

The second corollary concerns the transport theorem as well as the integration by parts formula for the linearized material derivative
D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}
.

Corollary A.5

(
Reynolds transport theorem for linearized
\kappa
-problem
)
.

Let
D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}:=\partial_{t}+(\mathring{\bar{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}
be the linearized material derivative. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}f)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\rho}+\mathring{\rho}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{v}\right)|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

(A.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\left(\partial_{3}(\mathring{\bar{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})(\mathring{\varphi}-\dot{\varphi})\right)\,\mathrm{d}x.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\varphi}}f)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{\mathring{\varphi}}\cdot\mathring{v}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

(A.8)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{2}\left(\partial_{3}(\mathring{\bar{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})(\mathring{\varphi}-\dot{\varphi})\right)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Appendix B
Preliminary lemmas about Sobolev inequalities

Lemma B.1

(Hodge-type elliptic estimates)
.

For any sufficiently smooth vector field
X
and
s\geq 1
, one has

\displaystyle\|X\|_{s}^{2}\leq C(|\psi|_{s},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}X\|_{0}^{2}\right),

(B.1)

\displaystyle\|X\|_{s}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times X\|_{s-1}^{2}+|X\cdot N|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\right),

(B.2)

\displaystyle\|X\|_{s}^{2}\leq C^{\prime\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times X\|_{s-1}^{2}+|X\times N|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\right),

(B.3)

for any multi-index
\alpha
with
|\alpha|=s
. The constant
C(|\psi|_{s},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
depends linearly on
|\psi|_{s}^{2}
and the constants
C^{\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
and
C^{\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
depend linearly on
|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}^{2}
.

Lemma B.2

(Normal trace lemma)
.

For any sufficiently smooth vector field
X
and
s\geq 0
, one has

\displaystyle\left|X\cdot N\right|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\lesssim C^{\prime\prime\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s}X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}\right)

(B.4)

where the constant
C^{\prime\prime\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
depends linearly on
|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}^{2}
.

We list two lemmas for the estimates of traces in the anisotropic Sobolev spaces. Define

L_{T}^{2}(H_{*}^{m}(\Omega^{\pm}))=\bigcap_{k=0}^{m}H^{k}((-\infty,T];H_{*}^{m-k}(\Omega^{\pm}))

with the norm
\|u\|_{m,*,T,\pm}:=\int_{-\infty}^{T}\|u(t)\|_{m,*,\pm}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t
. Similarly, we define

L_{T}^{2}(H^{m}(\Sigma))=\bigcap_{k=0}^{m}H^{k}((-\infty,T];H^{m-k}(\Sigma))

with the norm
|u|_{m,T}:=\int_{-\infty}^{T}|u(t)|_{m}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t
.

Lemma B.3

(Trace lemma for anisotropic Sobolev spaces,
[
61
, Lemma 3.4]
)
.

Let
m\geq 1,~m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, then we have the following trace lemma for the anisotropic Sobolev space.

1.

If
f\in L_{T}^{2}(H_{*}^{m+1}(\Omega^{\pm}))
, then its trace
f|_{\Sigma}
belongs to
L_{T}^{2}(H^{m}(\Omega^{\pm}))
and satisfies

|f|_{m,T}\lesssim\|f\|_{m+1,*,T,\pm}.

2.

There exists a linear continuous operator
\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}:L_{T}^{2}(H^{m}(\Sigma))\to L_{T}^{2}(H_{*}^{m+1}(\Omega^{\pm}))
such that
(\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}g)|_{\Sigma}=g
and

\|\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}g\|_{m+1,*,T,\pm}\lesssim|g|_{m,T}.

Proof.

The proof for the above lemma can be found in
[
49
, Theorem 1]
when we replace
(-\infty,T)
by
(-\infty,\infty)
. In our case, we can prove the same result by doing Sobolev extension. Namely, given
f\in L_{T}^{2}(H_{*}^{m+1}(\Omega^{+}))
, we can extend it to
F(t,x):R\times\Omega^{+}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\|f\|_{m+1,*,T,+}\lesssim\|F(t,x)\|_{H_{*}^{m+1}(\mathbb{R}\times\Omega^{+})}\lesssim\|f\|_{m+1,*,T,+}.

We can apply
[
49
, Theorem 1]
to
F
, and then do the truncation in
(-\infty,T]

|f|_{m,T}\lesssim|F|_{H^{m}(\mathbb{R}\times\Sigma)}\lesssim\|F(t,x)\|_{H_{*}^{m+1}(\mathbb{R}\times\Omega^{+})}\lesssim\|f\|_{m+1,*,T,+}.

∎

There is one derivative loss in the above trace lemma, which is 1/2-order more than the trace lemma for standard Sobolev spaces. Indeed, for
\Omega^{\pm}
defined in this paper, we have the following estimate that will be applied to control the non-characteristic variables
q,v\cdot\mathbf{N}
and
b\cdot\mathbf{N}
.

Lemma B.4

(An estimate for traces of non-characteristic variables)
.

Let
\Omega^{\pm}:=\mathbb{T}^{d-1}\times\{0\lessgtr x_{d}\lessgtr\pm H\}
,
\Sigma=\mathbb{T}^{d-1}\times\{x_{d}=0\}
and
\Sigma^{\pm}=\mathbb{T}^{d-1}\times\{\pm H\}
. Let
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}=(\omega(x_{d})\partial_{d})^{\alpha_{d+1}}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\cdots\overline{\partial}{}_{d-1}^{\alpha_{d-1}}\partial_{d}^{\alpha_{d}}
with
\langle\alpha\rangle:=\alpha_{0}+\cdots+\alpha_{d-1}+2\alpha_{d}+\alpha_{d+1}=m-1,~m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Let
q^{\pm}(t,x)\in H_{*}^{m}(\Omega)
satisfy
\|q^{\pm}(t)\|_{m,*,\pm}+\|\partial_{d}q^{\pm}(t)\|_{m-1,*,\pm}<\infty
for any
0\leq t\leq T
and let
f^{\pm}\in H_{*}^{2}(\Omega^{\pm})\cap H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega^{\pm})
be a function vanishing on
\Sigma^{\pm}
. Then we have

\int_{\Sigma}(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{\pm})\,(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle f^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq(\|\partial_{d}q^{\pm}\|_{m-1,*,\pm}+\|q^{\pm}\|_{m,*,\pm})\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\pm}\|_{1,\pm}

(B.5)

In particular, for
s\geq 1
, we have the following inequality for any
g^{\pm}\in H_{*}^{s}(\Omega^{\pm})
with
g^{\pm}|_{\Sigma^{\pm}}=0
.

|g^{\pm}|_{s-1/2}^{2}\leq\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s}g^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}\partial_{d}g^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\leq\|g^{\pm}\|_{s,*,\pm}\|\partial_{d}g^{\pm}\|_{s-1,*,\pm}.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Gauss-Green formula. Note that the unit exterior normal vectors for
\Omega^{\pm}
are
(0,\cdots,0,\mp 1)^{\top}
respectively, so we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{\pm})\,(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle f^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\mp\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial_{d}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{\pm})\,(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}f^{\pm})+(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{\pm})\,(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{d}f^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x

(B.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\|\partial_{d}q^{\pm}\|_{m-1,*,\pm}+\|q^{\pm}\|_{m,*,\pm})\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\pm}\|_{1,\pm}

In particular, let
q^{\pm}=g^{\pm}
and
f^{\pm}=\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{3}{2}}g^{\pm}
in (
B.5
) and we get

\displaystyle|g^{\pm}|_{s-1/2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1/2}g^{\pm})(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1/2}g^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\mp 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial_{d}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1/2}g^{\pm})(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1/2}g^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\overset{\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{1/2}}{=}

\displaystyle~\mp 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\partial_{d}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}g^{\pm})(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s}g^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x.

∎

The following lemma concerns the Sobolev embeddings.

Lemma B.5

(
[
61
, Lemma 3.3]
)
.

We have the following inequalities

\displaystyle H^{m}(\Omega^{\pm})\hookrightarrow H_{*}^{m}(\Omega^{\pm})\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle H^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}(\Omega^{\pm}),~~\forall m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H_{*}^{3}(\Omega^{\pm})},~~

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H_{*}^{5}(\Omega^{\pm})},~~|u|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H_{*}^{5}(\Omega^{\pm})}.

We also need the following Kato-Ponce type multiplicative Sobolev inequality.

Lemma B.6

(
[
27
]
)
.

Let
J=(1-\Delta)^{1/2}
,
s\geq 0
. Then the following estimates hold:

\|J^{s}(fg)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,p_{1}}}\|g\|_{L^{p_{2}}}+\|f\|_{L^{q_{1}}}\|g\|_{W^{s,q_{2}}},

(B.7)

where
1/2=1/p_{1}+1/p_{2}=1/q_{1}+1/q_{2}
and
2\leq p_{1},q_{2}<\infty
.

\|[J^{s},f]g\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|\partial f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|J^{s-1}g\|_{L^{p}}+\|J^{s}f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}

(B.8)

where
s\geq 0
and
1<p<\infty
.

We also need the following transport-type estimate in order to close the uniform estimates for the nonlinear approximate system.

Lemma B.7
.

Let
f(t)\in W^{1,1}(0,T)
and
g\in L^{1}(0,T)
and
\kappa>0
. Assume that

f(t)+\kappa f^{\prime}(t)\leq g(t)~~\text{a.e. }t\in(0,T).

Then for any
t\in(0,T)
,

\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}f(\tau)\leq f(0)+\mathop{\mathrm{ess~sup}}\limits_{\tau\in(0,t)}|g(\tau)|.

Appendix C
Construction of initial data satisfying the compatibility conditions

Given initial data
(v_{0}^{\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{\pm},S_{0}^{\pm},\psi_{0})
of the original current-vortex sheets problem (
1.30
) satisfying the compatibility conditions (
1.31
) up to 7-th order, we need to construct a sequence of initial data
(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa})
to the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximate system (
3.1
) satisfying the compatibility conditions (
3.4
) up to 7-th order that converge to the given data as
\kappa\to 0_{+}
.

C.1
Reformulation of the compatibility conditions

Let us first ignore the
\kappa
-regularization terms and consider the compatibility conditions (
1.31
) for the original system. Also, let us omit the fixed boundaries
\Sigma^{\pm}
, omit the density functions, consider the isentropic case and write
\varepsilon^{2}=\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}
for convenience. The heuristic idea is that the odd
(m=2r+1)
order compatibility condition is rewritten to be

-\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r+1}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0})\right\rrbracket=\cdots\quad\text{ on }\Sigma

and the even
(m=2r)
order compatibility condition is rewritten to be

\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}\right\rrbracket=\cdots\quad\text{ on }\Sigma

with
\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}:=\varepsilon^{-2}+|b_{0}|^{2}
. Such reformulation is convenient for us to add
\kappa
-perturbation terms to construct the desired data for (
3.1
). More specifically, let us start with the zero-th order compatibility conditions:

\displaystyle\left\llbracket q_{0}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi_{0}),\quad\psi_{t}|_{t=0}=v_{0}^{\pm}\cdot N_{0}=v_{03}^{\pm}-\bar{v}_{0}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}.

(C.1)

The first-order compatibility conditions are

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{t=0}=\sigma\partial_{t}\mathcal{H}(\psi)|_{t=0},\quad\psi_{tt}|_{t=0}=\partial_{t}(v^{\pm}\cdot N)|_{t=0},

(C.2)

which are not easy to compute, especially the first one. The left side is equal to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}q^{+}-\partial_{t}q^{-}=

\displaystyle~\overline{D_{t}^{+}}q^{+}-\overline{D_{t}^{-}}q^{-}-(\bar{v}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket-(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})q^{-}.

Using the continuity equation, the evolution equation of
b
, we get

\overline{D_{t}}q=-\varepsilon^{-2}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)+\overline{D_{t}}b\cdot b=-\underbrace{(\varepsilon^{-2}+|b|^{2})}_{=:\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)+(\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla})v\cdot b\text{ on }\Sigma,

and thus the time-differentiated jump condition becomes

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0})\right\rrbracket=

\displaystyle\left\llbracket(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})v_{0}\cdot b_{0}\right\rrbracket-(\left\llbracket\bar{v}_{0}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})q_{0}^{-}+\overline{D_{t}^{+}}(\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi))|_{t=0}\text{ on }\Sigma.

Here and thereafter, we will repeatedly use
\overline{D_{t}^{\pm}}\psi=v_{3}^{\pm}
on
\Sigma
and omit lots of redundant terms in order for simplicity of notations. For example, we will write
\mathcal{H}(\psi)\sim\overline{\Delta}{}\psi
, write
(1-\overline{\Delta}{})
to be
-\overline{\Delta}{}
, and omit the commutators between
\overline{D_{t}^{+}}
and
\mathcal{H},(1-\overline{\Delta}{})
, the density function
\rho
. Indeed, later we will see that the concrete form of those omitted term is not important, and we just need to find out the major term as in
[
32
, Appendix A]
. Under this setting, we have

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0})\right\rrbracket\sim

\displaystyle\left\llbracket(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})v_{0}\cdot b_{0}\right\rrbracket-(\left\llbracket\bar{v}_{0}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})q_{0}^{-}+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}v_{03}^{+}\text{ on }\Sigma.

(C.3)

For higher-order compatibility conditions, we invoke the wave equation for total pressure
q^{\pm}
to get (cf.
[
32
, Appendix A.1]
)

\displaystyle(\overline{D_{t}})^{2}q=\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b}\Delta^{\varphi}q+\mathcal{M}_{0}(v,b)+\mathcal{N}_{0}(v,b)\quad\text{ on }\Sigma,

(C.4)

where

\mathcal{M}_{0}(v,b)=-(\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}q+(\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}b\cdot b+\mathcal{R}_{0}(v,b),~~~\mathcal{N}_{0}(v,b)=\partial^{\varphi}_{i}v^{j}\partial^{\varphi}_{j}v^{i}-\partial^{\varphi}_{i}b^{j}\partial^{\varphi}_{j}b^{i}

and
\mathcal{R}_{0}(v,b)
only contains the first-order derivatives of
b,v
with the form

\mathcal{R}_{0}(v,b)=P_{0}(b)((\partial^{i_{1}}v)(\partial^{i_{2}}v)+(\partial^{j_{1}}b)(\partial^{j_{2}}b))

where
P_{0}(b)
is a polynomial of
b
only containing cubic and quadratic terms and
(i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2})=(0,0,1,1)
or
(1,1,0,0)
. Taking substraction between the equation of
q^{+}
and the equation of
q^{-}
, we get

\left\llbracket(\overline{D_{t}})^{2}q\right\rrbracket|_{t=0}=\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}\Delta^{\varphi_{0}}q_{0}\right\rrbracket+\left\llbracket\mathcal{M}_{0}(v_{0},b_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{0}(v_{0},b_{0})\right\rrbracket\quad\text{ on }\Sigma.

Then using
\overline{D_{t}^{+}}=\overline{D_{t}^{-}}+(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
, we get

\displaystyle\left\llbracket(\overline{D_{t}})^{2}q\right\rrbracket|_{t=0}=(\overline{D_{t}^{+}})^{2}(\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi))|_{t=0}+\mathcal{T}^{2}_{\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket}q^{-}|_{t=0},

where each
\mathcal{T}_{\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket}
represents either of
\overline{D_{t}^{-}}
and
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
. So, the second-order compatibility condition is reformulated as

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}\Delta^{\varphi_{0}}q_{0}\right\rrbracket=

\displaystyle~(\overline{D_{t}^{+}})^{2}(\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi))|_{t=0}+\mathcal{T}^{2}_{\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket}q^{-}|_{t=0}-\left\llbracket\mathcal{M}_{0}(v_{0},b_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{0}(v_{0},b_{0})\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle~-\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+}+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}(\bar{b}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla})b_{03}^{+}+\mathcal{T}^{2}_{\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket}q^{-}|_{t=0}-\left\llbracket\mathcal{M}_{0}(v_{0},b_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{0}(v_{0},b_{0})\right\rrbracket\quad\text{ on }\Sigma.

(C.5)

Taking one more material derivative in the wave equation and again use the continuity equation, we get

\displaystyle(\overline{D_{t}})^{3}q\sim-\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b}^{2}\Delta^{\varphi}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)+\varepsilon^{-2}(\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)+\mathcal{M}_{1}(v,b,q)+\mathcal{N}_{1}(v,b,q)

(C.6)

where the concrete form of
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{N}_{1}
will be specified later. Recursively, after long and tedious calculations (cf.
[
32
, (A.4)-(A.7)]
), we find that the time-differentiated wave equation (restricted on
\{t=0\}\times\Sigma
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle m=2r+1,

\displaystyle~~-\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r+1}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0})=(\overline{D_{t}})^{2r+1}q+\sum_{j=0}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}))\text{ on }\Sigma,

(C.7)

\displaystyle m=2r,

\displaystyle~~\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}=(\overline{D_{t}})^{2r}q+\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}))\text{ on }\Sigma,

(C.8)

where
\mathcal{M}_{-1}(v_{0},b_{0}):=-(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})v_{0}\cdot b_{0}
and
\mathcal{N}_{-1}:=0
, and for
r\geq 1
we define

\displaystyle m=2r-1,~~\mathcal{M}_{2r-1}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})=

\displaystyle(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0})+\sum_{l=2}^{r+1}\underbrace{b_{0}^{i_{1}}\cdots b_{0}^{i_{2l}}(\nabla^{2r+1}v_{0})}_{<2^{l}\text{ terms}}+\mathcal{R}_{2r-1}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}),

(C.9)

\displaystyle m=2r,~~\mathcal{M}_{2r}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})=

\displaystyle~-(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}+\mathcal{R}_{2r}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}),

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=2}^{r+1}\underbrace{(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{r+2}(\nabla^{r}b_{0})b_{0}^{i_{1}}\cdots b_{0}^{i_{2l}}+(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}(\nabla^{2r}q_{0})b_{0}^{j_{1}}\cdots b_{0}^{j_{2l}}}_{<2^{l}\text{ terms }};

(C.10)

and the term
\mathcal{R}_{m}
, where every top-order term has
(m+1)
-th order derivative, has the following form

\mathcal{R}_{m}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})=P_{k}(b_{0})\left(C^{m}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{p},j_{1}\cdots j_{n},k_{1}\cdots k_{l}}(\nabla^{i_{1}}v_{0})\cdots(\nabla^{i_{p}}v_{0})(\nabla^{j_{1}}b_{0})\cdots(\nabla^{j_{n}}b_{0})(\nabla^{k_{1}}q_{0})\cdots(\nabla^{k_{l}}q_{0})\right),

where
\nabla
may represent either of
\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}
or
\partial
, and
P_{k}(\cdot)
is a polynomial of its arguments and the lowest power is 4 and the indices above satisfy

\displaystyle 1\leq i_{1},\cdots,i_{p},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\leq k+1,0\leq k_{1},\cdots,k_{l}\leq m+1,

\displaystyle i_{1}+\cdots+i_{p}+j_{1}+\cdots+j_{n}+k_{1}+\cdots+k_{l}=m+1.

The term
\mathcal{N}_{m}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})
has the following form

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{m}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})=

\displaystyle~P_{m,1}(b_{0})(\nabla^{1+2\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor}v_{0})(\nabla v_{0})+P_{m,2}(b_{0})(\nabla^{2\lceil\frac{m}{2}\rceil}q_{0})(\nabla v_{0})+P_{k,0}(b_{0})(\nabla^{m+1}b_{0})(\nabla v_{0})

\displaystyle+P^{\prime}_{m}(b_{0})D^{m}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{p},j_{1}\cdots j_{n},k_{1}\cdots k_{l}}\left((\nabla^{i_{1}}v_{0})\cdots(\nabla^{i_{p}}v_{0})(\nabla^{j_{1}}b_{0})\cdots(\nabla^{j_{n}}b_{0})(\nabla^{k_{1}}q_{0})\cdots(\nabla^{k_{l}}q_{0})\right),

(C.11)

where
P_{m,1}(\cdot),P_{m,2}(\cdot),P_{m}^{\prime}(\cdot)
are polynomials of their arguments and
P_{m,0}(\cdot)
is a polynomial of its arguments and the lowest power is 2. The indices above satisfy

\displaystyle 1\leq i_{1},\cdots,i_{p},j_{1},\cdots,j_{n}\leq k,0\leq k_{1},\cdots,k_{l}\leq m,

\displaystyle i_{1}+\cdots+i_{p}+j_{1}+\cdots+j_{n}+k_{1}+\cdots+k_{l}=m+1.

Next we take the difference between the equations (
C.7
)-(
C.8
) in
\Omega^{+}
and those in
\Omega^{-}
and restrict the equation on
\{t=0\}\times\Sigma
to get the jump condition in the
m
-th order compatibility conditions

\displaystyle m=2r+1,

\displaystyle~-\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r+1}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0})\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket(\overline{D_{t}})^{2r+1}q\right\rrbracket+\sum_{j=0}^{r}\left\llbracket(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}))\right\rrbracket\text{ on }\Sigma,

(C.12)

\displaystyle m=2r,

\displaystyle~\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}\right\rrbracket=\left\llbracket(\overline{D_{t}})^{2r}q\right\rrbracket+\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}\left\llbracket(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}))\right\rrbracket\text{ on }\Sigma.

(C.13)

Then using
\overline{D_{t}^{+}}=\overline{D_{t}^{-}}+(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
, we get

\left\llbracket(\overline{D_{t}})^{m}q\right\rrbracket=(\overline{D_{t}^{+}})^{m}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket+\mathcal{T}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket}^{m}q^{-}|_{t=0},

where each
\mathcal{T}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket}
represents either
(\overline{D_{t}^{-}})
or
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})
. Using the jump condition for
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket
, we have

\displaystyle m=2r:

\displaystyle~(\overline{D_{t}^{+}})^{2r}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\sim\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}(\overline{D_{t}^{+}})^{2r-1}v_{3}^{+}\sim\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+}+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}(v_{0}^{+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{+})

(C.14)

\displaystyle m=2r+1:

\displaystyle~(\overline{D_{t}^{+}})^{2r+1}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\sim\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}(\overline{D_{t}^{+}})^{2r}v_{3}^{+}\sim-\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{+})+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r}(v_{0}^{+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{+})

(C.15)

where the leading-order terms in
\mathcal{S}_{m}
are

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}})^{r-2}(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-2}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+},\quad\mathcal{S}_{2r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-(\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}})^{r-1}(\bar{b}_{0}\cdot\overline{\nabla})^{2}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-2}\partial_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{+}).

(C.16)

Thus, the compatibility conditions for the original current-vortex sheets system (
1.30
) are reformulated as

\displaystyle m=2r+1,

\displaystyle~~-\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r+1}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0})\right\rrbracket\sim\sum_{j=0}^{r}\left\llbracket(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}))\right\rrbracket

(C.17)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{T}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket}^{2r+1}q^{-}|_{t=0}-\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{+})+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r}(v_{0}^{+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{+})\quad\text{ on }\Sigma,

\displaystyle m=2r,

\displaystyle~~\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}\right\rrbracket\sim\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}\left\llbracket(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0},b_{0},q_{0}))\right\rrbracket

(C.18)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{T}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket}^{2r}q^{-}|_{t=0}+\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+}+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}(v_{0}^{+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{+}))\quad\text{ on }\Sigma.

Note that the time-differentiated kinematic boundary condition is already implicitly used when deriving the above compatibility conditions. Similarly, the compatibility conditions for the
\kappa
-approximate problem (
3.1
) are reformulated as

\displaystyle m=2r+1,

\displaystyle~~-\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r+1}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{\kappa})\right\rrbracket\sim\sum_{j=0}^{r}\left\llbracket(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0}^{\kappa},b_{0}^{\kappa},q_{0}^{\kappa})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-1-2j}(v_{0}^{\kappa},b_{0}^{\kappa},q_{0}^{\kappa}))\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle+\mathcal{T}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket}^{2r+1}q^{-}|_{t=0}-\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{\kappa,+})+\kappa\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{\kappa,+})+\kappa\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+}

\displaystyle+(\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}-\kappa\overline{\Delta}{}^{2})\mathcal{S}_{2r}(v_{0}^{\kappa,+},b_{0}^{\kappa,+},q_{0}^{\kappa,+})+\kappa\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r+1}(v_{0}^{\kappa,+},b_{0}^{\kappa,+},q_{0}^{\kappa,+})\quad\text{ on }\Sigma,

(C.19)

\displaystyle m=2r,

\displaystyle~~\left\llbracket\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}^{\kappa}\right\rrbracket\sim\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}\left\llbracket(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}(\mathcal{M}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0}^{\kappa},b_{0}^{\kappa},q_{0}^{\kappa})+\mathcal{N}_{2r-2-2j}(v_{0}^{\kappa},b_{0}^{\kappa},q_{0}^{\kappa}))\right\rrbracket

\displaystyle+\mathcal{T}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket}^{2r}q^{-}|_{t=0}+\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+}-\kappa\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+}-\kappa\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{+})

\displaystyle+(\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}-\kappa\overline{\Delta}{}^{2})\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}(v_{0}^{\kappa,+},b_{0}^{\kappa,+},q_{0}^{\kappa,+})+\kappa\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r}(v_{0}^{\kappa,+},b_{0}^{\kappa,+},q_{0}^{\kappa,+})\quad\text{ on }\Sigma.

(C.20)

C.2
Construction of the converging initial data

Given initial data
(v_{0}^{\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{\pm},S_{0}^{\pm},\psi_{0})
of (
1.30
) satisfying the compatibility conditions (
C.17
)-(
C.18
) up to 7-th order, we now construct the initial data
(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa})
to (
3.1
) satisfying the compatibility conditions (
C.1
)-(
C.1
) up to 7-th order that converge to the given data as
\kappa\to 0_{+}
. To do this, we just need to
equally distribute the
\kappa
-term to the solution in
\Omega^{+}
and the solution in
\Omega^{-}
.

C.2.1
Recover the 0-th order and the 1-st order compatibility conditions

First, we pick
b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm}=b_{0}^{\pm}
,
\psi_{0}^{\kappa}=\psi_{0}
. We define
\partial_{t}\psi^{\kappa}|_{t=0}:=v_{0}^{\pm}\cdot N_{0}
and
\partial_{t}b^{\pm}|_{t=0}=(b_{0}^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi_{0}})v_{0}^{\pm}-b_{0}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{\pm})
in
\Omega^{\pm}
. Then the constraints for the magnetic field are automatically satisfied. Now, we construct
q_{0}^{(0)}
such that
(v_{0}^{\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(0),\pm},\psi_{0})
satisfies the 0-th order compatibility condition (
C.1
). The function
q_{0}^{(1),\pm}
is set to be the solution to the poly-harmonic equation

\begin{cases}\Delta^{2}q_{0}^{(0),\pm}=\Delta^{2}q_{0}^{\pm}&~~~\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}\\
q_{0}^{(0),\pm}=q_{0}^{\pm}\mp\frac{1}{2}\kappa\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}\psi_{0}\pm\frac{1}{2}\kappa\overline{\Delta}{}(v_{0}^{\pm}\cdot N_{0})&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
\partial_{3}q_{0}^{(0),\pm}=\partial_{3}q_{0}^{\pm}&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
\partial_{3}^{j}q_{0}^{(0),\pm}=\partial_{3}^{j}q_{0}^{\pm},~~0\leq j\leq 1&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma^{\pm}.\end{cases}

(C.21)

Then for
s\geq 4
, we have

\|q_{0}^{(0),\pm}-q_{0}^{\pm}\|_{s,\pm}\lesssim\kappa|\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}\psi_{0}|_{s-0.5}+\kappa|\overline{\Delta}{}(v_{0}^{\pm}\cdot N_{0})|_{s-0.5}\to 0\quad\text{ as }\kappa\to 0.

With this
q_{0}^{(0)}
, we define
\partial_{t}^{2}\psi|_{t=0}=\partial_{t}(v^{\pm}\cdot N)|_{t=0}
via
(v_{0}^{\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(0),\pm},\psi_{0})
on
\Sigma
. (Note that
\partial_{t}v\cdot N|_{t=0}
already includes
\partial_{3}q_{0}
. Only when we have
\partial_{3}q_{0}^{(0),\pm}=\partial_{3}q_{0}^{\pm}
on
\Sigma
can we keep the jump condition
\left\llbracket\partial_{t}(v\cdot N)\right\rrbracket=0
.) and also define the corresponding
\partial_{t}^{2}b|_{t=0}
in
\Omega^{\pm}
via the evolution equation of
b
. Thus, the
\partial_{t}
-differentiated boundary constraint for
b\cdot N
is also satisfied.

Now we introduce
v_{0}^{(0),\pm}
such that
(v_{0}^{(0),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(0),\pm},\psi_{0})
satisfies the 1-st order compatibility condition (
C.1
). We define
\bar{v}_{0i}^{(0),\pm}=\bar{v}_{0i}^{\pm}
for
i=1,2
and define
v_{03}^{(0),\pm}
via the following poly-harmonic equation

\begin{cases}\Delta^{3}v_{03}^{(0),\pm}=\Delta^{3}v_{03}^{\pm}&~~~\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}\\
\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{03}^{(0),\pm})=(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}v_{03}^{\pm})\mp\frac{1}{2}(\left\llbracket\bar{v}_{0}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})(q_{0}^{(0),\pm}-q_{0}^{\pm})\mp\frac{\kappa}{2}\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}v_{03}^{+}\pm\frac{\kappa}{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{(0),+}&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
v_{03}^{(0),\pm}=v_{03}^{\pm},\quad\partial_{3}^{2}v_{03}^{(0),\pm}=\partial_{3}^{2}v_{03}^{\pm}&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
\partial_{3}^{j}v_{03}^{(0),\pm}=\partial_{3}^{j}v_{03}^{\pm},~~0\leq j\leq 2&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma^{\pm}.\end{cases}

(C.22)

It is also straightforward to see the convergece for
s\geq 6

\|v_{0}^{(0),\pm}-v_{0}^{\pm}\|_{s,\pm}\lesssim|q_{0}^{(0),\pm}-q_{0}^{\pm}|_{s-0.5}+\kappa(|v_{03}^{+}|_{s+2.5}+|\partial_{3}q_{0}^{+}|_{s+0.5}).

C.2.2
Higher-order compatibility conditions

For
r\geq 1
, we can inductively define
q_{0}^{(r),\pm}
such that
(v_{0}^{(r-1),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(r),\pm},\psi_{0})
satisfies the compatibility condition up to
2r
-th order

\begin{cases}\Delta^{2r+2}q_{0}^{(r),\pm}=\Delta^{2r+2}q_{0}^{(r-1),\pm}&~~~\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}\\
\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}^{(r),\pm}=\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}q_{0}^{(r-1),\pm}\\
\quad+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{r-1}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}\left((\mathcal{M}_{2r-2-2j}+\mathcal{N}_{2r-2-2j})(v_{0}^{(r-1),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(r),\pm})-(\mathcal{M}_{2r-2-2j}+\mathcal{N}_{2r-2-2j})(v_{0}^{(r-2),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(r-1),\pm})\right)\\
\quad\pm\frac{1}{2}\bigg((\mathcal{T}^{2r}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}^{(r-1)}\right\rrbracket}q^{(r),-}-\mathcal{T}^{2r}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}^{(r-2)}\right\rrbracket}q^{(r-1),-})+\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\underbrace{\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(q_{0}^{(r),+}-q_{0}^{(r-1),+})}_{=0}\\
\quad\quad+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\big(\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}(v_{0}^{(r-1),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r),+})-\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}(v_{0}^{(r-2),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r-1),+})\big)\bigg)\\
\quad\mp\frac{\kappa}{2}\left(\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\underbrace{\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(q_{0}^{(r),+}-q_{0}^{(r-1),+})}_{=0}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot(v_{0}^{(r-1),+}-v_{0}^{(r-2),+})\right)\\
\quad\mp\frac{\kappa}{2}\left((\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}-\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r})(v_{0}^{(r-1),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r),+})-(\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}\mathcal{S}_{2r-1}-\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r})(v_{0}^{(r-2),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r-1),+})\right)&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
\partial_{3}^{j}q_{0}^{(r),\pm}=\partial_{3}^{j}q_{0}^{(r-1),\pm},~~0\leq j\leq 2r+1,j\neq 2r&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
\partial_{3}^{j}q_{0}^{(r),\pm}=\partial_{3}^{j}q_{0}^{(r-1),\pm},~~0\leq j\leq 2r+1&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma^{\pm},\end{cases}

(C.23)

and define
\bar{v}_{0}^{(r),\pm}=\bar{v}_{0}^{(r-1),\pm}
and
v_{03}^{(r),\pm}
such that
(v_{0}^{(r),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(r),\pm},\psi_{0})
satisfies the compatibility condition up to
(2r+1)
-th order

\begin{cases}\Delta^{2r+3}v_{03}^{(r),\pm}=\Delta^{2r+3}v_{03}^{(r-1),\pm}&~~~\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}\\
-\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{(r),\pm})=-\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r}(\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}^{(r-1),\pm})\\
\quad+\sum\limits_{j=0}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}\left((\mathcal{M}_{2r-1-2j}+\mathcal{N}_{2r-1-2j})(v_{0}^{(r),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(r),\pm})-(\mathcal{M}_{2r-1-2j}+\mathcal{N}_{2r-1-2j})(v_{0}^{(r-1),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(r-1),\pm})\right)\\
\quad\pm\frac{1}{2}\bigg((\mathcal{T}^{2r+1}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}^{(r)}\right\rrbracket}q^{(r),-}-\mathcal{T}^{2r}_{\left\llbracket\bar{v}^{(r-1)}\right\rrbracket}q^{(r-1),-})-\sigma\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\underbrace{\overline{\Delta}{}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot(v_{03}^{(r),+}-v_{03}^{(r-1),+})}_{=0}\\
\quad\quad+\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\big(\mathcal{S}_{2r}(v_{0}^{(r),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r),+})-\mathcal{S}_{2r}(v_{0}^{(r-1),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r-1),+})\big)\bigg)\\
\quad\pm\frac{\kappa}{2}\left(\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r-1}\underbrace{(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot(v_{0}^{(r),+}-v_{0}^{(r-1),+})}_{=0}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\varepsilon,b_{0}}^{r}(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{r-1}\partial_{3}(q_{0}^{(r),+}-q_{0}^{(r-1),+})\right)\\
\quad\mp\frac{\kappa}{2}\left((\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}\mathcal{S}_{2r}-\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r+1})(v_{0}^{(r),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r),+})-(\overline{\Delta}{}^{2}\mathcal{S}_{2r}-\overline{\Delta}{}\mathcal{S}_{2r+1})(v_{0}^{(r-1),+},b_{0}^{+},q_{0}^{(r-1),+})\right)&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
\partial_{3}^{j}v_{03}^{(r),\pm}=\partial_{3}^{j}v_{03}^{(r-1),\pm},~~0\leq j\leq 2r+2,j\neq 2r+1&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma\\
\partial_{3}^{j}v_{03}^{(r),\pm}=\partial_{3}^{j}v_{03}^{(r-1),\pm},~~0\leq j\leq 2r+2&~~~\text{ on }\Sigma^{\pm}.\end{cases}

(C.24)

Since we require the compatibility conditions up to 7-th order, we can stop at
r=3
and define
(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},b_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},S_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},\psi_{0}^{\kappa})
to be
(v_{0}^{(3),\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{(3),\pm},S_{0}^{\pm},\psi_{0})
. It is also straightforward to see the convergence after long and tedious calculations: For
s\geq 2\times(2r+3)=18
, we have the convergence as
\kappa\to 0

\displaystyle\left\|(v_{0}^{\kappa,\pm},q_{0}^{\kappa,\pm})-(v_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{\pm})\right\|_{s,\pm}\lesssim

\displaystyle~P(\|v_{0}^{\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{\pm},S_{0}^{\pm}\|_{s+1,\pm})\left(\kappa|\psi_{0}|_{s+3.5}+\sum_{j=0}^{r}\kappa|(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{j}v_{0}^{\pm}|_{s+1.5-2j}+\kappa|(\Delta^{\varphi_{0}})^{(j-1)_{+}}\partial_{3}q_{0}^{\pm}|_{s+0.5-2j}\right)\to 0,

provided that the given initial data is sufficiently regular. Specifically, picking
s=18
, the given data is required to satisfy
\|(v_{0}^{\pm},b_{0}^{\pm},q_{0}^{\pm},S_{0}^{\pm})\|_{20,\pm}+|\psi_{0}|_{21.5}<+\infty
. We may assume the given data belongs to
C^{\infty}
-class for convenience.
